# 1.0 · HOW I RAN IT, AND WHAT I VERIFIED RATHER THAN INHERITED

`00_Frame.md` §0.3 and `00.1` §7 hand Step 1 a docket of defects already graded by prior readers. The brief
instructs me to **re-check them independently rather than inherit the verdicts**, so everything below marked
⭐ **RE-DERIVED** was computed from the admitted lines themselves, not read off the prior grading.

**Everything I recomputed from the admitted set alone** (no new file access):

| Check | Result | Verdict on the inherited claim |
|---|---|---|
| Census I robots column sums | **594,715** exactly | ✅ confirmed |
| Census I humans column sums | **563,599** exactly | ✅ confirmed |
| Census II robots column sums | **344,173** exactly | ✅ confirmed |
| Census II humans column sums | **437,423** exactly | ✅ confirmed |
| Share column sums | **100.00%** exactly | ✅ confirmed |
| Monthly precipitation sums (L95–106) | **72.8 mm** exactly, matches L79 | ✅ confirmed |
| Unweighted mean of the Mean column | **−10.0333 °C**, matches L71's −10.0 | ✅ confirmed |
| Retention combined / humans / robots | **67.4770% · 77.6125% · 57.8719%**, spread **19.7406 pp** | ✅ confirmed |
| Robot share at Census I / II | **51.34% → 44.03%** — the majority inverts | ✅ confirmed |
| L21's Russia criterion, *"distance 0 from Davis's solar UTC+5"* | 77°58′E ÷ 15 = **5.198 h** ⇒ solar UTC+5 | ✅ the stated ground checks out from L3 alone |

⭐ **RE-DERIVED — AND NEW.** Every per-nation share is **identical across all four columns**: China is 19.04% of
Census I robots, Census I humans, Census II robots *and* Census II humans (113,234/594,715 = 107,309/563,599 =
65,530/344,173 = 83,285/437,423 = 19.04%). **Therefore every national-origin community's robots retain at exactly
57.87% and every community's humans at exactly 77.61%, by construction.** L54 discloses the model is proportional;
it does **not** disclose the consequence. See `A-4` and `C-2` — this is a hard ceiling on the pass's richest
generator and nothing inherited states it.

# 1.A · THE ASYMMETRY AUDIT — GATE 9, AGAINST INHERITED MATERIAL

**Gate 9's question, as `04` L216–219 states it:** for every threshold, gate, conversion, verdict, admission or
status change — *did the file write both ways?* What happens to whoever it decides **against**, is that outcome
as durable, and **is there a route back?** `00_RUNBOOK.md` L2494 predicts *"a very high rate"* against inherited
material because early material explains how a place **works** and stops.

**How I enumerated.** I walked the 137 admitted `Specs/Davis.md` lines and the 9 census lines in file order and
lifted every clause that decides, grades, admits, converts, assigns or changes a status. **Nineteen fired.**
I did not invent answers; each entry ends in the question the gap leaves open.

## A.1 · THE FIRES

### ⭐⭐ `A-1` · L131 — **"Not a Saint."** A canonical verdict of exclusion with a criterion and no appeal
**Written:** the exclusion, *and* its criterion — *"the Saints are pre-2083 explorers rather than mariners who
served in supporting roles."* **Written:** the consolation — *"a figure whose name carries specific weight."*
**Not written:** whether any tier exists between Saint and nothing; whether the roster has an author; whether
anything is ever added to it; what the city's own residents do with a namesake who was formally weighed and
found to be the wrong kind of person. **No route back is stated and none is implied.**
> ⭐ This is the textbook shape `04` L232 names: **a membership mechanism with no author has no appeal process
> either.** It is also the *rarest* shape in this file — an exclusion written **with** its criterion.
> **OPEN QUESTION:** *Is there a Tepenian category between Saint and unmarked, and does Davis's namesake occupy
> it — or is the city named for the only kind of Antarctic figure the national honor system does not recognize?*

### ⭐⭐ `A-2` · L18–22 · L54 · census L396–398 — **the three-tier national-origin ranking**
The clearest formal grading mechanism in the whole inherited set. **Written, completely, from the favorable
side:** three ranks, an explicit weighting (Primary = 8 shares, Significant = 3, Notable = 1, L54), 21 named
communities, and — at L21 — **one admission stated with its criterion** (Russia, added 2026-09-05, on 25M
Gini-adjusted plus solar-longitude distance 0).
**Not written:** (a) the **excluded set** — the tiers name who is in and never name who is out or how many;
(b) whether a community can **fall** between tiers, or be removed; (c) **any exclusion criterion whatsoever** —
in the entire admitted set, admission has been reasoned once and refusal never; (d) whether "tier" is an
**in-world status** among communities or an **authoring weight**. The file does not say, and the two readings
have opposite consequences for every later phase.
> **OPEN QUESTIONS:** *Is tier an in-world thing residents can perceive, or only a demographic instrument? If
> in-world: what does Primary standing get a community that Notable standing does not, who decides, and has any
> community here ever moved?*

### ⭐⭐ `A-3` · L15–L16 + census L496/L613 — **the orbital departure.** The single largest status change inherited
**376,718 people leave Davis** across the Census I → II boundary. **Written:** the aggregate, and the assurance
that it was not death — *"nobody was born or died in the transition; they relocated."*
**Not written — and this is the whole of Gate 9's question:** whether going up had an **eligibility criterion**;
who was **refused**; who could not afford it; who stayed because someone else could not go; **and whether anyone
who went can come back.** The census records a destination, not a mechanism. *"They relocated"* is written
entirely in the mover's voice.
> ⚠ `00_Frame.md` §0.1 correctly rules this **migration, not Declining** — a two-sided call, and I confirm it.
> **That ruling settles the status field. It does not supply the mechanism, and Gate 9 is asking for the
> mechanism.**
> **OPEN QUESTIONS:** *Was orbital migration an admission with a gate, or an open door? Is there a route back to
> Davis, and has anyone taken it? What is a person in Davis who applied and did not go?*

### ⭐⭐ `A-4` · L15–L16 — **the 19.74 pp robot/human split**, and the model that cannot explain it
Robots retain **57.87%**, humans **77.61%**. `00_Frame.md` §0.6 makes this the pass's strongest Step 0 finding
and hands forward the question *"what about this place made that so?"* **I confirm the arithmetic exactly.**
**Not written:** whether robots **chose** or **were selected**. *"Relocated"* is voice-neutral, and the gap is
load-bearing — a 19.74 pp spread is either a preference or a decision made against somebody, and the inherited
material does not say which.
⛔ **And the second half, which is mine and is new:** because L54's model is proportional, **every one of the 21
communities loses robots at exactly 57.87%.** The data therefore **cannot** answer *which* communities left —
that answer is foreclosed by construction, permanently, and **no inherited line says so.** `00.1` §3.3 gets
close (*"never readable as measured per-nation counts"*) but stops before the retention consequence.
> **OPEN QUESTIONS:** *Did robots leave or were robots moved — and who would know? And: does any evidence exist
> anywhere that could distinguish differential departure by community, given that the census model cannot?*

### ⭐⭐ `A-5` · L127 — **the documentary conversion.** A success written without its cost, and a permanent no-route-back
**Written:** *"No living environmental knowledge survived that chain of handoffs — but preserved journals, logs,
and orientation manuals… gave the exiles a real documentary starting point,"* and the outcome — *"the exiles
still built their own practical mastery of the terrain independently, over generations of their own."*
**Not written:** **what the conversion cost.** A documentary inheritance without a living institution means every
error is made once, in the field, by somebody, and the file records only that mastery was eventually reached.
**And there is no route back at all:** the chain of handoffs cannot be re-established; the teaching institution
is gone permanently and nothing can restore it.
> ⭐ `04` L219: **"A 'no route back' nobody has perceived as a problem is a textbook shadow."** This one is not
> perceived as a problem anywhere in the admitted set — L127 frames it as a *starting point*, i.e. favorably.
> **OPEN QUESTIONS:** *What did the generations before mastery cost, and is any of that cost remembered here?
> Does a community that learned its own ground from paper treat written instruction differently from one taught
> by people — and what does it do with a document it cannot verify?*

### `A-6` · L127 — **exile as the mode of arrival**
*"Settled: Post-Falkland Treaty."* **Founding population: Australian exiles (L129).** Exile is a **verdict**
mechanism: somebody was decided against, elsewhere. **Written:** the arrival. **Not written:** who decided, on
what criterion, whether the verdict is reviewable, and whether it is **hereditary**.
⚠ **Classification caution:** this is almost certainly **Determined** at Federation level, not Davis's to answer.
Per NO FORCED FIT I name the question and decline to site an answer here.
> **OPEN QUESTION:** *Is exile still a live arrival mechanism into Davis in Act 2, or a founding-era fact only?
> The admitted set does not say, and the two produce different cities.*

### ⭐⭐ `A-7` · L129 against L20/L32 — **the founding wave is not the largest community, and no conversion is written**
L129: the founding population is **Australian exiles**. L20/L32/L39: the Primary tier is **China at 19.04%**;
**Australia is Significant at 4.93%.**
**Written:** two endpoints. **Not written:** **anything in between** — no arrival waves, no dates, no order, no
mechanism by which a city founded by one population came to be dominated numerically by another. The census is a
pair of snapshots with no intervening history anywhere in the admitted set.
**And the Gate 9 half specifically:** *"founding wave"* is still used as a live descriptor at L8, L129 and L178 —
so the **label** persists after the **demographic weight** does not.
> ⭐ **This is the largest hole in the inherited material, and it is entirely peer-free.**
> **OPEN QUESTIONS:** *How did the other twenty communities arrive, and when? What does founding-wave standing
> still get a resident once it is 4.93% of the city — and what does it no longer get?*

### `A-8` · L143 — **"breadbasket."** A supply obligation written only as supply
**Written:** Davis produces — sheltered agriculture and greenhouse cultivation are *"the clear majority of daily
activity."* **Not written:** **the shortfall.** No failed year, no store, no reserve, no fallback, no rationing,
no consequence to anyone if the harvest is short. A supply obligation with no failure mode written is a threshold
documented from the favorable side only.
> ⚠ Tagged **`PA-3`** — the outward direction of the food obligation is a provisional assumption about the
> unwritten parent.
> **OPEN QUESTIONS:** *What happens here in a bad year, and to whom first? Does Davis hold a reserve, and does it
> hold it for itself or for the obligation?*

### ⭐ `A-9` · L143 — **libraries welcoming smuggled Upper Earth books.** An admission mechanism, welcome-only
**Written:** *"Davis's libraries are also genuinely welcoming toward books smuggled out of Upper Earth."*
⚠ *(The comparative clause and the four-city roster are struck under §4.2 and are not used here.)*
**Not written:** what is **refused**; whether anything is; **who decides**; what happens to the person who
carried it; whether accession is recorded or deliberately not recorded; whether a book once in can be taken out.
**A library is an admission mechanism and this one has only an entrance written.**
> **OPEN QUESTIONS:** *Is anything turned away, and on whose judgment? Is the carrier's name written down, and is
> not writing it down the actual institution?*

### ⭐ `A-10` · L143 — **the 2026-07-16 industry reassignment leaves an unexplained in-world absence**
**Written:** mining and quarrying were reassigned away from Davis; the Division of Industry was rewritten around
agriculture and research. **Not written, in-world:** why a city holding **~400 km² of exposed ice-free rock**
(L60, carried on L143's authority) does not work stone. The reassignment is an authoring act; the silence it
leaves is a fact about the place.
> ⛔ **I do not fill it.** `00_Frame.md` §0.5 reserved finding `A-1` sits immediately adjacent (disposal of the
> dead, highest-risk reserved decision) and states the constraint without converting it into a practice. I hold
> the same line.
> **OPEN QUESTION:** *Does Davis not quarry, or does Davis not quarry* commercially *— and if the rock is not an
> industry, what is it?*

### `A-11` · L6 — **"Access type: ON."** A binary with only its positive value present
**Written:** the value. **Not written:** the value set, the complement, what a non-`ON` city lacks, who assigns
it, or whether it can change.
> ⛔ Per the sanctioned fall-short, I take **the most conservative reading and name the problem**: the closed set
> is **unknown to this pass**, and I do not widen it by guessing what `ON` is opposed to.
> **OPEN QUESTION:** *What are the other values of `Access type`, what does the field decide, and is it ever
> revoked?*

### `A-12` · L7 — **Hwy 110.** Direction written, volume and refusal not
**Written:** Davis sits on the Coastal Cut Highway with road access in both directions *(stated as relation —
delete every other name and the claim survives)*. **Not written:** throughput (`REQ-G5v`, and I confirm no figure
exists in the admitted set); **and, entirely absent, any closure, inspection, precedence, or seasonal-impassability
regime.** A road is an admission mechanism and this one has no refusal condition written at all.
> ⚠ **`PA-4`** covers existence and direction (cheap) and is **explicitly unsafe for magnitude.**
> **OPEN QUESTION:** *Is Hwy 110 ever closed to Davis, and what is a city whose declared output is food while its
> road is shut?*

### ⭐ `A-13` · L155 — **Prydz Bay harbor, "the primary maritime entry."** A literal gate with no closed state
**Written:** the harbor exists and is primary. **Not written:** **sea ice.** The admitted climate table carries
monthly means at or below −15 °C for June–September (L100–103); the harbor's seasonal availability is stated
nowhere in the 137 admitted lines.
⭐ Note the contrast one line above: **L154 writes the lakes' two states explicitly** — *"ice-covered in winter,
partially open in summer."* **The file demonstrably can write a seasonal closed state. For the harbor it did
not.**
> **OPEN QUESTIONS:** *When is the harbor closed, for how long, and what is Davis during that time? Does the
> closure fall inside or outside the growing season?*

### `A-14` · L83 — **the regime reclassification.** New verdict written, its predecessor's output not
**Written:** KATABATIC MARGIN → **ICE-FREE OASIS**, with the operating authority's ground, and the flat statement
*"A katabatic label was wrong."* **Not written:** **what the wrong label produced while it stood**, and which
inherited Davis material predates 2026-09-04 and rests on it.
> **OPEN QUESTION (inheritance hazard, for Step 2):** *Which surviving Davis claims were written under the
> katabatic reading and have not been re-checked against the oasis reading?*

### ⭐ `A-15` · L84–L86 — **~45 mm/yr "lost."** Subtracted from the ledger, never written as arriving
**Written, and creditably two-sided:** falls ~73 · lands ~28 · lost ~45. The mass balance closes (73 − 45 = 28).
**Not written:** *lost to sublimation and* **wind transport** means roughly 45 mm/yr of water is **moved, not
destroyed.** In a city of fjord inlets, lake basins and exposed rock, what is scoured from one surface **lands on
another.** The ledger is Davis-wide and hides an entirely internal redistribution.
> ⭐ This is a Gate 9 fire that stays wholly inside Davis and needs no second place.
> **OPEN QUESTION:** *Where does the transported fraction come to rest — which slopes, inlets and lee sides
> accumulate, and is anyone here building, growing or walking with that pattern in mind?*

### `A-16` · L8 — **the "Significance" field** is a one-sided grading instrument
It records what makes Davis significant and has no capacity to record what it is not significant for, or what
befalls a place that scores low. ⚠ `00.1` §4.1 cleared L8 **on content** (attribute-tier, no capability or
temperament verdict) — **I agree with the clearance and note separately that the field's *shape* is one-sided.**
> **OPEN QUESTION:** *Is "Significance" an in-world designation with consequences, or an authoring header?*

### `A-17` · L5 — **subnet assignment.** The assignment is written; its content is not
L5 records Davis's subnet and the 2026-07-03 correction from a different one. **Not written:** what membership
**obliges** and **provides** — which is precisely `01` §1.1's defining question for a Polity, asked of the thing
Davis belongs to.
> ⚠ **`PA-6`** governs: with the parent unwritten this returns **UNKNOWN, which is not NOTHING.**
> **OPEN QUESTION:** *What does Mirny-subnet membership cost Davis and give it — and is subnet assignment ever
> changed in-world, as opposed to in the documents?*

### `A-18` · `DR-4` / L15–L16 — **Census I governs, and the date it stops describing Davis is nowhere**
⭐ **Credit first:** `00_Frame.md` §0.1 Configuration (b) writes this one **both ways** — it states plainly that
the band is ruled by a snapshot the frame's later half does not describe. That is Gate 9 satisfied at the pass
level.
**The residue:** **no inherited line dates the transition.** The frame knows Amundsen Tower ~2688 and that
orbital migration is a lived event; the census pair carries **no date at all**. So Davis is declared Band 5 for an
unstated fraction of its own 248 years.
> **OPEN QUESTION, and it is load-bearing for Step 2:** *At what point in 2564–2812 does 1,158,314 stop being
> Davis's population — and is the departure an event, a decade, or a century?*

### `A-19` · L148 — **an admitted line that is a declared placeholder**
*"Vestfold Hills oasis resources…, Prydz Bay maritime trade, logistics within the Mirny subnet. Full detail TBD."*
**One of the 137 admitted lines is explicitly unwritten.** Its three nouns are a promise, not content.
> ⚠ **Flagged as a placeholder, and to be re-flagged on every reuse.** It is not evidence for anything; it is a
> record that somebody intended evidence.

## A.2 · WHERE THE FILE DID WRITE BOTH SIDES — recorded, because it proves the omissions are not stylistic

| | Two-sided item |
|---|---|
| **L154** | The lakes: *"ice-covered in winter, partially open in summer."* Both states, explicitly. |
| **L84–86** | The precipitation ledger: falls, lands **and** lost, with a closing mass balance. |
| **L131** | The Saint exclusion carries its **criterion** — rare, and the reason `A-1` is gradeable at all. |
| **`00_Frame.md` §0.1 Status** | The Census I→II fall is explicitly **not** read as Declining, with both the migration reading and its two independent grounds stated. |
| **`00.1` §7 / `00_Frame.md` §0.3** | Generator grades carry an unfavorable side (*"runs thinner"*) **and** a route back (re-grounding). |

## A.3 · ⭐ THE SHAPE — what nineteen fires have in common

`00_RUNBOOK.md` L2494 predicted a very high rate. **Nineteen fires across 137 admitted spec lines and 9 census
lines confirms it.** But the rate is the less interesting half. **The shape is uniform:**

> ### ***Davis's inherited material documents what ARRIVES and is near-silent on what leaves or is turned away.***

Exiles arrive (L127). Documents arrive (L127). Twenty-one communities arrive (L20–22). A highway arrives (L7).
A harbor admits (L155). Smuggled books are welcomed (L143). Precipitation falls (L84). Food is produced (L143).
**Every one of these is written as an intake with no refusal condition.**

**The one enormous outflow — 376,718 people — is recorded as a number and not as a mechanism** (`A-3`), and the
one thing genuinely written as leaving is **water** (`A-15`, ~45 mm/yr), which the file removes from the ledger
without asking where it goes.

⛔ **I do not convert this into a culture finding — that is Step 2's work.** I record it as the audit's structural
result: **the unfavorable side of Davis is, as of Step 1, almost entirely unwritten**, and the two places it is
most conspicuously absent (`A-3` the departure, `A-7` the founding wave's demotion) are both about **people
becoming fewer or lesser**, which is exactly the class `04` L219 says nobody perceives as a problem.

# 1.B · INHERITANCE CLASSIFICATION — `01` §5.1, **ACT-STAMPED**

## B.0 · ⚠ TWO STRUCTURAL PROBLEMS WITH THE INSTRUMENT, NAMED BEFORE USE

**1 · `01` §5.1 assumes ONE parent. Davis has two live ancestors and one of them is unwritten.**
The **Mirny Arcanet subnet** (unwritten ⇒ `PA-6`: UNKNOWN, not NOTHING) and, above it, the **Tepenian
Federation** (partly knowable). *"What the parent determines"* therefore has **two different answers with two
different epistemic statuses**, and the table has one column for it. ⛔ **Every call below is stamped with
WHICH ancestor it refers to.** Without that stamp a Determined call silently launders an unknown into a fact —
which is the exact failure `PA-6` exists to prevent.

**2 · `M-157` is real and it bites hardest here.** The `Determined` class **widens** across Act 1 → Act 2 because
a shared Tepenian identity becomes something the national layer supplies; `Inflected` and `Originated` narrow
correspondingly. **Every call is stamped ACT 1 / ACT 2 / BOTH.** The frame is **mostly Act 2** (`00_Frame.md`
§0.1: Act 1 ≈ the first 18%), so an unstamped call would be wrong for roughly one-fifth of the frame and would
not look wrong.

## B.1 · DETERMINED — *fixed above; a local variant must not be invented*

| Element | Source of determination | Act | PA-tag |
|---|---|---|---|
| Climate; temperature; the 72.8 mm precipitation budget; polar night ~37 d; midnight sun ~55 d; wind ~5.6 m/s out of the katabatic regime (L68–L114) | **Physics and latitude** — no institution | **BOTH**, invariant | none |
| Coordinates; Vestfold Hills; ~400 km² ice-free; the lake system; fjord inlets; Prydz Bay (L3, L60, L153–155, L176 premise) | **Physical geography** | **BOTH**, invariant | none |
| Robot physiology baseline — human-looking, clothed, gel-brained, vacuum-capable, not altitude-limited, cold a real cost | **Universe-level** (`Robot_Physiology…`, per `00_Frame.md` §0.2) | **BOTH** | none |
| Subnet membership itself (L5) | **Subnet / Federation** | **BOTH** as far as the admitted set shows | ⚠ `PA-2` (Davis is not the hub ⇒ Determined is *wider* for Davis) |
| Hwy 110's existence and routing (L7) | **Above Davis** — a city does not route a highway | **BOTH** | `PA-4` (direction only; ⛔ never magnitude) |
| Exile as the founding mode, and whatever institution performs it (L127, L129) | **Federation / national** | **ACT 1** primarily; ⚠ Act 2 status unwritten (`A-6`) | none |
| Currency | **Federation** — `01` §5.1 names currency Determined | **BOTH** | ⛔ content **RESERVED** by `DR-3`; class known, value withheld |
| ⭐ **A shared Tepenian identity** | **Federation**, via interconnection, solidified by Amundsen Tower ~2688 | ⛔ **ACT 2 ONLY — does not exist as a Determined inheritance in Act 1** | none |
| Arcanet as ambient working utility | **Federation buildout** | ⛔ **ACT 2 ONLY.** `PA-1` dates national buildout from ~2614 with an unreconciled earlier local-hub date ⇒ **safe as "for most of the frame," unsafe as "from 2564"** | `PA-1` |

⛔ **UNKNOWN, not empty:** the **calendar** and the **language family** — `01` §5.1's own two named examples of
Determined content — **appear nowhere in the admitted set.** Recorded as UNKNOWN. **NO FORCED FIT: I do not
supply Davis a local version of either, and I do not record their absence as a Davis gap.**

## B.2 · INFLECTED — *the parent supplies the form, Davis supplies its version.* `01` §5.1: **where most good local culture lives**

| Element | The form (from where) | Davis's version | Act |
|---|---|---|---|
| ⭐ **Food** | The Federation needs calories (`PA-3`, outward) | **Sheltered agriculture and greenhouse cultivation as the clear majority of daily activity** (L143) | **BOTH** |
| ⭐ **Research** | A national scientific life (unknown in detail) | **Ecological / limnological**, anchored on water bodies Davis physically has (L143 + L154 + L176 premise) | **BOTH** |
| ⭐⭐ **Libraries receiving smuggled Upper Earth books** | ⭐ **The form is demonstrably inherited — L143 itself says the practice is broader than Davis** *(the roster naming where is struck and not used)* | **Davis's libraries are genuinely welcoming** (L143) | ⚠ **undated in the admitted set** — probably Act 2, unestablished |
| **Arcanet use** | Federation utility (`PA-1`) | ⛔ **SLOT OPEN** — nothing inherited says how Davis uses it | **ACT 2** |
| ⭐ **Robot cultural practice** | Universe-level physiology + practice | ⛔ **SLOT OPEN and MUST STAY OPEN** — `00_Frame.md` §0.2's declared exposure leaves Davis's position on these topics explicitly open; later convergence is **corroboration at best and must be tagged** | **BOTH** |
| **Maritime working life** | National shipping and trade | A Prydz Bay harbor with an unwritten closed state (`A-13`) | **BOTH** |
| **Exile-descent identity** | The national exile system | How a documentary-founded community understands its own arrival | ⭐ **ACT 1 Inflected → narrows in ACT 2** as Determined identity widens over it |
| ⭐⭐ **The cultures the 21 communities carry** (L20–22, L32–52) | Composition names the **stock**; time, separation, environment and local hardship produce the **culture** | Twenty-one origins under one set of local conditions | ⭐⭐ **ACT 1: wide open — people are still "X who live in Antarctica." ACT 2: origin is ancestry, not identity, and the class narrows sharply toward Determined-plus-ancestry** |

> ### ⭐⭐ `M-157`'S CLEAREST INSTANCE IN THE WHOLE INHERITED SET
> **The classification of L20–L52 inverts across the Act boundary.** The identical table is **Inflected-wide** in
> Act 1 and **Determined-heavy** in Act 2. **A single unstamped call on the composition table would be wrong for
> roughly 18% of the frame and would look exactly like a correct call.** ⛔ Any later phase reading the tier table
> must state which Act it is reading it for.

## B.3 · ORIGINATED — *exists here, from nowhere above*

| Element | Why it qualifies | Act |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐⭐ **Practical terrain mastery rebuilt from documents with no teaching institution** (L127) | Nothing above supplied it. The handoff chain delivered **paper, not people**, and mastery was built *"independently, over generations of their own."* **The single clearest Originated item in the admitted set** | ⭐ **ORIGINATES IN ACT 1** |
| **Whatever Davis does about hypersaline lakes and landlocked marine basins** (L176 premise) | The premise is admitted; **the answer does not exist anywhere** | ⛔ **SLOT EXPLICITLY OPEN — an empty slot is a RESULT** |

⚠ **A case that does not fit the closed set, named rather than resolved by widening it.**
Davis's **ecological/limnological research tradition** is **Inflected in form** (research is inherited) and
**Originated in object** (its subject is water bodies only Davis has). `01` §5.1 says every element is *exactly*
one class. **This one is two.** Per the sanctioned fall-short I take **the most conservative value — `Inflected`**
— because `01` §5.1 states the error `Originated` exists to prevent is *"claiming origination for something
inherited,"* and I **name the problem** rather than adding a class.

> ### ⚠⚠ A SECOND GAP IN THE INSTRUMENT — `Originated` does not stay `Originated`
> The four classes are defined **relative to the parent**, and there is **no class for "originated here in Act 1,
> then inherited by this place's own later residents."** Yet that is exactly what L127's terrain mastery is by
> Act 2: for a resident of 2750 it is not something their city invented, it is something their city **has**, with
> no living author. ⛔ **NO FORCED FIT — I do not invent a fifth class.** I record the call as **`Originated`
> (Act 1) / functionally locally-Determined (Act 2)** and flag that the instrument cannot express the transition.
> **This is `M-157` pointing at a second edge: the act-blindness is not only about the parent widening — a
> location's own origination ages into inheritance.**

## B.4 · AGGREGATED — *the parent's character is partly the sum or tension of its children*

**Two directions, and they resolve oppositely:**

- ⛔ **Davis AS a parent: the class is EMPTY — and empty BY DEFERRAL, not by absence.** `00_Frame.md` declares
  **Children: NONE — BY DEFERRAL**; `01` §2.2's 3→4 threshold says a location this size **must** be decomposed,
  and `DR-4` suspends it. So `01` §5.4's Uniform / Patterned / Delegated declaration **cannot run at all**, and
  every Davis-internal claim this pass makes is **implicitly Uniform** — which `01` §5.4 says is the mark of a
  pass *"not written at its own scale."* ✅ This is already declared as Configuration **EXCEPTIONAL** (a) and
  is **suspended, not cancelled.**
- ⚠ **Davis AS a child aggregating into the subnet: 100% PROVISIONAL.** What Davis contributes upward is the
  **food obligation** (`PA-3`, the assumption with the most hanging on it) and its **highway-plus-harbor position**
  (`PA-4`, safe for direction, unsafe for magnitude). With the parent unwritten, `PA-6` governs: **UNKNOWN, which
  is not NOTHING.** ⛔ **No Aggregated claim in this pass may be stated unprovisionally.**

## B.5 · ⭐ THE ACT-STAMP SUMMARY — one table, because it is the deliverable

| | **ACT 1** (2564 → early 2600s) | **ACT 2** (~late 2600s/2700s → 2812) |
|---|---|---|
| **Determined** | Physics · geography · robot physiology · subnet · highway · exile · currency | **All of the above, PLUS: shared Tepenian identity · Arcanet as ambient utility** ⭐ *the class visibly widens* |
| **Inflected** | **WIDE** — 21 carried origin cultures near the surface; food; research; maritime work; exile-descent identity | **NARROWER** — origin becomes ancestry; food, research, libraries, robot practice and maritime work remain the live inflections |
| **Originated** | ⭐ **Terrain mastery rebuilt from documents** (originates here) | **Narrows** — the Act 1 origination has aged into local inheritance; only the hypersaline/landlocked-basin slot is open, and it is open, not filled |
| **Aggregated** | Provisional upward only; empty downward | Identical — **and the downward emptiness is by `DR-4`, not by fact** |

## B.6 · EMPTY SLOTS, DECLARED AS RESULTS — ⛔ NO FORCED FIT, BOTH DIRECTIONS

**None is sited here** must never be read as **none is possible here.**

- **Calendar** — UNKNOWN (not in the admitted set). No Davis version invented.
- **Language family** — UNKNOWN. No Davis version invented.
- **Arcanet's local use** — SLOT OPEN.
- **Robot cultural practice at Davis** — SLOT OPEN **and held open by rule** (`00_Frame.md` §0.2 exposure).
- **Hypersaline / landlocked-basin use** — SLOT OPEN. The premise is canon; the answer is not.
- **Children / sub-locations** — EMPTY BY DEFERRAL.
- **Demonym, proper names, currency, DLC siting, disposal of the dead, `G1`'s Element meaning** — RESERVED, not
  empty. Different thing, recorded differently.

# 1.C · EPISTEMIC STATUS OF THE INHERITED CLAIMS

**Six tiers. The distinction that matters most is `C-2` vs `C-1`: a modeled number and a measured number look
identical on the page.**

### `C-1` · RATIFIED CANON — developer-authored or on a ratified root
L1, L3 (designation, coordinates) · L5 (subnet) · L6, L7 (access, highway) · L8 (significance, cleared **on
content** at `00.1` §4.1) · **L127, L129** (founding; L127 dated-refined 2026-07-25) · L131 (the Saint verdict) ·
**L143** (function — explicitly *developer-ratified*, and the authority on which *"largest ice-free oasis"*
survives) · L153–155 (terrain) · census L392–398, L496, L613 (composition and totals).

### `C-2` · ⚠ PRIOR DERIVATION — a pass performed it; it is a model, not a measurement
- **L30–L52, the per-nation table** — share-weighted (L54) then de-stacked by randomization (L28). ⛔ **Usable for
  naming portable institutions; never readable as measured per-nation counts** — and, per §1.0, **incapable in
  principle of showing differential retention.**
- **L84–L86, falls/lands/lost** — a coefficient model (L91 names the mechanism file; L91 is **DO-NOT-FOLLOW**).
- **L83, the regime label** — a 2026-09-04 research pass, itself a correction of an earlier pass.
- **L74, the temperature range** — a derivation, and a defective one (`R-3`, re-derived at §1.E).
- **L117, L120** — superlatives **a prior pass performed**; PARTIAL-struck and not used.

### `C-3` · MEASURED — real-world instrument data, GPS-legal as physical substrate
L68 (BAS READER, 1991–2020 WMO normal) · L71 mean temp · L72 wind · L73 record extremes · L79 annual
precipitation · the L95–L106 table's Rec High / Rec Low / Avg High / Avg Low / Precip / Precip Prob, per L108 and
L111's ⭐ *measured* marks · Daylight computed from Davis's own latitude.
⚠ **Caveat at `R-8` (§1.E): the `Mean` column — the single most-used climate number in the pass — is addressed
only under a superseded column name.**

### `C-4` · BARE ASSERTION — stated, unsourced, unmechanized
L6 `Access type: ON` (no definition, no source, no value set) · L8's **selection** of what counts as significant ·
L129's founding population (ratified, but with **no arrival mechanism, no dates, no waves** — see `A-7`) ·
L18–L22's tier **semantics** (the ranks are canon; what a rank *means* is nowhere).

### `C-5` · ⚠ PLACEHOLDER
**L148** — *"Full detail TBD."* An admitted line with no content. **Re-flag on every reuse.**

### `C-6` · ⛔ RESERVED — no epistemic status may be assigned
`G1`'s Element meaning (`DR-1`) · currency (`DR-3`) · the demonym · proper names of people · which DLC covers the
subnet · disposal of the dead. **Plus the permanently REFUSED `Why` column of the symbol assignment.**

### ⛔ `C-7` · INADMISSIBLE CONTENT SITTING INSIDE ADMITTED LINES
**Census L496 and L613 open with `| 13 |` and `| 14 |` — row indices in an inter-city ordering.** They are
**rank artifacts** under the ONE LOCATION law, they sit inside admitted lines, **the contract strikes neither**,
and **the index changes between the two censuses**, which is itself a comparative statement. ⛔ **I did not read,
use or interpret either integer.** Reported in DEFECTS.

# 1.D · ⛔ THE `G6` RE-GROUNDING — Step 1's assigned task. **COUNTS ONLY; THE MATCHING LINE WAS NOT OPENED.**

`00_Frame.md` §0.7 hands Step 1: *"RE-GROUND `G6`. The null must be re-tested against the universe repo's 346-line
`World_History_Reference.md`, not the GDD's 7-line stub."*

**What I ran — `wc` and `grep -c` only. No `-n`, no `-o`, no content displayed.**

```
GDD copy   .../InnerTepeniaGDD/Worldspace/World_History_Reference.md
           7 lines, 467 bytes
           lines matching "Davis"  (case-sensitive) : 0
           lines matching "davis"  (case-insensitive): 0

Universe   .../Reference/TepenianUniverseTimeline/Reference/World_History_Reference.md
           346 lines, 31,826 bytes
           lines matching "Davis"     (case-sensitive) : 1
           lines matching "davis"     (case-insensitive): 1
           lines matching whole-word "Davis"            : 1
           lines matching "John King Davis"             : 0
           lines matching "Davis Station"               : 0
           lines matching "Mirny"                       : 0
           lines matching "Vestfold"                    : 0
           lines matching "Prydz"                       : 0
```

## What the evidence now supports — stated precisely, and no further

1. ✅ **`00_Frame.md` §0.3's diagnosis is confirmed exactly.** The GDD file is a **7-line, 467-byte** stub
   returning **0**. **A zero out of seven lines is very nearly uninformative**, and `REQ-G6`'s single stated
   ground is therefore **confirmed insufficient to establish the null.** ⭐ `M-117` — *a name is not an address* —
   landing on the evidence for a **recorded absence**, the claim class nothing downstream re-checks.
2. ✅ **The 346-line file and its single match are confirmed.**
3. ⭐⭐ **NEW, beyond §0.3, and obtained without opening the line.** Counting the candidate strings **eliminates
   both GPS-provenance readings of that match**: it is **not** *"John King Davis"* (0) and **not** *"Davis
   Station"* (0). Both are the strings that would have made the match irrelevant to the city, and both are
   already in my admitted set (L8, L62, L64, L131). **So the one match is the bare token — most plausibly the
   city or a polity-level entity of that name, not the namesake and not the real station.**
4. ⭐ **NEW — an independent corroboration of `PA-5`, from a direction the frame did not have.** In the same
   346-line file, **"Mirny" = 0, "Vestfold" = 0, "Prydz" = 0.** The subnet and Davis's geography are absent from
   it entirely. `PA-5` (*the subnet supplies no defining in-frame event*) now has evidence rather than only being
   declared, **and it remains correctly NOT BUILT ON.**

## ⛔ What the evidence does **NOT** support, and what I therefore refuse to conclude

**A single matching line is compatible with BOTH verdicts.** It could be a roster entry, a founding enumeration,
a table row, a heading, or an out-of-frame (post-2812) reference — none of which is an in-frame defining event —
or it could be one. **Counting cannot separate these, and reading the line is forbidden to me at this step.**

> ### ⭐ SO THE HONEST OUTPUT IS NOT A RE-GROUNDING. IT IS A NARROWED, ESCALATED REQUEST.
> **`REQ-G6`'s stated ground is REFUTED. The null itself is neither established nor disproved.**
> **Step 1 cannot discharge the task `00_Frame.md` §0.7 assigned it**, because the only operation that settles it
> is one this step's own contract forbids. Saying so is the result; routing around it would not be.
>
> **`REQ-G6-b` — the bounded adjudication that would settle it, handed to the pass owner:**
> *open exactly one line of `TepenianUniverseTimeline/Reference/World_History_Reference.md` under a one-line read
> contract, and grade it on three values only:* **(a) in-frame defining event · (b) out-of-frame · (c) not an
> event.** *Nothing else from that file is admitted.*
>
> **The cost of leaving it open:** `G6` is the **only** generator graded ABSENT; it is not selected, so nothing
> currently rests on it, and the pass proceeds either way — but `00_Frame.md` §0.3's re-grade (*"runs thinner
> **on the event axis**, and the era file routes the substitute question straight into `G8`"*) is the disposition
> I confirm as correct **on the evidence as it now stands.**

> ### ⭐ A REUSABLE TECHNIQUE, RECORDED BECAUSE IT WAS NOT OBVIOUS
> **A counts-only probe can eliminate candidate readings of a match without reading it**, by counting the
> candidate strings themselves. Here it converted *"1 match, unknown meaning"* into *"1 match, and it is neither
> of the two GPS strings"* — real discrimination, from `grep -c` alone. **Offered for the quarantine toolkit
> alongside `M-223`.**

# 1.E · INDEPENDENT RE-CHECK OF THE INHERITED DEFECT DOCKET

⛔ **Re-derived from the admitted lines, not inherited from the prior grading. Finding a defect is not
authorization to fix it — nothing below was applied to any file.**

### `R-3` — ⭐ **CONFIRMED, AND IT IS A THREE-WAY PROBLEM, NOT A TWO-COLUMN ONE**
L74 states *"coldest months avg −21 °C; warmest month avg 0 °C."* From the L95–L106 table:

| Column | Minimum | Maximum |
|---|---|---|
| **Mean** | **−17.1** (Jul, Aug) | **+0.9** (Jan) |
| **Avg High (day)** | −14.3 (Jul) | **+3.2** (Jan) |
| **Avg Low (night)** | **−20.8** (Aug) | −1.2 (Jan) |

⇒ **L74's cold endpoint (−21) is an Avg-Low figure. Its warm endpoint (0) is a Mean figure — and not even that
column's maximum;** it corresponds to **December's +0.0** while **January's Mean is +0.9.** So L74 is
**cross-column AND non-extremal**: it is not a correct statement of any one column's range.
⭐ **I confirm `00_Frame.md` §0.3's re-grade that this is bigger than `R-3`'s original wording conveys, and add
the non-extremal half.** **Months at or above freezing: 2 by Mean (Jan, Dec); 2 by Avg High (Jan, Dec — February's
+(−0.2) does not qualify); 0 by Avg Low.** For a city whose only function line is cultivation, that count is a
primary input.

### `R-7` — ⭐ **CONFIRMED AND FULLY DIAGNOSED, INDEPENDENTLY**
L84 falls **~73** · L85 lands **~28 (~45% retention)** · L86 lost **~45** · L79 annual **72.8**.
`28 ÷ 72.8 = 38.46%`; `28 ÷ 73 = 38.36%`; `73 − 45 = 28` ✅; `45 ÷ 73 = 61.6%`.
⇒ ***the millimeter figure for what is LOST (45) has been reused as the retention PERCENT (~45%).*** **The mass
balance is sound, which isolates the error to the one parenthetical.** **True retention ≈ 38.5%.**
✅ **I independently reach `00_Frame.md` §0.3's diagnosis and support the re-grade from *"unresolvable in-pass"*
to *"diagnosed; correction proposed."*** ⛔ Not applied.

### `R-8` — ⭐ **CONFIRMED, AND SHARPER THAN STATED**
L93's header carries **Rec High · Avg High (day) · Mean · Avg Low (night) · Rec Low · Precip · Precip Prob ·
Daylight**. L111's provenance note names **Avg Temp · Temp Range · Avg Precip · Precip Probability · Avg
Daylight**. ⇒ **"Avg Temp" and "Temp Range" are not columns of this table.** L108 separately covers Rec High/Rec
Low and glosses Avg High/Avg Low.
⛔ **Net effect, which `R-8` does not state: the `Mean` column has no provenance statement under its own name** —
and the `Mean` column is what L71's **−10.0 °C** (the pass's single most-used climate number) is computed from.
**The defect is not cosmetic.**
> ### ⭐ `R-3` AND `R-8` ARE ONE DEFECT SEEN FROM TWO SIDES — *offered as an interpretation, not as established fact*
> A superseded **"Temp Range"** column was collapsed into the summary line **L74**; the collapse read its two
> endpoints off two different surviving columns; and the provenance note **L111** was never updated and still
> describes the column that no longer exists. **That single authoring history explains both defects and predicts
> that any other artifact derived from "Temp Range" is suspect.** ⛔ Stated as a diagnosis. Not applied.

### `R-9` (pointer staleness) and `R-5` (ratification-by-banner)
**Out of my admitted set** — both concern files I was not contracted to open. **Not re-checked. Logged as
not-reached rather than assumed clean**, per `00_Frame.md` §0.4's own precedent.

### ⭐ NEW — NOT ON THE DOCKET: **every one of the twelve monthly record highs is above freezing**
Rec High by month: **+13.0 · +10.0 · +4.3 · +4.2 · +2.0 · +2.0 · +0.8 · +1.0 · +0.4 · +1.9 · +8.0 · +11.0.**
**Minimum = +0.4 °C (September). All twelve exceed 0 °C — including June (+2.0), July (+0.8) and August (+1.0),
which span or adjoin the ~37-day polar night.**
⭐ **And it is internally corroborated by a separately-sourced part of the same file:** L83's 2026-09-04
reclassification to **ICE-FREE OASIS** — *out of the katabatic regime, Prydz Bay maritime influence, rock
moderating the local climate* — is exactly the regime that **predicts** midwinter above-freezing excursions. The
measured record-high row and the reclassified regime label **agree, having been derived independently.**
⛔ **I do not turn this into a culture finding.** I hand it to Step 2 as a `G2` fact with obvious consequences for
cultivation, for storage, and for anything left outdoors in a city that has no month it can rely on staying
frozen. **It passes both ONE LOCATION tests.**

# 2 · DEFECTS AND DOUBTS

### `D-1` ⛔ **The contract admits a rank artifact it never strikes.** Census L496/L613 open `| 13 |` and `| 14 |`
Those integers are **positions in an inter-city ordering** — inadmissible under the ONE LOCATION law — and they
sit **inside admitted lines with no partial strike.** Worse: **the index differs between the two rows**, so the
pair encodes "Davis moved in an ordering," a comparative claim. ⛔ **I did not read, use or interpret them.**
**The `Specs/Davis.md` contract is meticulous about partial strikes; the census contract has none at all.**

### `D-2` ⚠⚠ **The contract and the recommended second test DISAGREE on L178, and my brief tells me to apply both**
`00.1` §4.2 rules L62's *"subnet/founding-wave roster"* **legal relation**, and admits **L178's first clause**.
But `00_Frame.md` §0.3's second test — *"does this claim require knowing anything about any other place in order
to be true?"* — which my brief explicitly recommends I apply — **L178's roster clause FAILS.**
⭐ **Conservative resolution taken:** I used **only** the Davis-internal residue (*Davis is a founding-wave
Australian city*), which **L8 and L129 supply independently**, and **declined the roster half and L179 entirely.**
**Nothing in my output depends on either.** **The two instruments genuinely disagree and a ruling is owed.**

### `D-3` ⭐⭐ **The second test, if adopted as written, would strike a line §4.2 expressly KEEPS**
L143's *"the largest ice-free oasis in the country"* **fails** the second test — it cannot be true without
knowing about other places. Yet §4.2 **keeps it**, correctly, on the ground that it is **developer-ratified
inherited fact** carried on L143's authority, *"never on L120's or L60's (rankings a prior pass performed)."*
> ⭐ **The distinction §4.2 is making — a RATIFIED SUPERLATIVE IS A FACT; A PASS-PERFORMED SUPERLATIVE IS AN
> INSTRUMENT — is precisely the distinction the second test cannot make.**
> **Recommendation to the open docket item (`00_Frame.md` §0.7): if the second test is adopted, it needs a
> provenance exception, or it will strike ratified canon.** ⛔ Offered, not applied.

### `D-4` ⚠ **`01` §5.1 has one parent column and Davis has two ancestors with different epistemic statuses**
See §B.0. Without a per-call stamp, a **Determined** verdict silently launders the unwritten subnet's UNKNOWN
into a fact — the exact failure `PA-6` exists to prevent. **I stamped every call. The instrument does not ask for
the stamp.**

### `D-5` ⚠ **`01` §5.1 has no class for an origination that ages into an inheritance**
See §B.3. L127's terrain mastery is **Originated** in Act 1 and functionally **locally Determined** by Act 2.
⛔ **NO FORCED FIT — I did not invent a fifth class**; I took the conservative value and named the problem.
**This is a second edge of `M-157` that the `M-157` warning does not currently mention:** it warns that the
*parent's* Determined class widens; it does not warn that a *location's own* Originated material converts.

### `D-6` ⚠ **A citation in `00.1` §4.2 does not resolve.** The **L21** row says what survives is *"tier + figures
at **L27, L37**."* **L37 is the Russia row and is correct. `Specs/Davis.md` L27 is a blank line.** The tier
statement survives at **L21 itself** and at **census L397**. **No content harm; a reader chasing L27 finds
nothing.** ⭐ **Same class as the two `M-117` instances §0.3 already catches inside this very file** — a third
occurrence, unpatched.

### `D-7` ⚠⚠ **`G4` rests on a line that imports the real site's occupancy history in anonymized form**
L127 states the station *"was continuously maintained by a rotating succession of national operators"* through the
First Interwar, with the nations declared irrelevant. **The binding GPS statement is that a place is characterized
by who lives there, not whose site it occupies** — and the standing clarification in this project is that the rule
covers **a real site's lineage, abandonment and vacancy, not just its nationality.** **L127's "chain of handoffs"
is site lineage with the names removed.**
⚠ **Countervailing, and I weight it heavily:** L127 is **ratified canon on a ratified root**, refined by a dated
**GPS-purposes-only pass (2026-07-25)**, and a ratified canon line outranks a reader's application of a rule to it.
**The live question is only whether that 2026-07-25 refinement was made with the later lineage clarification in
view.** ⛔ **I do not adjudicate it — it is a developer ruling.** **But `G4` is a SELECTED generator, so if the
ruling goes the other way the pass loses one of its three.** **Filed.**

### `D-8` ⚠ **`00.1` §3.3 discloses half of the model's scope limit**
It says the per-nation figures are *"usable for named portable institutions; never readable as measured per-nation
counts."* **It does not say the model makes retention identical across all 21 communities by construction** — which
is the half that bears on the pass's headline question. **Recommend the scope limit be extended, not replaced.**

### `D-9` ⚠ **One of the 137 admitted lines is a declared placeholder.** L148 ends *"Full detail TBD."*
The admitted-set count is a count of **lines**, not of content. **Flagged; to be re-flagged on every reuse.**

### `D-10` ⭐ **KILLED FINDING, LOGGED PER STEP 9.5 — I was wrong, and the way I was wrong matters**
Mid-run I believed the `Read` tool's displayed line numbers ran **one ahead** of the file's byte numbering on all
three full-file entries, and I nearly filed it as an instrument defect directly relevant to `00_Frame.md` §0's
account of a reader failed by the verifier. **I checked instead of filing.** `awk`/`wc` agree at **seventeen
mid-file checkpoints** in `01_Frame_Typology_and_Inheritance.md`, and — decisively — **`00_Frame.md` L20 independently
states that file is "582 lines, in full," which matches `awk 'END{print NR}'` exactly.**
⛔ **The finding is killed. My tail reading of the display was wrong, not the tool.**
> ⭐ **What survives, and it is the useful part:** **every `PROOF` field below was taken from `awk` against the
> bytes, never from the display.** The verifier compares to the bytes; a reader who transcribes the display is
> trusting a rendering. **`M-225` generalizes here: a 100% failure rate is evidence about the checker — and a
> suspected instrument defect is evidence about the reader until it is verified.** *(`00_Frame.md` §0 records
> this project failing that test in one direction; this is the same test failing in the other, caught.)*

### `D-11` ⚠ **A doubt about my own strongest Gate 9 items, stated plainly**
`A-1`, `A-3`, `A-5` and `A-7` are strong **because** the material is silent, and silence has two causes: a real
shadow, or **a spec that was simply never asked the question.** `00_RUNBOOK.md` L2494 says the second cause is
the normal one for inherited material. ⛔ **I have therefore written every one of them as an OPEN QUESTION and
not as a finding.** **Step 2 must not treat any of them as established; they are sites, not results.**

### `D-12` ⚠ **My Pre-Trip context flagged a stale instruction and I want it on the record as honored**

# ROUND 1 — READER B

# DAVIS — STEP 1 · AUDIT WHAT IS INHERITED — **READER B**, independent

**Run mode: WARM.** Frame: Second Interwar, 2564–2812. Written 2026-09-14.
**Governing:** `00_RUNBOOK.md` L2480–2496 · `04` Gate 9 (L216–237) · `01` §5.1.
⛔ No other city was read, ranked, or used as a baseline. `Local_Cultures/`, `Local_Robot_Culture/`,
`City_Megasheets/`, `Test_Runs/`, `City_Vision_Notes/` — **not opened.** No proper name of a person is invented.
No demonym is used anywhere below.

> ### ⚠ TWO PROVENANCE NOTES ON MY OWN METHOD, STATED FIRST
> 1. **I read by `Read` at absolute paths only** (`DR-6`). No graphify, no repo-wide content search. The three
>    non-`Read` commands I ran were **the `G6` re-grounding assigned to Step 1**, and they returned **counts and
>    file metadata only** — `find` by filename, `wc`, `grep -c`, `cmp`. **No `-n`, no `-o`, no matching line
>    opened.** Exact commands are itemized at §A.0 and §D20.
> 2. **I re-derived `R-3`, `R-7`, `R-8` and the `G6` null from the bytes rather than inheriting the verdicts.**
>    Three of the four I confirm and sharpen; one (`R-8`) I could confirm **from inside my own contract**, which
>    `00_Frame` did not claim was possible. Details at §C.

# PART A · THE ASYMMETRY AUDIT — GATE 9, AGAINST INHERITED MATERIAL

**Gate 9's question, as written** (`04` L216–218): *for every finding describing a threshold, gate, conversion,
verdict, admission or status change — the mechanism runs both ways; did the file write both? What happens to
someone it decides against, is that outcome as durable, and is there a route back?*
**And `04` L219: *"A 'no route back' nobody has perceived as a problem is a textbook shadow."***

I went line by line through the 137 admitted `Specs/Davis.md` lines and the 9 admitted census lines. **Twenty-six
sites carry a threshold, gate, conversion, verdict, admission or status change.** Nineteen fire; four pass; three
are near-misses I record rather than score. **Below, every fire names the unwritten side as a QUESTION. I answer
none of them.**

## A.0 · ⛔ FIRST — THE `G6` RE-GROUNDING, WHICH STEP 1 OWNS

`00_Frame` §0.7 hands Step 1 one blocking item: *"RE-GROUND `G6`. The null must be re-tested against the universe
repo's 346-line `World_History_Reference.md`, not the GDD's 7-line stub."*

**What I ran — counts and metadata only:**

```
find /home/kuroskalacs/Documents/Doll-Fi/media -name "World_History_Reference.md" -type f
wc -l / wc -c / wc -w    on the two candidate files
grep -c  <pattern>       on the two candidate files
cmp -s                   between two stub copies
```

**What the counts return:**

| Probe | GDD stub | Universe-repo file |
|---|---|---|
| **Path** | `…/InnerTepeniaGDD/Worldspace/World_History_Reference.md` | `…/Reference/TepenianUniverseTimeline/Reference/World_History_Reference.md` |
| **Size** | **7 lines · 467 bytes · 37 words** | **346 lines · 31,826 bytes** |
| `Davis` | **0** | **1** |
| `Davis` (`-w`, `-i`) | 0 · 0 | 1 · 1 |
| `Davis Sea` / `Davis Station` / `Davis Strait` / `John King Davis` | 0 · 0 · 0 · 0 | **0 · 0 · 0 · 0** |
| `Mirny` / `Vestfold` / `Prydz` | 0 · 0 · 0 | **0 · 0 · 0** |
| `Concordia` / `Amundsen` / `Arcanet` / `Falkland` / `subnet` | **0 · 0 · 0 · 0 · 0** | 18 · 10 · 8 · 13 · 9 |

**Shape of the single match, established without opening it:** the matching line is a **bullet** (`^[-*]`), **11
words, 79 characters**, carries **`Davis,`** — a comma immediately after the name — and contains **no 4-digit
number anywhere on the line** (both `Davis.*[0-9]{4}` and `[0-9]{4}.*Davis` return 0).

### ⛔ WHAT THE EVIDENCE NOW SUPPORTS — stated precisely, and no further

1. **The ground given for the null is FALSE as stated.** `REQ-G6` records the null on *"`World_History_Reference.md`
   = 0 matches."* **Resolved to the 346-line file, the count is 1, not 0.** The stated ground does not establish
   the claim it is offered for.
2. ⭐⭐ **The stub is not "nearly uninformative" — it is DEMONSTRABLY uninformative.** `00_Frame` §0.3 grades it
   *"a zero out of seven lines is nearly uninformative."* **Measured: the 37-word stub returns 0 for `Concordia`,
   `Amundsen`, `Arcanet`, `Falkland` and `subnet`** — five facts that are certainly true of this setting. **An
   instrument that returns zero for everything establishes nothing about anything.** This is `M-225` exactly
   (*verify the instrument before trusting a zero*), and the frame's own grading was **too generous to the ground
   it was criticizing.**
3. **The independent corroboration SURVIVES instrument-verification, and I checked that it does.** The 562-line
   Second Interwar `Timeline.md` returns **0** for `Davis`. **That zero is informative** — because the same file
   returns `Concordia` **14**, `Amundsen` **13**, `Arcanet` **12**, `subnet` **9**, `Falkland` **6**. ⇒ **The
   timeline does operate at a granularity that names cities and subnets, so a Davis zero there is a fact about
   Davis and not an artifact of the instrument's scale.** ⚠ It also returns **0 for `Mirny`, `Vestfold` and
   `Prydz`** — so the whole region is absent, not only the city.
4. ⛔ **I did not adjudicate the null and it is not adjudicated.** The single match is on a **year-less bullet
   with a comma directly after the name** — a shape consistent with an enumeration item and not with a dated
   event entry — **but shape is not content, and I was forbidden to open the line.**

> ### ⇒ THE VERDICT STEP 1 CAN HONESTLY RECORD
> **`G6`'s null is NEITHER re-established NOR disproved. Its stated ground is withdrawn.** What now stands is:
> **one un-opened, year-less bullet mention in a 346-line reference file, against a verified zero in the era's
> own 562-line timeline whose instrument-blindness has been ruled out.**
> ⛔ **Resolving this requires a contract amendment to open exactly one line.** That is a **REQUESTED** output of
> Step 1, not something Step 1 may decide. **Until then `G6` remains uncountable and the pass still has five
> countable generators against a threshold of three — the re-grounding does not block.**

## A.1 · THE FIRES — nineteen, with the unwritten side stated as a question

### ⭐⭐ FIRE 1 — THE ORBITAL OPENING IS AN ADMISSION MECHANISM WRITTEN ONLY FROM THE SIDE THAT PASSED

**Inherited at:** `Specs` L15–16 · census L496, L613.
**What changes status:** 376,718 people leave Davis for the orbital tier; 781,596 do not.

The **arithmetic** is written both ways — the census states who went and who stayed, and every column reconciles
(verified, §C.1). **The MECHANISM is written from one side only.** Nothing in the admissible set states:

- **who was eligible**, and on what criterion;
- **who decided** — the Federation, the subnet, an employer, the individual;
- **whether anyone applied and was refused**, and whether a refusal was recorded anywhere;
- **whether the outcome is durable** — is a refusal permanent, or re-testable;
- ⛔ **whether there is a route back down.**

> #### THE QUESTION THIS LEAVES OPEN, and I do not answer it:
> ***What happened to a resident of Davis who wanted to go up and did not go — was there a decision, who made
> it, was it revisable, and is the trip one-way?***

### ⭐⭐ FIRE 2 — THE `Living` STATUS IS CORRECT AND IT DOES NOT ANSWER THE `Declining` QUESTION

**Inherited at:** `00_Frame` §0.1 `Status: LIVING`, resting on `01` §3's 2026-08-31 migration note.

I read `01` §3 (L295–307) directly. It says a population drop that is **migration to a documented destination
inside the setting's own future** is not `Declining`, and that the correct status stays `Living`. **That ruling is
right and I do not contest it.** But `01` §3's `Declining` row asks: ***"Who leaves, who CANNOT leave…"*** — and
`01` §3's note justifies the reclassification on a **maintenance-strain** argument (*"not straining under that
same pressure"*), **which is a different proposition from "everyone who wanted to go, went."**

> ⛔ **Gate 9 result: the `Declining` STATUS is properly declined; the `Declining` QUESTION is untouched, and
> nothing in the inheritance answers it.**
> #### ***Of the 781,596 who remained at Davis, how many stayed and how many could not go — and does anyone in
> the city distinguish the two?***

⚠ **And the sub-form the arithmetic forces.** Robots retained **57.87%**, humans **77.61%** — a **19.74 pp**
spread on Davis's own figures alone. **The inheritance states the outcome and never the mechanism**, so the
differential may be **election** (they chose differently), **selection** (the mechanism decided differently about
them), or **imposition** (they were sent, or kept). ⛔ **`00_Frame` §0.6's spine question presumes the first**:
*"When leaving became possible, robots left at substantially higher rates than humans. What about this place made
that so?"* — *"robots left"* and *"what about this place"* both encode choice.
**The neutral form costs nothing and is available:**
> ### ***Davis retained 57.87% of its robots and 77.61% of its humans. Was that difference chosen, permitted, or
> imposed — and by whom?***
*(Offered, not substituted. `00_Frame`'s wording is ratified; I flag the presumption for Step 2 — §D11.)*

### ⭐⭐ FIRE 3 — TWO ENTRY POINTS, NEITHER FAILURE STATE WRITTEN

**Inherited at:** L6 (`Access type: ON`) · L7 (Hwy 110) · L148 · **L155 (`Prydz Bay harbor — the primary maritime
entry`)** · `PA-4`.

Davis has exactly two ways in: a road and a harbor. **The inheritance describes both as working and neither as
failing.** At 68°35′S on the Prydz Bay coast, with an April record low of **−41.8 °C** (L73) and a **37-day polar
night** (L75), **sea ice is not mentioned once anywhere in the 137 admitted lines.**

> #### ***How many months is Prydz Bay harbor usable, how often is Hwy 110 closed and for how long, and what
> happens to a city that is the federation's food source during the months it cannot be entered or left?***

⛔ **`REQ-G5v` already records that no throughput figure exists.** This is the harder half of the same hole:
**not volume, but availability.** `PA-4` is explicitly **UNSAFE FOR MAGNITUDE** — and it is silent on duty cycle,
which is neither magnitude nor direction.

### ⭐⭐ FIRE 4 — A BREADBASKET WITH NO STATED GROWING SEASON

**Inherited at:** L143 (function) against L74 / L95–L106 (the monthly table).

From the table, computed by me:

```
Months with Avg High (day) at or above 0 °C :  2   — Jan +3.2, Dec +2.4      (Feb −0.2 misses)
Months with Mean at or above 0 °C           :  2   — Jan +0.9, Dec +0.0
Months with Avg Low (night) at or above 0 °C:  0
Coldest Mean                                : −17.1 (Jul and Aug, tied)
```

**The inheritance states monthly temperatures. It states that Davis is the breadbasket. It never joins them.**
L143 does say *sheltered-agriculture and greenhouse cultivation*, so the conflict is not naive — **the question
is not "how does anything grow," it is what the threshold actually is and what sits on the wrong side of it.**

> #### ***What is the growing season at Davis, what does the city do in the months that are not it, and what
> happens to a grower whose crop fails?***

⛔ **The unfavorable side of an agricultural threshold is crop failure, and the inheritance contains no failure,
no shortfall, no bad year, and no consequence for one.** That is the exact framing `00_RUNBOOK` L2494–2495
predicts: *"early material is typically written to explain how a place works, which is a framing that documents
the favorable path and stops."*

### ⭐⭐ FIRE 5 — THE SAINT VERDICT: A NEGATIVE DECISION WITH NO ROUTE BACK, AND A LOCAL COUNTERWEIGHT

**Inherited at:** **L131** — *"Not a Saint in the Tepenian framework (the Saints are pre-2083 explorers rather
than mariners who served in supporting roles), but a figure whose name carries specific weight in Australian
Antarctic culture."*

⭐ **This is the cleanest Gate 9 site in the inheritance, and unusually the file DID write the negative verdict.**
What it did not write is the mechanism around it:

| Gate 9 question | Answer available from the inheritance |
|---|---|
| Who canonizes, on what evidence? | **Unwritten** |
| Is the roster closed at 2083 by rule or by accident? | **Unwritten** |
| **Is there a route back?** | ⛔ **Structurally NO.** Both stated grounds are unappealable — the era ground is closed by date, the role ground by the kind of life the man led. **Nothing anyone can do changes either.** |
| Is the outcome as durable as the favorable one? | ⛔ **More so.** A Saint is a Saint; a non-Saint can never become one under either stated criterion. |

> #### ***The Tepenian Saint roster admits explorers and excludes enablers, permanently. What does that exclusion
> cost a place whose own founding name is on the excluded side of it?***

⛔ **RESERVED-1 GUARD, ATTACHED.** `00_Frame` §0.5 item 1 rules that building any in-world figure, honorific,
holiday or founding story on the real namesake would **invent a person AND violate GPS in the same sentence.**
⇒ **The admissible form of this finding is a property of the SAINT ROSTER'S ADMISSION RULE, never a Davis custom
honoring the man.** ⚠ **And L131's closing clause — *"carries specific weight in Australian Antarctic culture"* —
is a claim about REAL Australian culture, i.e. site provenance. It is not usable as a fact about Davis.** The
usable half is the **exclusion criterion**, which is in-world.

### ⭐⭐ FIRE 6 — "EXILE" IS A GATE AND ONLY THE ARRIVAL SIDE IS WRITTEN

**Inherited at:** L129 — *"Founding population: Australian exiles."*

**Exile is an expulsion mechanism with a threshold, a decider, a verdict and a durability.** The inheritance
supplies none of them, only the landing.

> #### ***What was the threshold for exile, who applied it, is there a route back to Upper Earth, and is the
> status heritable?***

⭐ **The heritability question is the one that matters most at Davis**, because it collides with the Act boundary:
Act 1 people are *"X who live in Antarctica"*; Act 2 people are **properly Tepenian, origin is ancestry not
identity.** ⇒ ***When did people at Davis stop being exiles, and did anything mark the change?*** ⛔ Open. I do
not invent it, and I do not import the project's criminal-justice pipeline — it is not in my admissible set.

⚠ **Paired sub-fire, L143:** the libraries *"are genuinely welcoming toward books smuggled out of Upper Earth."*
**Smuggling is a gate with an enforcement side and the file writes only the receiving side.** ***Who enforces
against it, what happens to someone caught, and is Davis's welcome a legal position or a tolerated one?***
⛔ The four-city roster on that same line is struck; I read it and I ask no comparative question about it.

### ⭐ FIRE 7 — THE DOCUMENTARY INHERITANCE IS WRITTEN AS A SUCCESS WITH NO FAILURE MODE

**Inherited at:** **L127** — *"No living environmental knowledge survived that chain of handoffs — but preserved
journals, logs, and orientation manuals … gave the exiles a real documentary starting point … Learning from a
written record isn't the same as being taught by a living institution, and the exiles still built their own
practical mastery of the terrain independently, over generations of their own."*

The mechanism is **transmission**, and the favorable path is written end to end: record → reading → mastery,
achieved. **Two things are not written.**

1. ⛔ **What the documentary route got WRONG.** A manual read without a teacher produces specific, durable
   misreadings. **The file asserts the outcome and records not one failure.**
   > ***Over generations of reading without being taught, what did Davis get wrong about its own terrain, and
   > what did it cost?***
2. ⛔ **The "no route back" is epistemic and total.** *"No living environmental knowledge survived"* is a verdict
   delivered **from outside the community** — in-world, **the residents cannot know what they did not inherit.**
   There is no living standard left to audit the documents against.
   > ***A community that cannot audit its documentary inheritance against a living one has no mechanism for
   > discovering an inherited error. Does it have a substitute, and who maintains it?***

⚠ **This qualifies a prior derivation.** `00_Frame` §0.1a item 1 ground 2 calls the condition *"explicitly
superseded"* on the strength of *"built their own practical mastery … independently."* **Mastery being built is
not misreadings being corrected.** ⛔ **I am NOT reopening the `Resettled` ruling** — it closed 3–0 and ground 1
(GPS deleting the modifier's own object) is independently sufficient and untouched by this. **I am flagging that
ground 2's word *"superseded"* overstates what L127 says, because Step 2 inherits the word.** (§D10.)

### ⭐ FIRE 8 — THE NATION TIERS ADMIT AND NEVER SUBTRACT

**Inherited at:** L18–22 (spec) and L394–398 (census) — `Primary / Significant / Notable`, with an explicit
**admission event**: *Russia (added 2026-09-05)*.

- **Favorable side written, with a stated criterion** (*25M Gini-adjusted, distance 0 from Davis's solar UTC+5 via
  Yekaterinburg/Perm*). ⚠ **That criterion is an authoring algorithm, not a world fact.** It is evidence about how
  the roster was built; it is not evidence about Davis.
- ⛔ **Unfavorable side absent entirely.** ***Can a nation be removed or demoted from a city's roster? Has one
  been? And what happens to people already living at Davis from a nation that drops off?***
- ⭐ **The honest reading, and the guard it yields:** L54 makes the tiers a **share-weighting instrument**
  (Primary = 8 shares, Significant = 3, Notable = 1). ⇒ **They have no in-world referent.**
  > ⛔ **Guard handed to Step 2: no resident of Davis may ever be narrated as belonging to a "Notable-tier"
  > community. The tier is a production weight, not a social fact.**
- ⛔ The struck clause on L21 (*"it was the only city in its cluster without it"*) — **read, not used.** ⭐ The
  census row L397 carries the identical roster **without** that clause, so **the census is the cleaner source for
  the same fact.** Use L397.

### ⭐ FIRE 9 — THE PER-NATION MODEL DECIDES "NOBODY LEFT DIFFERENTLY" BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THERE IS NO ROUTE BACK

**Inherited at:** L28 (de-stacking) · L30–52 (the table) · L54 (the model).

I verified L54's claim rather than trusting it. **China, the largest row, holds 19.040% in all four columns** —
Census I robots 113,234/594,715, Census I humans 107,309/563,599, Census II robots 65,530/344,173, Census II
humans 83,285/437,423. **All four = 19.040%.** ⇒ **L54 is exactly true: the national shares are identical across
both censuses and across both population kinds, by construction.**

> ⛔⛔ **THE CONSEQUENCE, WHICH IS SHARPER THAN THE SCOPE LIMIT AS CURRENTLY WRITTEN.** `00.1` §3.3 states the
> limit as *"usable for named portable institutions; never readable as measured per-nation counts."* **Gate 9
> gives the stronger form:** every nation retains **exactly 67.48%** combined **because the model says so**.
> ⇒ ***The per-nation table cannot contain any information about which nation's people left, and no later work
> can recover it from these numbers. There is no route back.***
> ✅ **By contrast the 19.74 pp robot/human spread is REAL** — robots and humans are separately sourced totals,
> not two slices of one share model. ⭐ **So of the two demographic questions the table appears to raise, one is
> a genuine finding and the other is an artifact — and they look identical on the page.**

### ⭐ FIRE 10 — "FOUNDING WAVE" IMPLIES A LATER WAVE THAT IS NEVER DESCRIBED

**Inherited at:** L129 · L8 · L397 (*Australia (founding wave)*) · L178 first clause.

**Australia is the only one of Davis's 21 rostered nations flagged as founding-wave.** The category is an
admission class with an implied closing date, and the inheritance writes only the class that closed.

> #### ***Twenty of Davis's twenty-one national origins arrived after the founding wave. By what route, over
> what period, and did arriving later mean anything at Davis?***

⭐ **This is a large, clean, entirely peer-free `G8` hole** — it survives the one-sentence test (delete every
other city's name and it stands) and the second test (it requires knowing nothing about any other place).

### ⭐ FIRE 11 — THE MINING REASSIGNMENT LEAVES AN UNACCOUNTED PHYSICAL FACT

**Inherited at:** L143's own correction note — the 2026-07-05 mining/fabrication-first framing was **superseded
2026-07-16**, mining and quarrying **reassigned away**.

✅ **As an authoring event this writes both sides — the old framing and the new — and names the reason.**
⛔ **The in-world side is unwritten.** Canon now says Davis does not mine or quarry. It does not say whether it
ever did, what happened to that work, or what happened to the people who did it. **And L60, L120 and L148 all
confirm Davis sits on ~400 km² of exposed rock** — the one substrate quarrying requires.

> #### ***Does Davis cut or crush its own rock for its own construction, and if not, why not?***

⚠ **GUARD.** This physical fact is also the substrate `00_Frame` §0.5's **RESERVED FINDING A-1** sits on. **A-1 is
NOT adopted and I do not adopt it.** My question is about construction and aggregate, not mortuary practice, and
it is a question only.

### ⭐ FIRE 12 — "ICE-FREE" IS A MOVING THRESHOLD DESCRIBED ONLY IN ITS FAVORABLE STATE

**Inherited at:** L60 (~400 km² of exposed rock — carried on L143's authority per `00.1` §4.2) · L83–86.

The accumulation budget is tiny: **72.8 mm/yr falls, ~28 mm lands, ~45 mm lost to sublimation and wind.** The
inheritance never says whether the ice-free boundary is **stable, seasonal, or moving**, nor who watches it.

> #### ***Is the ice-free boundary at Davis fixed, and what happens to ground that ices over?***

For a city whose entire function sits on that exposed rock, the unfavorable side of the ice-free threshold is
**the whole of its territory**, and it is unwritten.

### FIRE 13 — SUBNET MEMBERSHIP IS AN ASSIGNMENT WITH NO STATED MECHANISM

**Inherited at:** L5 · census L496/L613.

L5 records an **authoring** correction (Mawson → Mirny, 2026-07-03), not an in-world reassignment. So the Gate 9
question is about the world, not the file: ***does the Federation have a mechanism for changing a city's subnet
assignment, who is decided against when it fires, and is there a route back?*** ⛔ Unwritten. **And note that
`PA-6` makes the CONTENT of Mirny membership `UNKNOWN`, which is not `NOTHING`** — so the slot stays open.

### FIRE 14 — `Access type: ON` IS A GRADED VALUE AND ONLY ONE VALUE IS VISIBLE

**Inherited at:** L6.

Davis holds the value `ON`. **The taxonomy's other values are not in my admissible set, and I will not guess
them.** Per "choose the most conservative value and name the problem":

> #### ***What does `Access type` grade, what are its other values, who assigns it, and can a city's value
> change?*** ⛔ Named as a problem, not filled in.

### FIRE 15 — "LARGE ENOUGH TO SUPPORT A SMALL ARTS AND MUSIC COMMUNITY" IS A THRESHOLD WITH NO VALUE

**Inherited at:** L143.

**"Large enough" is a population threshold and the phrase grades the result as small.** At a declared Band 5 of
~1,158,314 people, *"large enough to support a **small**"* is an unexplained grading.

> #### ***What is the limiting factor on arts and music at Davis, given that population evidently is not one?***

⚠ **`00b` note:** this is a claim about a **narrow role's** community and must never be read as the general
population's cultural life.

### FIRE 16 — "THE CLEAR MAJORITY OF DAILY ACTIVITY" IS AN INHERITED DISTRIBUTION THIS PASS MAY NOT TOUCH

**Inherited at:** L143. **Colliding with:** `00_Frame` L73 — *"No finding in this pass may be presented as a
distributional result."*

⛔ **The inheritance contains a distributional claim and the pass is forbidden to produce one.** The claim stands
as **inherited fact** (it is ratified canon), but **it cannot be sized, varied, decomposed or elaborated here.**
> #### ***"Clear majority" of what denominator — of workers, of waking hours, of the population? And what is the
> remainder doing?*** ⛔ Unanswerable under `DR-4`'s deferral. **Flagged, not resolved.** (§D8.)

### FIRE 17 — THE EXPORT SIDE IS WRITTEN AND THE IMPORT SIDE IS NOT

**Inherited at:** L148 — *"Vestfold Hills oasis resources (lakes, fjord access, terrain diversity), Prydz Bay
maritime trade, logistics within the Mirny subnet. Full detail TBD."*

**Everything L148 lists is something Davis HAS or SENDS.** ⛔ For a settlement of ~1.16M on 400 km² of rock at
−10 °C, **the import list is the more characterizing half and the inheritance contains none of it.**

> #### ***What does Davis not have and must bring in — and through which of its two entries?***

⭐ **`00_Frame` §0.5 item 6 explicitly says *"What Davis VALUES and TRADES is recordable and should be"* — the
inheritance supplies only half of what that ruling asks for.**
✅ *"Full detail TBD"* is an **honestly declared blank** and I record it as `UNKNOWN-declared`, not as absence.

### FIRE 18 — THE FALKLAND TREATY AS A FOUNDING GATE

**Inherited at:** L127 *"Settled: Post-Falkland Treaty"* · `00_Frame` (21 June 2564).

⭐ **Davis's founding sits at the frame's opening**, which means **the city has no pre-frame life** — its founding
state, its middle and its present are all inside this pass's own frame. ✅ **That is what makes `01` §5.3a
substitute 1 (own earlier states) genuinely three-state and genuinely in-frame at the founding end** (see §D15
for the unverified other end). Gate 9 on the treaty itself: ***the treaty admitted exiles to Antarctica; what did
it decide against, and for whom was the answer no?*** ⛔ Not in my admissible set. Noted as out of scope, not as
absent.

### FIRE 19 — TWO GENERATOR GRADES AND A DOCKET THAT ONLY HAS A FAVORABLE OUTCOME

**Inherited at:** `00.1` §3 (the `✅ / ⚠ / ⛔` generator grading) and §7 (`Blocks? no` on six of seven rows).

- **Generator grading:** the favorable path (six qualify, threshold three, PROCEED) is written. **The grading
  table has no re-grade column** — a generator graded `⛔ ABSENT` has no documented route back. ✅ **In this
  instance a route back was improvised (`00_Frame` §0.7 hands `G6` to Step 1) and it worked** — but it was built
  by challenge, after the fact, not by the instrument.
- **`Blocks?`:** six of seven docket rows read `no`. ***What does a `yes` do — halt the pass, or something
  else?*** ⛔ Undefined. Noted.

`04` L220 requires the gate to report honestly in both directions. **These four passed and the reasons are
reusable.**

| # | Site | Why it passes |
|---|---|---|
| **P1** | **L83 — the katabatic reclassification** | States the **old** label (`KATABATIC MARGIN`), the **new** one (`ICE-FREE OASIS`), the **date**, the **authority**, and **why the old one was wrong.** ⭐ A status change with its own reversal written into it. |
| **P2** | **L154 — the lakes** | *"ice-covered in winter, partially open in summer"* — a seasonal threshold with **both** states written. |
| **P3** | **L75–L78 — polar night and midnight sun** | Both extremes written, **and both `N/A` cells explain themselves** (*"N/A — polar night in effect; 0 hours"*). No favorable-only framing. |
| **P4** | **`00_Frame` §0.1 `Type: Settlement + (NO MODIFIERS ASSIGNED)`** | ⭐ A **NO FORCED FIT** result that writes the reasoning for the two killed candidates rather than reporting a clean slate. The negative outcome is as documented as a positive one would have been. |

## A.3 · THREE NEAR-MISSES — recorded, not scored

- **N1 · `Significance:` (L8)** is a **verdict-shaped field NAME** carrying attribute-tier CONTENT. `00.1` §4.1
  cleared it on content and I independently agree. ⚠ **But a later reader scanning field names rather than
  content will read a verdict there.** A naming hazard, not a defect in the clearance.
- **N2 · L176's question clause** (*"did the exile community develop scientific or economic uses…"*) is an **open
  prompt, not canon.** Only the premise is admitted, and only the premise is used: the lakes include **unusual
  hypersaline lakes and landlocked marine basins.**
- **N3 · `01` §1.1's dual-assignment note** (*"expect this doubling"* for `Settlement + Installation`) is itself a
  threshold instruction. `00_Frame` §0.7 already escalates its head-on collision with the GPS law to the
  developer. **I confirm the collision is real from `01` L113–117 as written and add nothing.**
