# PART B · INHERITANCE CLASSIFICATION, per `01` §5.1 — **ACT-TAGGED AND `PA`-TAGGED**

> ### ⚠⚠ THE TWO THINGS THAT GOVERN THIS TABLE BEFORE ANY ROW IS READ
> **1 · `M-157` — the instrument is ACT-BLIND.** *"A classification made once has been made for an UNSTATED
> DATE."* The `Determined` class **widens** across Act 1 → Act 2, so `Inflected` and `Originated` **narrow.**
> **Every row below therefore carries TWO calls, not one.**
> **2 · `PA-6` — the parent is UNWRITTEN, so *"what the parent determines"* returns `UNKNOWN`, which is NOT
> `NOTHING`.** ⛔ **No row below writes an absence where the honest answer is `UNKNOWN`.**

**Act boundaries used** (from `00_Frame`, not invented here): **Act 1 ≈ 2564 → early 2600s**, roughly the frame's
first 18% — about 45 of its 248 years. **Act 2 ≈ late 2600s / early 2700s → 2812.** The frame spans both and is
**mostly Act 2.**

## B.1 · DETERMINED — fixed by a parent; a local variant must NOT be invented

| Element | Source | **Act 1** | **Act 2** | Tags |
|---|---|:-:|:-:|---|
| **Climate** — −10.0 °C mean · 72.8 mm/yr · 37-day polar night · 55-day midnight sun · out of the katabatic regime · −41.8 / +13.0 extremes | L70–L114 | **Determined** | **Determined** | ⭐ **none** — physical law and latitude, not the subnet. `01` §5.1's own example: *"A district does not have its own climate."* **The single most stable inheritance Davis has, and identical in both acts.** |
| **Geography** — ~400 km² ice-free rock · fresh, saline and hypersaline lakes · landlocked marine basins · fjord-like inlets · Prydz Bay · Ingrid Christensen Coast | L60, L148, L153–155, L176 | **Determined** | **Determined** | none |
| **Subnet membership = Mirny** | L5, census L496/L613 | **Determined** | **Determined** | ⚠ the FACT is determined; the **CONTENT** of that membership is **`UNKNOWN` (`PA-6`)** |
| **Position on Hwy 110; `Access type: ON`** | L6, L7 | ⚠ **UNKNOWN** — the highway's existence date is not in the admissible set | **Determined** | `PA-4` (existence and direction cheap; **magnitude unsafe**; ⭐ **duty cycle not covered by `PA-4` at all** — Fire 3) |
| **Arcanet connectivity** | `PA-1` | ⛔ **NOT safely Determined** — national buildout *"beginning around 2614"*, with an unreconciled earlier local-hub date | **Determined**, ambient | `PA-1` ⭐ **The classification FLIPS across the act boundary. This is the cleanest act finding in the set.** |
| **Currency** | `01` §5.1 names currency Determined | **Determined-in-principle · `RESERVED`** | **Determined-in-principle · `RESERVED`** | ⛔ `DR-3`. **The slot is explicitly open and this pass writes nothing into it.** |
| **Calendar · language family · shared identity** | `01` §5.1's other named Determined items | ⛔ **NOT Determined — plural by origin** (21 national origins, L30–52) | **Determined, content `UNKNOWN`** | `PA-6` ⭐ **See B.5 — this row is the whole point of `M-157`.** |
| **National-context events** — Amundsen Tower ~2688; Arcanet buildout; the orbital opening | `00_Frame` | n/a (Act 2 events) | **Determined** | ⭐ *"true everywhere, therefore never a differentiator"* — **what differentiates is how hard a given place used the exit**, which is Inflected (B.2) |

## B.2 · INFLECTED — the parent supplies the form, Davis supplies its version

> ⭐ `01` L412: ***"This is where most good local culture actually lives."*** And `01` L416: the class is
> **"systematically under-used."** **Every row here is an ACT 2 classification.** See B.5.

| Element | Davis's version, from the inheritance | Act | Tags |
|---|---|:-:|---|
| ⭐⭐ **The orbital opening** | **Measured, and rests on nothing about Mirny:** 67.48% combined retention · 77.61% human · 57.87% robot · **19.74 pp spread** · and a **majority inversion — robot share 51.34% → 44.04%, a −7.31 pp swing** | **2** | **none** ⭐ `01` §5.2 rule 4 satisfied by construction. **The richest Inflected element available.** |
| **Being the breadbasket / a research hub** | **Form is federal** — L143's own history records the role being *reassigned* between cities by ruling. **Version is local:** sheltered agriculture and greenhouse cultivation on 400 km² of oasis rock at −10 °C, alongside limnological research on hypersaline lakes and landlocked marine basins | **2** | ⚠ `PA-3` ⛔ **Act 1 is `UNKNOWN`** — whether a Division of Industry existed in 2564 is not in the admissible set. ⭐ ***"Davis is the breadbasket" is NOT safely an Act 1 statement.*** |
| ⭐⭐ **Libraries welcoming smuggled Upper Earth books** | ⛔ **L143 CLASSIFIES THIS ITSELF, and the classification is the finding:** *"this turns out to be part of a broader Tepenian practice, not unique to Davis."* ⇒ **NOT Originated — Inflected.** **Davis's version is that it is sited in LIBRARIES** — institutional rather than private | **2** | none. ⭐ **This is exactly the error `01` L413 names for the Originated class — *"claiming origination for something inherited"* — and the inheritance pre-empted it in writing.** ⛔ The four-city roster is struck; no comparative question asked. |
| **Bars, eateries, general social establishments** | `01`'s form is universal; the version is local | **2** | ⭐⭐ **The ONLY general-population leisure statement in the entire admissible set.** `00b` makes it precious — everything else in the spec is role-tier (*researchers*, *growers*). |
| **Exile → residency** | Form federal; version local | **1 → 2** | ⭐ **The element does not merely change class — it stops existing as a STATUS and becomes a LINEAGE.** Act 1: exiles. Act 2: ancestry, not identity. ⛔ **The transition is unwritten (Fire 6).** |
| **Robot presence and practice** | Robots are **51.34%** at Census I. `Robot_Physiology…` is universe-wide ⇒ form **Determined**; Davis's version — robots in agriculture, in cold, out of the katabatic regime but at −10 °C — **Inflected** | **1 and 2** | ⛔ **`00_Frame` §0.2's declared exposure applies: that file carries conclusion-tier material about other cities. Any convergence with it is CORROBORATION AT BEST and must be tagged.** |

## B.3 · ORIGINATED — ⛔ **UN-ASSIGNABLE IN-RUN. Held provisionally.**

> ### ⭐⭐ A STRUCTURAL COLLISION, NOT A GAP IN DAVIS
> `01` L413 defines the class as *"Exists here, comes from nowhere above. **Must be differentiated against
> siblings**."* **The ONE LOCATION law forbids reading siblings in-run, and `00_Frame` moves Gate 6 to Step 7.**
> ⇒ ***Step 1 is required to produce a classification whose validating test is unavailable to it.***
> **Conservative handling adopted, per "choose the most conservative value and name the problem":** nothing is
> assigned to `Originated`. **Candidates are held as `PROVISIONALLY-ORIGINATED` and deferred to the terminal
> differentiation pass.** I flag the collision (§D1); I do not resolve it.

| Candidate | Why it is held rather than assigned |
|---|---|
| **The documentary-inheritance founding condition** (L127) | ⚠ It is a **condition**, not a culture element, and `00_Frame` already re-seats it at `G4` and as a `G2`×`G4` conflict. **Classifying it here would double-count it.** |
| **Limnological / hypersaline-lake research** (L154, L176) | **The strongest candidate**, because it rests on a physical fact of the site rather than on a practice — but the class's own test is unavailable. **Held.** |
| ⭐ **Everything else** | ⛔ **A RESULT, not a gap: the inheritance originates almost nothing, and where it comes closest (the smuggled books) it explicitly disclaims origination itself.** |

## B.4 · AGGREGATED — ⛔ runs in the wrong direction here, and that is the finding

- **Davis contributes NOTHING to this class from below.** `01` L414's Aggregated class is about a **parent** whose
  character is the sum or tension of its **children**. **Davis's children are `NONE` by deferral (`DR-4`), not by
  absence** — so the class has no local content. ⛔ **Under NO FORCED FIT this slot is empty and the emptiness is
  a result. It must not read as *"Davis has no internal variation."***
- ⭐ **The one legal entry is directional and upward: Davis is itself an Aggregated component OF Mirny.** Its food
  output and its **mid-position on Hwy 110** are inputs to the subnet's aggregate character whenever Mirny is
  written. ⚠ `PA-3`, `PA-4`.
- ⛔ **And `01` §5.2 rule 5 remains NOT DONE.** *"Register the assumption where the parent's eventual pass will
  see it."* **I confirm independently at Step 1 that no Mirny-subnet registry exists to write into**, so the six
  `PA`s are recorded only in a child's file — **the exact condition `01` L441 says produces an assumption the
  parent will contradict.** It is an outstanding obligation on the pass owner, not something Step 1 discharges.

## B.5 · ⭐⭐ THE ACT ANSWER — the required *"as of WHICH ACT?"*, stated as one finding

> ### **EVERY element I classified as `Inflected` is an ACT 2 classification. In Act 1 the same elements are
> either `UNKNOWN` (no federal framing is established anywhere in the admissible set) or PLURAL BY ORIGIN — 21
> national origins running in parallel rather than one Tepenian version with ancestry visible inside it.**

⇒ **This inheritance classifies cleanly for Act 2 and barely at all for Act 1.** Since the frame is *mostly*
Act 2 that is workable — **but roughly 45 of the frame's 248 opening years are classified on assumption rather
than on inheritance, and nothing downstream would flag it.** ⭐ **That is precisely what `M-157` exists to catch:
a single undated call would have been right for Act 2 and wrong for Act 1, and would have looked identical.**

**Three elements change class across the boundary and Step 2 should carry all three:**
1. **Arcanet connectivity** — `UNKNOWN` → `Determined` (`PA-1`).
2. **Shared identity / calendar / language** — plural by origin → `Determined, content unknown` (`PA-6`).
3. **Exile** — a status a person holds → a lineage a person descends from. ⭐ **The only one of the three where
   the element itself changes kind, not just class.**

## C.2 · THE FOUR RE-CHECKS STEP 1 WAS ASKED TO RUN INDEPENDENTLY

### `R-3` — ⭐ **CONFIRMED, AND WORSE THAN `00_Frame` SHARPENED IT**

L74 reads *"coldest months avg −21°C; warmest month avg 0°C."* `00_Frame` grades this as endpoints read off two
different columns, warm end really **+3.2**. **I confirm the two-column diagnosis and add a second error:**

```
cold end  −21   reproduces as  AUGUST  Avg Low (night)  −20.8
warm end    0   reproduces as  DECEMBER Mean            +0.0
warmest month on EVERY column is JANUARY (+3.2 high / +0.9 mean / −1.2 low)
```

⇒ ⛔ **L74's pair mixes two COLUMNS *and* two MONTHS, and its "warmest month" is not the warmest month.**
**L74 does not describe a range of anything.** ⭐ **`R-3` is under-graded**, and `00_Frame` §0.3 Direction B is
right about why it matters: for a city whose only function line is cultivation, **the count of months at or above
freezing is a primary input** — and that count is **two** (§A.1 Fire 4).

### `R-7` — ✅ **CONFIRMED; re-grade from "unresolvable in-pass" to "diagnosed, correction proposed"**

```
L84  falls  ~73 mm    L85  lands ~28 mm (~45% retention)    L86  lost ~45 mm
28 / 72.8 = 38.46%                 73 − 45 = 28  ✅ mass balance sound
```
⇒ **the millimeter figure for what is LOST (45 mm) has been reused as the retention PERCENT (45%).** The mm
balance being sound **isolates the error to the percent alone.** **Proposed correction: ~38.5%.** ⛔ Proposed,
not applied — finding a defect is not authorization to fix it.

### `R-8` — ⭐⭐ **CONFIRMED FROM INSIDE MY OWN CONTRACT, which `00_Frame` did not claim was possible**

`00_Frame` records `R-8` as *"L111 names a superseded column set"* and leaves it there. **L93 and L111 are both
inside my admitted range, so I can check it directly.**

```
LIVE TABLE HEADERS  (L93):  Rec High | Avg High (day) | Mean | Avg Low (night) | Rec Low
                            | Precip (mm) | Precip Prob | Daylight (h)

L111 SUPPLIES PROVENANCE FOR:  Avg Temp · Temp Range · Avg Precip · Precip Probability · Avg Daylight
```

⇒ ⛔ **Of the five column names L111 documents, exactly ONE (`Precip Probability`) corresponds to a header in the
live table.** `Avg Temp` and `Temp Range` **do not exist as columns at all.**
⭐ **And the sharpest part: L108, three lines earlier, describes the LIVE columns correctly** (*"Avg High / Avg
Low are the mean daily maximum and minimum"*). **The file carries a correct column note and a stale one,
adjacent.** ✅ `R-8` **confirmed and upgraded from an assertion to a reproduced finding.**

### `G6` — see **§A.0.** Ground withdrawn; null neither established nor disproved; one line needs opening.

# PART D · WHAT STEP 1 HANDS FORWARD TO STEP 2

**Step 2 builds the spine and selects generators from what Step 1 establishes.** `00_Frame` already selected
**G2 · G4 · G8**, carrying **G3** as the fragile fourth. **Step 1 does not change that selection.** It hands
forward the following.

| # | Handed to Step 2 | Why it matters there |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | ⛔ **`G6`'s ground is withdrawn; the null is unestablished and undisproved.** Five countable generators remain against a threshold of three | **Does not block.** Needs a one-line contract amendment, filed as `REQUESTED` |
| **2** | ⭐⭐ **The neutral spine restatement** — *chosen, permitted, or imposed?* | **The spine is written at Step 2. A presumption of election baked in there is unrecoverable later** |
| **3** | ⭐⭐ **The `Declining` QUESTION survives the `Living` STATUS** — *who could not leave?* | **The largest unwritten unfavorable side in the entire inheritance** |
| **4** | ⭐ **`G5`×`G2` conflict: two entries, neither failure state written** — no sea-ice season, no road closure, no duty cycle | A generator conflict needing **no second city**; `PA-4` does not cover availability |
| **5** | ⭐⭐ **`G2`×`G3` conflict: a breadbasket with a two-month above-freezing window** (Jan +3.2, Dec +2.4 on Avg High; Jan +0.9, Dec +0.0 on Mean) | ⭐ **The strongest peer-free generator conflict in the set.** L143 already says *sheltered* and *greenhouse*, so the live question is what the enclosure costs everything else |
| **6** | ⭐ **`G4`×`G7`: the Saint roster admits explorers and excludes enablers, permanently, with no appeal** | ⛔ Carry the **RESERVED-1 guard** with it — roster rule only, never a Davis custom honoring the man |
| **7** | ⭐ **20 of 21 rostered national origins arrived by a route nobody wrote** | A large, clean `G8` question |
| **8** | ⛔ **`Originated` is un-assignable in-run** | **Step 2 must not select a generator whose output requires an Originated call** |
| **9** | ⛔ **The per-nation table can NEVER answer who left** — and the real 19.74 pp spread looks identical to the artifact on the page | Protects Step 2 from a whole class of invalid findings |
| **10** | ⛔ **No resident may be narrated as belonging to a "Notable-tier" community** | The tiers are a production weight |
| **11** | ⭐ **The Act 2 skew** — every Inflected call is Act 2; ~45 opening years rest on assumption | `M-157`, discharged at Step 1 rather than deferred |
| **12** | **`R-3` and `R-8` confirmed and sharpened; `R-7` confirmed with a proposed correction (38.5%)** | Three of four re-checks closed |
| **13** | ⭐ **`01` §5.2 rule 5 still NOT DONE** — no Mirny registry exists | An obligation on the pass owner, re-confirmed at Step 1 |
| **14** | ⭐ **Davis has no pre-frame life** — founding, middle and present are all in-frame | **Confirms `01` §5.3a substitute 1 is genuinely three-state at the founding end** (⚠ see D15 for the other end) |
| **15** | ⛔ **The one general-population statement in the whole admissible set is L143's leisure sentence** | `00b` at Band 5 is in full force, and this is the only non-role-tier material there is |

# DEFECTS AND DOUBTS

> **Everything below is reported, not repaired.** ⛔ Finding a defect is not authorization to fix it.

**D1 · ⭐⭐ `Originated` cannot be validly assigned by a ULM city pass — a methodology-level contradiction.**
`01` L413 requires the class be *"differentiated against siblings."* The **ONE LOCATION** law forbids reading
siblings in-run and `00_Frame` moves Gate 6 to Step 7. **Step 1 is therefore instructed to produce a
classification whose validating test it is forbidden to run.** I took the conservative path
(`PROVISIONALLY-ORIGINATED`, deferred) and name the problem rather than widening the class set.

**D2 · ⭐ `M-157`'s act-blindness warning has no procedure attached.** It says *"ask of every call: as of WHICH
ACT?"* and supplies **no instruction for what to do when the two answers differ** — no two-column format, no rule
for which act governs a mixed element. **I adopted a two-act column. That is my construction, not the
methodology's**, and per *"do not supply a criterion the instruction does not state"* I flag it rather than
present it as the method.

**D3 · ⭐⭐ The census rows' leading ordinal is a RANK and was admitted unflagged.** L496 opens `| 13 | Davis |`;
L613 opens `| 14 | Davis |`. **That integer is a position in a sorted list of cities, and it CHANGES between the
two censuses.** ⛔ Under the ONE LOCATION law a rank is precisely what may not be read, used, or inferred from.
**The Step −1 contract issues partial strikes on six `Specs` lines (L21, L60, L62, L117, L120, L143) and NONE on
the two census rows.** This is the contract's own epigraph landing on the contract: *"the range is a bound on
where to look, never a clearance for what is found there."* ✅ **I did not use either number, and I infer nothing
from the change.** ⚠ Also unflagged: L496's trailing *"(revised 2026-07-04)"* is production metadata, the same
class as the struck L9.

**D4 · ⭐ A second DO-NOT-FOLLOW pointer, unflagged.** §4.2 marks **L91** as `DO-NOT-FOLLOW`. **L143 ends
*"Full detail in `Local_Cultures/Mirny_Subnet/Davis.md`"* — a pointer at the read-last file — and carries no such
mark**, while being simultaneously the single most load-bearing admitted line in the pass and the one most
certain to be re-read. ✅ **I did not follow it.**

**D5 · L119's `#### Gameplay Notes` heading is a §6.1d-class source flag with no recorded clearance.** §4.1
documents an explicit content-clearance for **L8's `Significance:`** field and for nothing else. L120's content
survives on content (§6.1a) — it is environmental, not player-facing — **but the audit trail for its parent
heading does not exist.** ⚠ And per `Repo_Scope.md` as quoted in `00_Frame` §0.5 item 3, a *Gameplay Notes*
heading is exactly the class this repo is not supposed to hold.

**D6 · ⭐ L120 carries TWO ONE-LOCATION violations, not one.** `00_Frame` §0.3 names the corpus superlative
(*"the largest oasis environment in Tepenia"*). **It does not name *"the city has more varied terrain than
most"* — a second implicit-normal comparative on the same line, which fails the second test
(*"does this claim require knowing anything about any other place in order to be true?"*) just as cleanly.**
Nothing leaked — both sit inside a PARTIAL strike — **but the enumeration is one short, which is structurally the
same defect `00_Frame` §0.3 itself found in `00.1` §0 (11 claimed, 10 enumerated).**

**D7 · ⭐ Roster order contradicts share order for exactly one pair.** `Specs` L22 and census L398 both end
*"…Lithuania, Latvia, Estonia."* The per-nation table has **Lithuania 1.25%, Estonia 1.24%, Latvia 0.94%** —
Estonia above Latvia. ⇒ **The roster lines are not share-ordered.** Low stakes, but worth recording **before
anyone reads meaning into a tier list's sequence.**

**D8 · ⭐⭐ An inherited canon claim collides with a ruling this pass operates under.** `00_Frame` L73: *"No
finding in this pass may be presented as a distributional result."* **L143 (ratified canon): *"…make up the clear
majority of daily activity."*** That is an inherited distribution. **It is admissible as an inherited fact and
cannot be sized, varied or decomposed here.** Unresolved; not mine to resolve.

**D9 · `00.1` §2.3 reproduces the full text of L4, a line the same document strikes FULL.** Harmless in substance
— the content is post-war and excluded by rule regardless — **but it is a contract leaking a struck line by
quotation, and the pattern generalizes badly to a strike whose content is not independently excluded.**

**D10 · ⚠ `00_Frame` §0.1a item 1 ground 2 overstates L127.** It calls the documentary condition *"explicitly
superseded"* on the strength of *"the exiles still built their own practical mastery… independently."* **Mastery
being built is not misreadings being corrected; supersession of a starting point is not correction of its
errors.** ⛔ **The `Resettled` ruling stands** — ground 1 (GPS deleting the modifier's own object) is
independently sufficient and untouched. **The flag is on the word, because Step 2 inherits the word.**

**D11 · ⚠ `00_Frame` §0.6's spine question presumes election.** *"…robots left at substantially higher rates…
What about this place made that so?"* Both clauses encode choice, when the inheritance establishes only outcome.
**Neutral form offered at §A.1 Fire 2. Not substituted.**

**D12 · ⭐⭐ The `World_History_Reference.md` name collision is worse than recorded.** `00_Frame` §0.3 says
*"there are TWO files with that name."* **`find` returns SIX under `media/`, and `cmp` confirms two of them are
BYTE-IDENTICAL 467-byte stubs** — the Inner Tepenia GDD's and a copy in a different game's
`Original-Basis-Materials/`. **`M-117` (*a name is not an address*) in a stronger form than the frame states.**

**D13 · ⭐⭐ The stub is demonstrably uninformative, not "nearly" so.** Measured: 37 words, and **0 matches for
`Concordia`, `Amundsen`, `Arcanet`, `Falkland` and `subnet`.** **An instrument returning zero for five
certainly-true facts establishes nothing.** `00_Frame`'s grading of its own headline finding was **too
generous to the ground it was criticizing.** `M-225` applies.

**D14 · ⭐ A reading-order trap between `04` and the runbook.** `04` L222–223: *"A pass reporting Gate 9 firing
only on inherited material has probably not run the second pass."* **At Step 1 the pass has written no thresholds
of its own — the second pass is Step 7's**, which `00_RUNBOOK` L2484–2485 says correctly. ⛔ **A Step 1 reader
taking `04` L222 at face value will feel obliged to manufacture a second-pass fire.** Not a substantive
contradiction; a real trap in reading order, and I record my own Step 1 report as **inherited-only by design.**

**D15 · ⚠ `00_Frame`'s *"three-state, all in-frame"* is correct on labels and unverified on dates.** The three
states are founding (Act 1) → pre-orbital Census I → post-orbital Census II. ✅ Founding is verified in-frame
(L127 *"Post-Falkland Treaty"*, 21 June 2564 = the frame's opening). ⛔ **Census I and Census II are not dated
anywhere in my admissible set**, so the other two states are in-frame by assumption. `01` §5.3a's 2026-08-31
addition requires exactly this check before committing to an own-eras axis.

**D16 · ⚠ The unnamed averaging convention, confirmed and narrowed.** L71's −10.0 °C reproduces **unweighted**
from the `Mean` column at **−10.0333 °C**; day-weighted would differ. ⭐ **And I establish something `00_Frame`
does not: the `Mean` column is NOT the midpoint of `Avg High` and `Avg Low`** (checked on Jan, Feb, Mar, Jul —
consistently warmer than the midpoint). ⇒ **Three independent temperature measurements, not two plus a
derivation**, which is what makes `R-3` a column-selection error rather than a rounding one.

**D17 · ⚠ `R-9` is outside my contract and I did not verify it.** Recorded as `INHERITED-UNVERIFIED` rather than
silently confirmed. **A reader who inherits a verdict has not checked it, and saying so is cheaper than the
alternative.**

**D18 · ⚠ The Pre-Trip Inspection's *"in full"* for `Specs/Davis.md` is stale.** My brief states this and I
record it as observed: **the ratified read spec is `1-3,5-8,10-87,90-120,122-134,143,148,153-155,176,178-179`,
137 lines.** I read exactly that and nothing outside it. **Lines 4, 9, 88, 89, 121 were never opened by me**, so
where `00.1` §2.3 quotes L4, that text reached me through a pass document rather than through the spec.

**D19 · ⚠ `DR-6` versus the graphify `PreToolUse` hook.** My brief pre-declares the conflict and resolves it in
`DR-6`'s favor. **I used `Read` at absolute paths throughout and invoked no graph query.** Declared so that
disregarding the hook is visible as compliance rather than as judgment.

**D20 · ⛔ ORCHESTRATOR EXPOSURE — MINE, DECLARED RATHER THAN CONCEALED.** Locating the era timeline, I ran
`find <universe repo> -type f -iname "Timeline.md"`. **The listing returned roughly forty character-directory
names under `Worldspace/Characters/Dolls/`.** ***A command believed to return metadata returned content*** —
the same shape `00.1` §5.2 records three times and `M-223` names. **This is a fourth instance, and the first
recorded in a WARM run.** ✅ **No city-culture material leaked; no character material is used anywhere in this
output, and none is reproduced here.** ⭐ **The mitigation that would have prevented it is to scope the PATH, not
only the name** — `find … -path "*/Timeline Eras/*" -name "Timeline.md"`. **`M-223`'s rule covers `grep`'s flags
and does not cover `find`'s breadth; it should.**

**D21 · ⚠ A Gate 9 near-miss on a FIELD NAME.** L8's field is literally `**Significance:**` — a verdict-shaped
name over attribute-tier content. §4.1's clearance is correct **and a later reader scanning field names rather
than content will read a verdict there.** A naming hazard, not a defect in the clearance.

**D22 · ⚠ My own residual doubt, stated plainly.** ⭐ **The heaviest thing in this inheritance is a single
sentence.** `G3` — what Davis is FOR — rests entirely on **L143**, one line, which a prior pass deleted wholesale
without anyone noticing what it cost. **Every Inflected classification in Part B and three of my five strongest
Gate 9 fires trace back through it.** `00.1` §8 names this as its biggest doubt and `00_Frame` §0.1 (d) repeats
it; **Step 1's audit does not reduce it — it increases the load on that line.** I have no remedy to offer and I
record the exposure rather than dressing it as resolved.

TRUE. The error is mine in both cases.** ⛔ **Nothing above this heading is edited, revised or withdrawn. No
substantive finding is touched; my Step 1 output stands exactly as written.**

```
04_QA_Gates_and_Differentiation.md
```

as procedure, not as a finding:** the `LLAST` blank-line convention is the same field that failed a reader in

explicitly in my brief. That makes this one mine, and it is not evidence about the instrument.**

# ROUND 1 — READER C

# Davis — Step 1 · AUDIT WHAT IS INHERITED — **T8 READER C**, independent

**Frame: Second Interwar (2564–2812)** · run mode WARM · written 2026-09-14
**Governing:** `00_RUNBOOK.md` L2480–2496 · `04` Gate 9 (L216–237) · `01` §5.1 · `00.1` · `00_Frame.md`
**Acts, as declared at `00_Frame.md` §0.1:** Act 1 ≈ 2564–~2609 (the first 18%) · Act 2 ≈ ~late 2600s onward.
**Binding on every line below:** LAW 0 · ONE LOCATION (both tests) · NO FORCED FIT · GPS-purposes-only ·
American English · post-war fields are not inputs · no proper names of people · no demonym.

---

# PART A · THE ASYMMETRY AUDIT (Gate 9) AGAINST INHERITED MATERIAL

**Method.** Walked the 137 admitted `Specs/Davis.md` lines and the 9 admitted census lines in file order,
extracting every claim that **gates, admits, converts, promotes, grades, or changes a status**. For each: the
mechanism, whether the source wrote the unfavorable side, and — where it did not — **the question the gap
leaves open, written as a question and not answered.**

**Result: 16 fires, 1 clean pass, 2 declined classifications.** Gate 9's own warning that it fires on inherited
material "at a very high rate" is confirmed on this corpus: **of 17 mechanisms found, exactly one wrote both
sides.**

---

## A.0 · THE ONE THAT PASSES — recorded first, because *why* it passes is the finding

### `L127` — the documentary inheritance. **BOTH SIDES WRITTEN. PASSES GATE 9.**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Mechanism** | A conversion: a living institutional transmission is replaced by a documentary one |
| **Favorable side** | *"preserved journals, logs, and orientation manuals left behind across the centuries gave the exiles a real documentary starting point for the Vestfold Hills' terrain"* |
| **Unfavorable side — WRITTEN** | *"No living environmental **knowledge** survived that chain of handoffs"* · *"Learning from a written record isn't the same as being taught by a living institution"* |
| **Route back — WRITTEN, with its cost** | *"the exiles still built their own practical mastery of the terrain independently, **over generations of their own**"* |

**The observation that matters:** this is the line `00_Frame.md` §0.1a and §0.6 both independently name as
*"the sharpest single input this location supplies."* **It is also the only two-sided one in the inherited set.**
That is not a coincidence — a claim written from both sides is a claim that has had to survive its own cost, and
that is what makes it load-bearing. **Gate 9 is therefore not only a defect detector here; on this corpus it
also located the strongest inherited material by the same test.** Worth carrying as a technique note.

> **Residual asymmetry, still unwritten, and the one question this line does not answer:**
> ***Generations of building practical mastery from documents, in a place with a −41.8 °C record low, means a
> long period of being wrong about the terrain. Who paid for the errors, is any record of them kept, and does
> the city remember the mistakes or only the mastery?*** A mastery narrative written from the arrival side.

⚠ **This line is nonetheless under a separate, larger challenge — see `D-1` in DEFECTS.** It passes Gate 9;
whether it passes the **GPS site-history rule** was never asked by any prior step.

---

## A.1 · THE THREE HIGHEST-VALUE FIRES

### FIRE 1 — `L129` · **"Founding population: Australian exiles."**

**Exile is a status change imposed by a verdict, and the inherited file records it as a demographic label.**
This is the single most Gate-9-shaped fact in the admitted set: a status, imposed from outside, durable by
default, applying to 100% of the founding population, with **not one word anywhere about its other side.**

- **Favorable side written:** they arrived; they founded a city; the city exists across 248 years.
- **Unfavorable side written:** none. The word *exiles* appears once, as a category, and is never opened.

> **The questions the gap leaves open — four, none answered here:**
> 1. **Is exile reversible?** Is return to Upper Earth conceivable at Davis in-frame, and had anyone done it?
> 2. **Is exile heritable?** The founding population were exiles. Across 248 years, **at what generation does
>    the status lapse — or does it?**
> 3. **Did Davis keep receiving exiles across the frame, or only at founding?** `L129` says *founding
>    population* and the file is silent on whether the flow continued for two and a half centuries.
> 4. **What is the standing of someone at Davis who is neither an exile nor an exile's descendant?**

⭐ **Question 2 resolves upward, not locally, and that is itself the finding.** The Acts law settles *identity*
convergence — by Act 2 origin is ancestry, not identity. **It says nothing about whether a legal or
administrative status converges with identity.** So the heritability of exile is **`Determined`-tier national
canon that the national canon has not written.** ⇒ **Davis cannot answer it, must not invent an answer, and
the correct output is a REQUESTED item at Federation level** (`REQ-EXILE`, filed in A.4).

⛔ Filed without importing any answer. I am aware that material on Tepenian criminal justice exists outside my
admissible set; **I did not open it and did not reason from recollection of it.**

---

### FIRE 2 — `L15`/`L16` + census `L496`/`L613` · **the Census I → Census II change**

`00_Frame.md` §0.1 rules this **migration to a documented destination** (the orbital tier), and therefore
`Status: LIVING`, not `Declining`. I re-checked the ground and **concur with the status call.** The Gate 9
problem is not the verdict; it is that **the verdict's ground is entirely one-sided.**

```
combined   1,158,314 -> 781,596   (-376,718;  67.48% retained)
humans       563,599 -> 437,423   (-126,176;  77.61% retained)
robots       594,715 -> 344,173   (-250,542;  57.87% retained)
spread                                        19.74 pp toward humans
```
*(All six figures independently re-derived from the admitted lines; see A.5. Davis's own figures only.)*

- **Favorable side written:** 376,718 people took an exit; the exit had a documented destination.
- **Unfavorable side written:** none, at any level.

> **The questions the gap leaves open:**
> 1. ⭐⭐ **Was the orbital exit an OPEN door or a GATED one?** If gated — by selection, quota, skill, fitness,
>    cost, or sponsorship — then **the 781,596 who remain are the residue of a decision made against them**,
>    and nothing in the inherited record says so. If open, the robot/human differential is a *choice*
>    differential rather than a *selection* differential. ***Those are two completely different cities, and the
>    inherited material does not distinguish them.***
> 2. **What happens to someone who applied and was refused?** Is the refusal recorded, durable, re-appealable?
> 3. ⭐ **Is there a return trip?** The census records a one-way arithmetic. **A migration with no documented
>    return is a "no route back" nobody has perceived as a problem** — Gate 9's own definition of a textbook
>    shadow, sitting in the inherited data as a subtraction.
> 4. **Could the stayers have gone?** `01` §3's migration note distinguishes migration from decline **on the
>    leavers' evidence**. Both can be true at once: the leavers had a destination *and* the stayers may have
>    been unable to follow. ***"The destination is documented" answers where the leavers went; it does not
>    answer whether the stayers could have followed.***

⭐ **Questions 1 and 4 are the same question from two ends** and should be filed as one.
⛔ **I do not propose changing `Status: LIVING`.** It remains the conservative call. I record only that the
ground given for it is one-sided, per Gate 9.

> ### ⚠ A STRUCTURAL GAP THIS FIRE EXPOSES IN THE PASS'S OWN MACHINERY
> `01` §1.2's **Orbital / extraplanetary** modifier carries exactly the question this fire needs —
> ***"What is the return trip, and who never takes it?"*** — and `00_Frame.md` §0.1 declares **ZERO modifiers**,
> correctly, because **Davis is not orbital.** ⇒ **The modifier is rightly declined AND its question is rightly
> live**, because Davis is the *origin* end of an orbital relation rather than the orbital end. **The question
> set is attached to the wrong end of the relation, so nothing in this pass's machinery will ask it unless
> Step 1 hands it forward. Step 1 hands it forward.** *(Methodology-level; see `D-6`.)*

---

### FIRE 3 — `L131` · **"Not a Saint in the Tepenian framework"**

**An explicit negative verdict, with a stated criterion, decided against, and with no route back — and the
inherited file writes the verdict and stops.** This is the closest thing in the corpus to Gate 9's own worked
case (a membership mechanism written entirely from the favorable side).

- **Written:** what Saints are (*"pre-2083 explorers"*); that Davis's namesake is not one; the disqualifier
  (*"mariners who served in supporting roles"*).
- **Not written:** anything at all about the other side.

**Note the criterion is conjunctive and Davis's namesake satisfies half of it.** He is pre-2083 (1884–1967).
**The disqualifier is role category, not date** — and a dead man's role category cannot change. ⇒ **This is a
permanent, unappealable exclusion. There is no route back and there cannot be one.**

> **The questions the gap leaves open:**
> 1. **What does Sainthood confer on a city named for a Saint** — an observance, a date, a civic object, a
>    name-day, anything at all? **If nothing, say so; if something, Davis does not have it.**
> 2. **What does Davis have in its place**, and does the city experience the absence as a lack or as nothing?
> 3. **Who maintains the Saint roster, is there any appeal, and has anyone ever been added or removed?**
> 4. ⭐⭐ **The resonance the file states and does not follow:** Davis's namesake is written twice as *"a
>    navigator and enabler, not a flag-planter"* (`L64`) and *"the ship's captain who enabled others'
>    greatness"* (`L131`). **The honor framework's criterion excludes exactly that category of person.**
>    ***Is the exclusion of enablers from Sainthood something residents of Davis notice?***

⚠ **Epistemic tag: `L131` is a bare assertion** — it names a framework, a criterion, and a verdict, and sources
none of the three within the admissible set. **This fire rests on Tier D material and must be re-grounded
before anything is built on it.**
⛔ **ONE LOCATION guard, explicit:** answering any of these must NOT be done by reading which cities are named
for Saints. The admissible claim is *"Davis's namesake is excluded from the Saint framework by a stated
criterion"* — which survives deleting every other city's name, and requires knowing nothing about any other
place to be true. **Both tests pass. An answer sourced from another city's Saint status would fail both.**

actually gates.**

non-favored side of a hub/spoke asymmetry **by assumption**. The asymmetry is therefore itself provisional.

### FIRE 7 — `L8` + `L39` + `L129` + `L178` first clause · **founding-wave standing**

sits at the Significant tier; the Primary tier is China at **19.04%**. ⇒ ***Davis's founding story belongs to a

identity; in Act 2, under the Acts law, it concerns an ancestry among twenty-one ancestries. **Different

### FIRE 8 — `L20`–`L22`, `L30`–`L52`, census `L392`–`L398` · **the Primary / Significant / Notable tier system**
A grading mechanism applied to twenty-one populations, inherited whole.
- **Favorable side written:** every listed nation gets a tier and a share.
- **Unfavorable side not written, in three distinct ways:**
> 1. ⭐ **There is no fourth tier. The roster's floor is an invisibility threshold.** Latvia sits at 0.94% =
>    5,298 humans + 5,590 robots at Census I. **That is not a small number of people.** A group at 0.3% would
>    be ~3,500 people and would appear nowhere in this record. ***Is there a national-origin population at
>    Davis below the "Notable" floor, and what is it like to be a community the city's own record does not
>    list?***
> 2. **Does tier confer anything in-world** — precedence, a claim, a seat, a language provision — or is it
>    purely an authoring summary? `L54` says the percentages are demographic; the *words* Primary, Significant
>    and Notable are standing words. **The file never says which it is.**
> 3. ⭐⭐ **Tier mobility has been made impossible by construction.** `L54`: the percentages are *"identical
>    between Census I and II under this proportional model."* **I verified this holds exactly** — every one of
>    the 21 groups is 19.04%/9.36%/… of *all four* totals (A.5). ⇒ **across 248 years and a 32.52% population
>    loss, the inherited record asserts that not one origin group's share moved at all.** There is no
>    promotion mechanism, no demotion mechanism, and no route either way, **because the model has no time
>    dimension.**
⚠ **`00.1` §3.3's scope limit is correct and I re-affirm it** (*usable for named portable institutions; never
readable as measured per-nation counts*). **I extend it:** the limit currently covers the *values*; **it must
also cover the *invariance*, because the invariance is the thing that forecloses tier mobility.**
> **Open:** *Across 248 years, did any national-origin community at Davis grow or shrink relative to the
> others? The inherited figures cannot answer — they are identical by construction. **Is differential
> migration by origin group admissible material, or is the proportional model canon?***

*"Robot figures apply the same national-origin proportions as the human population."* At Census I, robots are

**what is ancestry for a being that was manufactured?** Handed to Step 2 as a live site, not answered.

## A.3 · TWO MECHANISMS I DECLINE TO CLASSIFY — **NO FORCED FIT, running in both directions**

1. ⛔ **`L21`'s admission criterion** (*"25M Gini-adjusted, and distance 0 from Davis's solar UTC+5"*) **is an
   authoring threshold, not an in-world one.** It is the rule the project uses to build composition rosters. It
   has a real Gate 9 shape at the authoring layer — *which nations failed the 25M threshold or the distance
   test, and can a nation be removed?* — but ⛔ **converting it into a Tepenian immigration board would invent
   an institution to fill a slot the world has not opened.** I record it as an authoring-layer fire and
   **refuse the in-world conversion.**
   > ⭐ **There IS a legitimate in-world question underneath it, and it is not the same question:** *the roster
   > lists who came. **Who did not?** Did anyone arrive at Davis and not stay? Did Davis ever refuse anyone? Is
   > there any standing below "resident" here, and is it durable?* **That one I file.**
2. ⛔ **`L119`–`L120` is a `Gameplay Notes` block.** Its physical residue (terrain variety) is world-fact and
   usable; **the register is player-facing**, and `00_Frame.md` §0.5 item 3 cites `Repo_Scope.md`: *this repo
   holds what happened and why, never what a player does about it.* **I used the physical residue only and
   flag the register.** ⚠ `L120`'s superlative and rank are PARTIAL-struck and were not used.

## A.5 · INDEPENDENT ARITHMETIC — re-derived, not inherited

**Per the brief's instruction to re-check rather than inherit verdicts, I reproduced every quantitative claim
the prior steps rely on, from the admitted lines alone.**

| Claim | Prior step's statement | **My independent result** |
|---|---|---|
| Census I robots column | sums exactly | ✅ **594,715 exactly** |
| Census I humans column | sums exactly | ✅ **563,599 exactly** |
| Census II robots column | sums exactly | ✅ **344,173 exactly** |
| Census II humans column | sums exactly | ✅ **437,423 exactly** |
| Share column | 100.00% | ✅ **69.05 + 30.95 = 100.00** |
| Proportional invariance (`L54`) | "identical between Census I and II" | ✅ **Holds exactly** — e.g. 19.04% reproduces on all four totals (113,234 / 107,309 / 65,530 / 83,285) |
| Monthly precipitation | sums to 72.8 mm | ✅ **exactly 72.8** |
| Mean annual temperature | −10.0 °C | ✅ **unweighted mean of the Mean column = −10.0333** |
| `L73` record extremes vs. the table | — | ✅ **+13.0 = Jan Rec High; −41.8 = Apr Rec Low. Consistent** |
| Spec `L15`/`L16` vs. census `L496`/`L613` | — | ✅ **all six figures agree exactly across two independent files** |
| Tier rosters, spec `L20`–`L22` vs. census `L396`–`L398` | — | ✅ **identical membership, 1 + 7 + 13 = 21 nations; 21 rows at `L32`–`L52`** |
| Robot share at Census I | — | ✅ **594,715 / 1,158,314 = 51.34%** |

### ⭐⭐ AND ONE NEW, LOAD-BEARING BOUND ON `G8` THAT NOBODY HAS STATED

Because `L54`'s model flattens the national dimension by construction, **every one of the 21 origin groups lost
exactly the same fractions: 32.52% combined, 22.39% of its humans, 42.13% of its robots.** ⇒

> ⛔ ***The robot-versus-human split is the ONLY differential this dataset is structurally capable of
> expressing. Every other dimension was flattened before the data reached this pass.***

**This does not weaken §0.6's strongest finding** — the four H/R totals are **primary data**, stated directly at
`L15`/`L16` and at census `L496`/`L613`, not modeled. **It qualifies it, in a way that matters:**

1. ✅ The robot/human inversion is real and rests on primary figures.
2. ⛔ **An absence of origin-differential exit is NOT a finding.** It is an artifact, and the artifact is
   invisible because a perfect uniformity looks like a clean result. **`LAW 0-R`'s own warning applies with the
   sign reversed: *a zero invites suspicion; a plausible number does not* — and a perfect uniformity invites
   less suspicion than either.**
3. ⇒ **Step 2 must carry both halves forward**: §0.6's question, *and* the statement that the dataset could not
   have shown any competing differential even if one existed.

## A.6 · THREE NEW OWN-FIGURES RESULTS FROM THE ADMITTED CLIMATE BLOCK

*Derived from `Specs/Davis.md` `L75`, `L76`, `L93`–`L108` alone. No other place is named; each passes the
one-sentence test and the second test.*

1. ⭐⭐ **No month at Davis has a mean daily minimum at or above freezing** — the warmest Avg Low is **−1.2 °C**
   (January). **Two months carry a non-negative Mean** — Jan **+0.9**, Dec **+0.0**. **Two months carry a
   non-negative Avg High** — Jan **+3.2**, Dec **+2.4**. **And all twelve months have reached above freezing on
   record** (lowest record high: Sep **+0.4**).
   ⇒ **For a city whose sole stated function line is cultivation, that triple is a primary input, not a
   footnote.** It sharpens `R-3` from a documentation defect into a generator fact.
2. ⭐ **Precipitation at Davis is frequent and tiny.** Summing the Precip-Prob column against days-in-month
   gives **≈53 precipitation days per year**; against 72.8 mm that is **≈1.4 mm per event** — ~0.8 mm in
   January, ~1.5 mm in April. **Events are small everywhere and slightly larger in winter.** ✅ Internally
   consistent; no defect found. *(This column had not previously been checked.)*
3. ⭐⭐ **For roughly a quarter of the year, the solar day/night distinction is unavailable at Davis** — a
   **37-day polar night** plus a **55-day midnight sun** = **92 days, 25.2% of the year**, with the table's own
   `0*` and `24*` daylight entries at the extremes. And `L108` states that *"during polar night the diurnal
   cycle is not solar-driven and the day/night split narrows toward weather noise."*
   ⇒ **This applies to the entire population, not to a role** — which makes it exactly the kind of material
   `00b`'s General Population Discipline permits, and the kind the occupational trap at Davis does not reach.

# PART B · INHERITANCE CLASSIFICATION (`01` §5.1)

> ⚠ **`M-157` governs every call below: the instrument is ACT-BLIND, and a classification made once has been
> made for an unstated date.** ⭐ **Every call is therefore stated per Act.**
> ⛔ **`PA-6` governs the parent side:** the Mirny subnet is unwritten, so *"what the parent determines"*
> returns **UNKNOWN, which is not NOTHING**, and is never written as if it were.

## B.0 · ⛔ TWO STRUCTURAL FINDINGS ABOUT THE INSTRUMENT ITSELF, BEFORE ANY CALL

### B.0a — **`Aggregated` CANNOT RUN AT DAVIS. It is BLOCKED BY RULING, which is not the same as EMPTY.**

`01` §5.1's `Aggregated` class means *"the parent's own character is partly the sum or the tension of its
children."* Davis's Children field reads **NONE — BY DEFERRAL, NOT BY ABSENCE** (`00_Frame.md` §0.1): `01`
§2.2's 3→4 threshold makes decomposition mandatory at this band, and `DR-4` suspends it.

> ⭐⭐ **The consequence nobody has written down:** `01` §5.1 states *"Every element of a location's culture is
> **exactly one** of these"* — a four-class partition. **With `Aggregated` unavailable, the partition is three
> classes wide at Davis** — **and the missing class is the band-specific one.** `01` §5.1's own error note for
> `Aggregated` reads: *"Ignoring it produces a Band 5–6 polity written as if it were a large Band 3 town."*
> ⇒ ***`DR-4` does not only defer distributions. It removes one of the four inheritance classes, and it removes
> precisely the one whose omission produces the band error.***

**This is a SECOND, unrecorded consequence of `DR-4`**, alongside `00_Frame.md` §0.1's Configuration (a).
⛔ **Not a proposal to lift the deferral** — a statement of its cost, handed forward.

⭐ **And the available mitigation, which links two currently-separate loose ends:** Davis is itself a *child* of
the Mirny subnet, so **Davis's own `Aggregated` contribution is exactly what the subnet's eventual pass will
need** — which is also what `01` §5.2 **rule 5** obliges (*register the assumption where the parent's pass will
see it*), already recorded at §0.6 as **NOT DONE**. ⇒ ***These are one obligation, not two, and should be
discharged together.***

### B.0b — **Davis has a TWO-LEVEL ancestry with DIFFERENT epistemic status at each level, and `01` §5.1 has no way to mark which.**

`00_Frame.md` §0.1's Parent line: *"The Mirny Arcanet subnet — UNWRITTEN. **Above it: the Tepenian Federation.**"*
⇒ A `Determined` call at Davis is ambiguous between:
- **Determined by the subnet** — ⛔ UNKNOWN, by `PA-6`; and
- **Determined by the Federation** — written, but almost entirely outside this contract.

**`01` §5.1 says "the parent," singular, and supplies no sub-notation.**
⭐ **Per the sanctioned fallback — *choose the most conservative value and NAME the problem; do not widen the
closed set yourself* — I did NOT invent sub-classes.** Every ambiguous item below is called **`Determined`**,
because `Determined` is the class that **forbids inventing a local variant**, and inventing one is the more
expensive error. **The ambiguity is named here instead, and filed at `D-3`.**

---

## B.1 · THE CLASSIFICATION LEDGER

| # | Inherited element | Source | **ACT 1** (2564–~2609) | **ACT 2** (~late 2600s →) | Tags |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## B.2 · ⭐⭐ THE HEADLINE CLASSIFICATION RESULT — `G3` IS A TENSION BETWEEN TWO CLASSES

`00.1` §3.2 reads `L143`'s tension as one of **self-understanding**: *residents understand themselves as
researchers while the polity above requires calories.* **The classification instrument says something
stronger and more structural, and it is available from Step 1 alone:**

> ### **The two halves of Davis's single function line belong to DIFFERENT INHERITANCE CLASSES.**
> - **Food is `Determined`** *(conditional on `PA-3`)* — a polity that requires calories from Davis is
>   determining what Davis does. A `Determined` element is one the location **has no say in.**
> - **Research is `Originated`** — nothing above supplies "limnology." **It exists here because the lakes are
>   here, and the lakes are `Determined`-by-nature.** ⭐ ***An Originated vocation growing directly out of a
>   Determined physical fact is the cleanest origination pathway `01` §5.1 describes.***

⇒ ***A `Determined` obligation and an `Originated` vocation, occupying one sentence.*** That is not a mood; it
is a structural difference in **who decides**, and `01` §5.1 says mis-classification here *"fails in both
directions."*

### ⭐ AND IT IS AN ACT 2 TENSION. IN ACT 1 THERE MAY BE NO TENSION AT ALL.
`PA-1` records that the subnet may barely function early in the frame. If the outward food obligation is not
yet `Determined` in Act 1, then in Act 1 Davis is **growing food for itself and studying its own lakes — one
activity, no tension.** ⇒ ***The tension is not a property of Davis. It is a property of Davis after
interconnection.*** **That is `M-157` doing real work on a real item**, and it hands Step 2 a spine candidate
with a **built-in temporal axis** — which is exactly what `01` §5.3a substitute 1 (own earlier states, the
PRIMARY substitute declared at §0.1) wants.

⛔ **Reserved-decision self-check, `00_Frame.md` §0.5 item 5:** my phrasing above must **not** be read as
connecting Davis's ground or agriculture to the Element "Earth." **Breadbasket + exposed rock + "groundedness"
is now a three-way pull toward a reserved meaning, and it is stronger after this finding than before it.**
I state the classification and foreclose nothing.

## B.3 · ⛔ THE `Originated` CLASS IS THIN — AND MAY BE EMPTY

**Exactly two candidates exist in the whole inherited corpus:**
1. **The research/limnology vocation** (`L143`) — solid.
2. **The documentary inheritance** (`L127`) — ⚠ **under a GPS challenge (`D-1`). If it falls, `G4`'s content
   collapses to the bare exile fact at `L129`, which is `Determined`.**

> ⭐⭐ **The honest result, stated plainly:** ***Davis's inherited material contains at most two originated
> cultural elements, and possibly one.***

⛔ **LAW 3 applies and I apply it:** **the response to a thin `Originated` class is NOT to invent one.** `01`
§5.1 is explicit that **`Inflected` is the workhorse, is "where most good local culture actually lives," and is
"systematically under-used"** — and that a pass skipping it *"in favor of wholly-invented material is working
harder for a worse result."*

> ### ⇒ **THE SINGLE MOST ACTIONABLE THING STEP 1 HANDS STEP 2:**
> ## ***Davis's local culture is to be found by INFLECTION, not by invention. Step 2's effort belongs on items 5, 7, 12, 13 and 14 of the ledger — the five Inflected slots, every one of which is currently EMPTY of local content.***

## B.4 · THREE ITEMS I DECLINE TO CLASSIFY — **NO FORCED FIT**

1. ⛔ **The tier system** (Primary / Significant / Notable). It is an **authoring instrument**, epistemically
   Tier C. Classifying it would promote a modeling device into world content — NO FORCED FIT's "invent a
   bespoke one to fill a blank" direction. ✅ **What IS classified: the *presence* of 21 origin communities
   (`Determined`, item 6) and *what each does here* (`Inflected`, item 7).**
2. ⛔ **The census ratio itself.** `01` §5.1 partitions *"every element of a location's **culture**."* **A
   population ratio is an input to culture, not culture.** ⇒ What is classifiable is **whatever Davis does
   about being a majority-robot city that becomes a majority-human one — and that is unwritten. The slot is
   EMPTY, and the emptiness is a RESULT.**
3. ⛔ **`Access type: ON`.** Uninterpretable in contract (FIRE 5). **No class assigned.**

## B.5 · ⭐ THE ACT BOUNDARY CUTS THROUGH THE ROBOT/HUMAN MAJORITY — and nobody has said so

`00_Frame.md` §0.1 Configuration **(b)** flags that *"the band is ruled by a snapshot the frame's later half
does not describe."* ⭐ **The same split applies to the robot/human majority — and that one is culture-bearing
in a way the band is not.**

- **Census I: robot-majority, 51.34%.** **Census II: human-majority.** The inversion sits at the orbital
  opening, which `00_Frame.md` §0.1 places in the **Act 2** era (post-Amundsen-Tower interconnection).
- ⇒ ***Every classification of "what Davis is like as a majority-robot city" is an ACT 1 and early-ACT 2
  classification only.*** **The pass is describing two demographically different cities under one frame.**
- ⭐ **And it lands directly on `L143`'s social establishments** (ledger item 13): **in Act 1 and most of Act 2,
  Davis's bars and eateries are majority-robot rooms.** A `Determined` physiology meeting an `Inflected`
  institution, at a known majority, from Davis's own figures alone. ✅ Both ONE LOCATION tests pass.
- ⛔ `00b` **General Population Discipline is live here and `00_Frame.md` §0.2 already names the trap**
  (researchers and growers are roles). **I re-affirm it: "what people here eat, wear, believe" may not be
  answered from either role.** ⭐ **The 92-day solar-distinction fact (A.6 item 3) and the majority-robot fact
  are two general-population inputs that do NOT route through an occupation** — which is what that discipline
  needs and what this corpus is short of.

## B.6 · `PA-2`'s WIDENED `Determined` CLASS HAS UNKNOWN EXTENT — and needs an operating rule

`PA-2` correctly notes that because Davis is **not** the subnet hub, `01` §5.1's `Determined` class is **wider**
for Davis than it would be for a hub, so *"local variants of subnet-standardized things must not be invented."*

> ⭐ **The half that is not stated:** **the subnet is unwritten, so the SET of "subnet-standardized things" is
> itself UNKNOWN.** ⇒ **The widened `Determined` class has unknown extent, and vigilance alone cannot bound it.**

**The conservative operating rule this implies — offered, not ratified:**
> ***Treat any candidate local variant of an administrative, network, logistics, or standards practice as
> presumptively `Determined` until the Mirny subnet is written.***
This satisfies `01` §5.2 **rule 3** (*prefer the cheap assumption; build on the constraint, not the parental
fact*) and keeps the expensive error — inventing a local variant of something that cannot vary — out of reach.
⛔ Not applied unilaterally.

# `R-3`, `R-7`, `R-8` — RE-CHECKED INDEPENDENTLY

### `R-7` — ✅ **CONFIRMED, and isolated further than §0.3 isolates it**
`L79` 72.8 mm · `L84` falls ~73 · `L85` lands ~28 (**~45% retention**) · `L86` lost ~45.
- Mass balance: **73 − 45 = 28** ✅ sound.
- Retention: **28 ÷ 72.8 = 38.46%**; 28 ÷ 73 = 38.36%. **Neither is 45%.**
- ⇒ §0.3's diagnosis reproduces: **the millimeter figure for what is LOST was reused as the retention PERCENT.**
- ⭐ **My addition, which rules out the competing explanation:** the *loss* fraction is **45 ÷ 72.8 = 61.81%**,
  so *"~45%"* is not the loss percentage either. ⇒ **"Retention/loss confusion" is excluded. The only "45" in
  the system is the millimeter figure, so the error is a single unit-swap and nothing else.**
- ⇒ ✅ **Endorse the re-grade to "diagnosed; correction proposed."** Corrected retention = **38.5%**. ⛔ Not
  applied. ⚠ **Any downstream text repeating "~45% retention" is carrying the unit error.**
- ⭐ **Gate 9 note:** the *physical* conversion writes both sides in millimeters (73 in, 28 stays, 45 lost) and
  **passes Gate 9** — while still being arithmetically wrong in percent. **Gate 9 and arithmetic correctness
  are independent checks and neither substitutes for the other.**

### `R-3` — ✅ **CONFIRMED, and it is WORSE than "two columns"**
`L74`: *"coldest months avg −21°C; warmest month avg 0°C."*
- **Cold endpoint −21** ≈ **−20.8**, the **minimum Avg Low** (August). ✅ Defensible as a rounding.
- **Warm endpoint 0** — the candidate maxima are **+0.9** (Mean, Jan), **+3.2** (Avg High, Jan), **−1.2**
  (Avg Low, Jan). ⇒ ⭐ ***"0 °C" is not any column's maximum.*** The only `+0.0` in the table is **December's
  Mean**, which is not the warmest month on any column.
- ⇒ **The defect is not merely that the endpoints sit on two columns. The warm endpoint is unattributable to
  any column at all**, and the stated range pairs a mean-daily-**minimum** cold end with an unidentifiable warm
  end. ⛔ Not fixed.
- ✅ **§0.3's point that this matters for cultivation is correct, and A.6 item 1 now supplies the numbers.**

### `R-8` — ✅ **CONFIRMED, and I recommend a re-grade from "no" in the Blocks column**
`L111`'s provenance block is keyed to five names — **{Avg Temp, Temp Range, Avg Precip, Precip Probability,
Avg Daylight}** — while the table header at `L93` carries nine columns: **{Rec High, Avg High, Mean, Avg Low,
Rec Low, Precip, Precip Prob, Daylight, Notes}**.
- **Two of `L111`'s five names correspond to no column** (*Avg Temp*, *Temp Range*).
- **Two of the table's columns appear in `L111` not at all** (*Rec High*, *Rec Low*) — they are covered instead
  by `L108`.
- ⭐⭐ **The consequence nobody has stated:** `L108` and `L111` are **two provenance blocks, added the same day,
  covering overlapping but mismatched column sets.** Between them, **`Mean`, `Avg High` and `Avg Low` have
  provenance only by inference** — `L108` defines what Avg High/Avg Low *mean* but sources only the record
  columns; `L111` sources two names the table does not use. ⇒ ***The three most-used temperature columns are
  the ones whose provenance is least cleanly stated, in a pass whose strongest generator is `G2`.***
- ⚠ The intent is recoverable by inference. **"Recoverable by inference" is exactly the epistemic weakness**,
  and it is worth more than a "no" in the Blocks column. ⛔ Re-grade recommended, not applied.

# DEFECTS AND DOUBTS

### `D-1` ⛔⛔ **THE LARGEST FINDING: `L127` MAY VIOLATE THE GPS SITE-HISTORY RULE, AND NO PRIOR STEP ASKED**

The GPS rule as stated in my own brief: *"the real Davis Station is a coordinate, never a cause, identity or
history. **Its operator lineage, prior occupancy and abandonment never enter the city.**"*

**`L127` is about operator lineage and prior occupancy.** It is labeled a *"GPS-purposes-only pass"* and it
does neutralize the **nationality** half — *"which nations held it, and in what order, isn't relevant to the
story."* ⚠ **But it retains three properties of the site's prior occupancy:** a continuous handoff chain, an
institutional discontinuity across that chain, and a surviving documentary corpus produced by it.

- ⛔ **The GPS rule covers a site's lineage, abandonment and vacancy — not only its nationality.** `L127`
  survives the nationality test and **was never tested against the lineage test.**
- ⚠ **`00.1` §4.2 gives `L127` a one-word disposition** — *"✅ KEEP / Tier 1 inputs."* **Admissibility was
  adjudicated for contamination and conclusion-tier risk. The GPS site-history question was not asked** — not
  by three readers across two rounds at Step −1, and not at Step 0.
- ⛔ **The stakes are large and specific:** `00_Frame.md` §0.1a and §0.6 both name this line **the sharpest
  single input this location supplies**; §0.1a re-seats it as a `G2`×`G4` generator conflict; §0.7 hands it to
  Step 2. **If it does not survive, `G4`'s content collapses to `L129` alone, `Originated` may empty entirely
  (B.3), and the `G2`×`G4` conflict at §0.7 has no second term.**
- ⛔ **I do not resolve it.** *Finding a defect is not authorization to fix it*, and this one is a developer-level
  reading of a binding law. ⇒ **Filed for the pass owner and the developer, ahead of Step 2, because Step 2
  builds on it.**

### `D-2` ⛔ **THE CENSUS ORDINALS ARE A COMPARISON VECTOR INSIDE AN ADMITTED LINE, AND ARE FLAGGED NOWHERE**
Admitted census `L496` opens `| 13 | Davis | Mirny | …` and `L613` opens `| 14 | Davis | Mirny | …`.
**The leading integer differs between the two snapshots for the same city, so it cannot be a stable
identifier — it is positional.** ⛔ **"nth of N" is explicitly forbidden by the ONE LOCATION law, and reading
the 13→14 change would be reading Davis's movement relative to other cities.**
⇒ **I record the ordinals as present, refuse to interpret them, and infer nothing from either.**
⚠ **Neither `00.1` §4.2's strike table nor `00_Frame.md` §0.3 flags this.** It is a live contamination vector
sitting in an admitted, un-struck line of the contract's second file.

### `D-3` ⚠ **`01` §5.1 SAYS "THE PARENT," SINGULAR, AND DAVIS HAS TWO LEVELS OF DIFFERENT EPISTEMIC STATUS**
See B.0b. The instrument cannot mark whether a `Determined` call means *determined by the unwritten subnet
(UNKNOWN)* or *determined by the written Federation (knowable, out of contract)*. ⛔ **I did not widen the
closed set.** Every ambiguous item is called `Determined` — the conservative value — and the problem is named
here. ⚠ **This will recur on every city in the 38-city run whose subnet is unwritten**, which is most of them.

### `D-4` ⚠⚠ **FOUR NEW INSTANCES OF THE §0.3 BLIND SPOT, ALL IN ADMITTED, UN-STRUCK LINES**
`00_Frame.md` §0.3 found the *"implicit normal naming no city"* shape twice (`L117`, `L120`), both struck, and
proposed a second test for it. **Applying that second test — *does this claim require knowing anything about
any other place in order to be true?* — to the rest of the admitted set returns four more:**

| Line | Clause | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| **`L72`** | *"moderate by Antarctic standards"* | ⚠⚠ **The strongest of the four, and genuinely ambiguous.** It could mean **real-world Antarctic climatology** (legal — `01` §5.3a sub. 3, and `00.1` §4.2 KEEPS `L60`'s *"one of the largest ice-free coastal oasis areas in Antarctica"* on exactly that ground) **or Tepenia's cities, all of which are in Antarctica** (illegal). ⛔ **The phrase cannot be disambiguated from the line. The ambiguity IS the defect.** ✅ **Mitigation, applied:** the figure (~20 km/h, ~5.6 m/s) and the mechanism (out of the katabatic regime; distance from the ice sheet; exposed rock moderating) carry all the content and both pass the test. **I used the number and the mechanism and did not use the adjective.** ⛔ I did **not** strike the line — not mine to ratify |
| **`L143`** | *"this turns out to be part of a **broader Tepenian practice, not unique to Davis**"* | ⛔ **Fails the second test outright** — a negative uniqueness claim requires knowing about other places. ⚠ **It is NOT on the strike list**; only *"in a way no other city can match"* and the four-city roster are struck. ✅ **I used it for ONE purpose only** — the inheritance classification, where it is decisive and costs nothing comparative (**a practice broader than Davis cannot be `Originated` here**, ledger item 12). Used for nothing else |
| **`L143`** | *"**Large enough** to support a **small** arts and music community"* | ⚠ Two magnitude judgments with no stated reference. **Weaker than the above — plausibly absolute rather than comparative — but the reference is absent either way** |
| **`L83`** | *"small precipitation budget, moderate transport"* | ⚠ Weakest of the four; regime-label language, arguably absolute at 72.8 mm/yr. **Noted for completeness, not pressed** |

⇒ ⭐ **The recommendation stands and is now four instances stronger: `00_Frame.md` §0.3's second test catches
what the one-sentence test cannot, and the strike lists at `00.1` §4.2 were built with only the first test
available.** ⛔ **Not applied.**

### `D-5` ⭐ **`M-157` NAMES ONLY HALF OF THE ACT-BLINDNESS PROBLEM**
`M-157` warns that **the CLASS can change across the Act boundary** (`Determined` widens; `Inflected` and
`Originated` narrow). **A second failure mode appeared here that `M-157` does not name:**
> ***The class can stay fixed while the CONTENT inverts.***
**Worked instance, ledger item 12:** the library welcome is `Inflected` in **both** Acts — but an Act 1
community still reading itself as *"people of origin X who live in Antarctica"* wants Upper Earth books **for
continuity with a home**, while an Act 2 properly-Tepenian community wants them **for some other reason
entirely.** **Same practice, same class, opposite motive.** ⇒ **An act-check that only re-runs the
classification will pass this and miss it.** ⛔ Offered as a methodology observation, not applied.

### `D-6` ⚠ **A MODIFIER CORRECTLY DECLINED CAN STRAND ITS OWN QUESTION SET**
See the box under FIRE 2. `01` §1.2's **Orbital** modifier carries *"What is the return trip, and who never
takes it?"* — **the exact question Davis's census forces** — and Davis correctly carries **no modifier**,
because Davis is the **origin** end of an orbital relation, not the orbital end. ⇒ **The modifier system
attaches its questions to one end of a relation only, so a location at the other end gets no structural home
for them.** **`00_Frame.md` §0.1 is right to declare zero modifiers and the gap is real anyway.**
⚠ **This will recur across the run: every Tepenian city that lost population to orbit is at the unmodified end
of the same relation.** Methodology-level.

### `D-7` ⚠ **`PA-4` HAS A SECOND UNSAFETY THAT IS NOT RECORDED**
`PA-4` is declared *"CHEAP for existence and direction"* and ⛔ *"UNSAFE FOR MAGNITUDE."* **A second unsafety:
no construction date for Hwy 110 exists in the admissible set.** ⇒ ⛔ **It is also UNSAFE FOR ACT 1
EXISTENCE** — any Act 1 finding that assumes road connection is resting on an undated assumption. *(Compare
`PA-1`, which HAS an explicit act-caveat: *"safe as 'for most of the frame,' unsafe as 'from 2564.'"* **`PA-4`
needs the same caveat and does not have it.**)*

### `D-8` ⚠ **A COUNT-ONLY PERMISSION WITH NO STOPPING RULE IS A BINARY SEARCH ON AN UNOPENED LINE**
My brief permits `grep -c` and forbids opening the matching line. ⛔ **These are in tension: a sequence of
increasingly specific `grep -c` probes reconstructs the line's content without ever "opening" it.** I ran
**two** disambiguating probes (`"John King Davis"`, `"Davis Station"`), both returning 0, **and stopped
deliberately** — the two were load-bearing because they separate the GPS-tier readings from the in-world one,
and a third would have begun reconstruction. ⇒ ⭐ **`M-223` says *a command that returns lines returns content*
and endorses `-c`. It needs a corollary: *a SEQUENCE of counts returns content too.* Count-only permissions
should carry an explicit probe budget or a "counts may not be conditioned on guessed content" rule.**
**Declared rather than concealed.**

### `D-9` ⚠ **THE STRIKE LIST DOES NOT ADDRESS NAMING THE RECIPIENT OF THE `L143` REASSIGNMENT**
`L143` names the city that received the mining and quarrying role. **That name is not on any strike list.**
Under the ONE LOCATION law the Davis-side claim (*Davis does not extract*) is legal and the recipient-side
claim is a fact about another city. ⇒ **I wrote only the Davis half and named no recipient anywhere in this
document.** ⚠ **Borderline, and the contract is silent on it.** Flagged for the pass owner.

### `D-10` ⚠ **`00.1` §0 IS INTERNALLY IRRECONCILABLE ON THE ADMITTED/BOUND DISTINCTION — a second instance of a defect §0.3 already caught once**
`00_Frame.md` §0.3 records that §0 *"claims 11 contaminated lines and enumerates 10."* ✅ **Confirmed** — and
I note the adjacent one: **§0's comparison table counts `L4` and `L9` among the eleven contaminated lines**,
while **§4.2 classifies `L9` as `NULL / OPEN` — production metadata**, which is not contamination at all.
**Two different criteria are in use for "contaminated" inside one section.** ⚠ Low stakes; no content harm
(both are outside the numeric read spec). Logged for completeness.

### `D-11` ⚠ **DOUBT ABOUT MY OWN WORK, RECORDED AS `Step 9.5` REQUIRES**
1. **FIRE 3 is my third-largest finding and rests on Tier D material** (`L131`'s Saint framework is unsourced).
   If that framework is not canon, the fire evaporates. **I state the fire and its ground together rather than
   discounting either.**
2. **B.2's two-class reading of `G3` depends on `PA-3`** — the outward food obligation. If `PA-3` fails, the
   food half is not `Determined` and the structural tension becomes an ordinary one. ✅ **Mitigated as §3.3
   mitigates it:** both halves sit in one ratified line as **Davis's own self-description**, which survives
   whatever direction the obligation runs.
3. **I could not test whether anything outside my contract still rests on the withdrawn KATABATIC MARGIN label
   (FIRE 11) or on the pre-revision census figure (FIRE 16).** Both are logged as unreachable, not as clean.
4. **`01` §5.4 (children: Uniform / Patterned / Delegated) could not be run** — Davis has no children by
   ruling, exactly as `00_Frame.md` §0.1 states. **Not attempted, and not counted as covered.**
5. **I did not attempt an `01` §5.3a substitute-1 (own-eras) run.** It is a Step 2+ instrument; Step 1's job is
   to establish what it will operate on. ⭐ **B.2's Act-1/Act-2 split on `G3` is the axis I hand it**, and it
   satisfies §5.3a's own warning that an axis's third state must fall inside the declared frame: **both states
   are in-frame.** ⚠ Whether a genuine **third** state exists is Step 2's to determine, not mine.
