# ROUND 3 — CROSS-CHECK A

# Davis — Step 1 · ROUND 3 · **CROSS-CHECKER A**

**Comprehension cross-check of Readers A, B and C.** Written 2026-09-14. Run mode WARM.
**My Round 1 position = Reader A's output.** Where I say HOLD or MOVE below, I am moving from that document.
⛔ No other city read, named or used as a baseline. No proper name of a person invented. No demonym.
⛔ `DR-6` honored: `Read` at absolute paths and targeted `grep -n` on the three proof files only. The graphify
`PreToolUse` hook fired and was disregarded per the project carve-out — **compliance, not judgment.**

---

# 0 · TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE BRIEF'S OWN PREMISES, BEFORE ANY ITEM

**These are not quibbles. Both change what the divergences are.**

### 0.1 ⛔ **Divergence 3 was flagged by TWO readers, not three. Reader B never raised it.**

I grepped B's full output for `GPS`, `lineage`, `occupancy`, `handoff`, `site history`. B applies the GPS rule
in four places — to **L62** (*"AAD operated the station since 1957 — GPS coordinate only"*), to **L131's**
closing clause (*"a claim about REAL Australian culture… not usable as a fact about Davis"*), to the
`Resettled` ruling's ground 1, and to `00_Frame` §0.7's escalation of the `01` §1.1 collision. **B never
challenges L127 itself.** B's D10 flags that `00_Frame` §0.1a ground 2 *overstates* L127 — a wording flag,
not a GPS challenge — and B's own epistemic tier places L127 under **BARE ASSERTION**, not under
*not-an-in-world-claim*.

> ⭐ **Why this matters rather than being bookkeeping:** **B is the reader who put L127 under the most textual
> pressure** — Fire 7 dissects it clause by clause, and B is the only reader who tiered its central assertion
> as *bare*. **B applied the GPS rule correctly to two other lines in the same document and did not see it
> here.** That is evidence about how the line reads: **the violation, if it is one, is invisible to a reader
> actively running the rule elsewhere in the same pass.** It cuts both ways — either the line is genuinely
> camouflaged, or there is nothing there to see. **Either way the developer should know the flag is 2-of-3 and
> which reader dissented by silence.**

### 0.2 ⛔ **The brief's divergence 1 contains a substantive disagreement it describes as a counting problem.**

C scores **L127 as a Gate 9 PASS** — the only pass in C's corpus. A and B score the same line a **FIRE**
(A-5, B-Fire 7). **This is not granularity and not scope.** It is a 2-1 split on the scoring verdict of the
line `00_Frame` L190 calls *"the sharpest single input this location supplies"* — and it is the line divergence
3 is about. **It is adjudicated at ITEM 1.C below, and it is the reason C's denominator is 17 while B's is 26.**

---

# ITEM 1 · THE GATE 9 ENUMERATION

## 1.A · VERDICT, FLATLY

> ### **The three counts do not conflict about the world. They conflict about three things the gate does not specify: GRANULARITY, CORPUS SCOPE, and — the one nobody noticed — WHICH LAYER Gate 9 SCORES.**
> **Reconciled: eleven mechanisms fire unanimously. One scoring verdict is genuinely disputed (L127). The
> LOAD-BEARING set is FOURTEEN, and it is not a subset of any single reader's list.**

**19 · 19 · 16 is an artifact of three undeclared conventions, and the two readers who agree on the number do
not agree on the items.** A's nineteen and B's nineteen overlap on roughly twelve. That is the finding: **the
count agreed and the content did not**, which is exactly the shape a count cannot detect.

## 1.B · THE THREE AXES, RECONCILED

### Axis 1 — **GRANULARITY.** Is "the way in" one mechanism or three?

| Site | A | B | C |
|---|---|---|---|
| L6 `Access type` · L7 Hwy 110 · L155 harbor | **three** fires (A-11, A-12, A-13) | **one** fire (F3, *"two entry points"*) **plus** a re-split of L6 alone (F14) | **two** (F5 access, F6 road+harbor+maritime) |
| The tier roster + the proportional model | **one** fire (A-2), with the model's ceiling filed separately at §1.0 | **two** fires (F8 roster, F9 model) | **one** fire (F8) with the invariance as its third sub-part |
| The departure + the 19.74 pp split | **two** (A-3, A-4) | **two** (F1, F2) | **one** (F2) |

⇒ **Pure bookkeeping. No reader disagrees that any of these mechanisms is one-sided.** Netting granularity
alone reconciles roughly five of the nine-item gap between B's 26 and C's 17.

### Axis 2 — **CORPUS SCOPE.** What counts as "inherited material"?

Three different corpora were walked under one instruction:

- **A:** the 137 admitted spec lines + 9 census lines, **plus** pass-produced configuration where it carries a
  mechanism (A-18 on `DR-4`/the undated transition; and §A.2 credits `00_Frame` §0.1 and `00.1` §7 as
  two-sided).
- **B:** the same, **plus out-of-set material** (F18, the Falkland Treaty — which B itself marks *"not in my
  admissible set"*) **plus the pass's own instruments** (F19, the generator-grading docket's missing re-grade
  column).
- **C:** the admitted lines only, held tightly, with two mechanisms **explicitly declined** rather than scored
  (L21's authoring criterion; the `Gameplay Notes` register).

⇒ ⭐ **C's discipline is the correct one for Step 1 and I say so as someone who scored outside it.**
`00_RUNBOOK` L2494's *"very high rate"* prediction is about **inherited** material; `04` L220 reserves the
pass's own thresholds for the **second** pass, which is not Step 1's. **B's F19 and my own A-18's framing are
second-pass fires arriving early.** They are not wrong; they are **filed under the wrong pass**, and B's own
D14 diagnoses the reading-order trap that produces them without noticing B had walked into it.

### Axis 3 — ⭐⭐ **THE LAYER. This one is not bookkeeping, and no reader declared it.**

**Four inherited lines carry a dated authoring correction:** L83 (the regime reclassification), L143 (the
2026-07-16 Division of Industry reassignment), L79 (*"corrected 2026-09-04 from an estimated figure"*), census
L496 (*"revised 2026-07-04"*).

**On L83 the readers split cleanly, and the split is invisible in the counts:**

| Reader | L83 | Reasoning |
|---|---|---|
| **B** | **PASS** (P1) | *"States the old label, the new one, the date, the authority, and why the old one was wrong. A status change with its own reversal written into it."* — **scored at the authoring layer** |
| **A** | **FIRE** (A-14) | *"Not written: what the wrong label produced while it stood"* — **scored at the in-world layer** |
| **C** | **FIRE** (F11) | *"nothing records what, if anything, was built on the withdrawn label"* — **in-world layer** |

> ### ⚖ MY RULING — **GATE 9 SCORES THE IN-WORLD LAYER.**
> **The rule, quoted exactly** — `04_QA_Gates_and_Differentiation.md` L216–218, Gate 9:
> > *"For every finding describing a **threshold, gate, conversion, verdict, admission or status change**: the
> > mechanism runs both ways — did the file write both? Ask **what happens to someone the mechanism decides
> > against**, whether that outcome is as durable, and **whether there is any route back.**"*
>
> ⛔ ***"Someone"* is a person, in a world.** An authoring correction decides against **nobody**. A file that
> records its own revision history well has good **provenance hygiene** — a real and separate virtue, and
> `04` L216 does not award Gate 9 credit for it.
> ⇒ **B's P1 is mis-scored. L83 fires.** The same ruling re-scores **C's F16** (census *"revised 2026-07-04"*)
> and the authoring half of **A-14 / C-F11** — all three are in-world fires with a provenance note attached,
> not passes and not authoring-layer fires.
> ⭐ **And it re-scores in the other direction too:** B's **P3** (the L75–78 daylight table's self-explaining
> `N/A` cells) is a **documentation** virtue, not a two-sided in-world mechanism. **Of B's four passes, only
> P2 (L154, the lakes: *"ice-covered in winter, partially open in summer"*) survives as an in-world
> two-sided mechanism.**

## 1.C · THE ONE REAL SCORING DISPUTE — **L127 FIRES. 2-1. C IS WRONG, AND C SUPPLIES THE REFUTATION.**

C's case: the unfavorable side is written (*"No living environmental knowledge survived that chain of
handoffs"*; *"Learning from a written record isn't the same as being taught by a living institution"*), and
the route back is written with its cost (*"built their own practical mastery… over generations of their own"*).

> ### ⚖ **The rule that decides it, quoted exactly** — `04` **L219**:
> > ***"A 'no route back' nobody has perceived as a problem is a textbook shadow."***

**Apply it.** The mechanism L127 describes is a **conversion**: living institutional transmission → documentary
transmission. **The route back would be the restoration of living institutional transmission, and it is
structurally impossible** — the chain of handoffs cannot be re-established, and nothing in the admitted set
suggests anyone tries. **What C scores as "the route back written with its cost" is not a route back. It is a
substitute outcome**, and L127 frames that substitute favorably, as *"a real documentary starting point."*
⇒ **A permanent no-route-back, framed as a starting point, is L219 verbatim.** L127 fires.

⭐ **And C already knows this.** C's own residual box under the PASS asks: *"Who paid for the errors, is any
record of them kept, and does the city remember the mistakes or only the mastery?"* — **which is A-5's
question and B-Fire 7's question, word for word in substance.** ⇒ **All three readers found the identical
residue. Only the verdict label differed.** That is the cleanest possible reconciliation and I did not have to
manufacture it.

⛔ **What C got right and must be carried:** C's observation that *"a claim written from both sides is a claim
that has had to survive its own cost"* is a real technique note — but it does not apply here, because L127 is
two-sided about the **loss** and one-sided about the **cost of living with it.** **Re-scored, C's corpus is
17 fires of 17 — zero passes** (C scored L154's lakes as F15, on access rather than season). **B's P2 is the
only surviving pass in the union of all three lists.**

## 1.D · ⭐⭐ THE LOAD-BEARING SET — **FOURTEEN**, not nineteen and not a merge

**Admission test, stated so it can be checked:** (a) unanimous, or 2-1 with the dissent adjudicated above;
(b) attaches to a selected generator (`G2` · `G4` · `G8`, or `G3` as the fragile fourth); (c) the gap
**constrains what Step 2 may write**, rather than noting a documentation flaw; (d) it survives the one-sentence
test with every other city's name deleted.

| # | The load-bearing fire | Gen | Found by | What it constrains |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | ⭐⭐ **The orbital exit is a MECHANISM-SHAPED HOLE.** Eligibility, refusal, durability and any return trip are all unwritten; the census records a destination, not a gate | `G8` | **A-3 · B-F1 · C-F2** | **Decides which of two cities Davis is** — all three readers reach that phrasing independently. The 781,596 are either stayers or a residue |
| **2** | ⭐⭐ **HARD CEILING: the proportional model forecloses per-nation differential retention, permanently, by construction** | `G8` | **A §1.0+A-4 · B-F9 · C-A.5** | ⛔ `G8` can **never** say which communities left. **An absence of origin-differential exit is an ARTIFACT, not a finding** — and a perfect uniformity invites less suspicion than a zero |
| **3** | ⭐⭐ **Founding wave vs. composition, with nothing written in between** — the founding population is one community at 4.93%; the Primary tier is another at 19.04%; no arrival waves, dates, order or mechanism exists anywhere | `G8`+`G4` | **A-7 · B-F10 · C-F7** | The largest clean, peer-free hole in the set. ⭐ **C adds the binding refinement: ask it ONCE PER ACT** |

| **6** | ⭐ **The Saint exclusion: a negative verdict with its criterion written and its mechanism not** — permanent and unappealable | `G4` | **A-1 · B-F5 · C-F3** | ⚠ **Carry C's Tier-D caveat** (the framework is unsourced in the admitted set) **and B's RESERVED-1 guard** (roster rule only, never a custom honoring the namesake) |
| **7** | ⭐ **Two ways in, neither failure state written** — no sea-ice season, no road closure, no duty cycle | `G5`→`G2`×`G3` | **A-12/13 · B-F3 · C-F6** | ⭐ All three independently note the L154 contrast: **the file demonstrably CAN write a seasonal closed state and did, one line away, for the lakes.** `PA-4` covers direction, **not availability** |
| **8** | ⭐ **A breadbasket with no failure mode and no stated growing season** | `G3`×`G2` | **A-8 · B-F4 · C-A.6** | ⛔ No bad year, no reserve, no rationing, no consequence. B joins it to the table; C supplies the numbers |
| **9** | ⭐ **~400 km² of exposed rock, not worked, with no in-world reason** | `G3` | **A-10 · B-F11 · C-F12** | ⚠ All three attach the same guard (reserved finding `A-1`, adjacent, **not converted**). ⭐ **C adds the ONE LOCATION guard: never write the answer as another city's role** |
| **10** | ⭐ **Libraries as an admission mechanism with only an entrance** | `G3` | **A-9 · B-F6sub · C-F13** | ⭐ **C's ledger item 12 is decisive and costs nothing comparative: the same line classifies the practice as broader than Davis, so it cannot be `Originated` here** |

B states it as a rule, A as an open question, C by declining to classify them at all |

It is the Acts law pointed at robots, and it is the one composition question about the majority |

### ⛔ THREE GUARDS that ride with the set but are not fires

- ⛔ **`Access type: ON` is uninterpretable in contract.** Unanimous (A-11 · B-F14 · C-F5), and unanimously
  handled by the conservative fallback. **A negative constraint: do not read a meaning off it.**
- ⛔ **No two nations' figures may be compared, at any precision, in either direction** — the within-tier
  ordering is an artifact of L28's de-stacking. **C-F10 states it as a determinable rule; A and B record the
  model's effects without drawing it. I adopt C's rule.** ⭐ Corroborated twice independently: **B-D7 and
  C-F10 both found the same roster/share order mismatch in the Notable tier.**
- ⛔ **L148 is a declared blank AND its promise is export-shaped.** A-19 (placeholder, re-flag on every reuse),
  C-Tier E (premise only) and B-F17 (the import side is the more characterizing half and is absent) **are all
  true of one line and none excludes the others.** Carry all three.

### ⏸ DROPPED from the load-bearing set, with the reason

**B-F16** (the *"clear majority of daily activity"* collision with `00_Frame` L73) — real and unresolved, but a
**constraint on the pass**, not a Gate 9 fire; **B-F18** (Falkland) — out of the admissible set by B's own
statement; **B-F19** (the docket's missing re-grade column) — a second-pass fire arriving at Step 1;
**A-16 / B-N1 / C** (the `Significance:` field's verdict-shaped name) — **unanimous, and unanimously agreed
that §4.1's clearance on content is correct.** A naming hazard for later readers, nothing more.

## 1.E · HOLD / MOVE

**MOVE.** I filed nineteen fires confined to the admitted lines and treated the number as the deliverable.
**Four items I did not file belong in the set** — C-F9, C-F15 (lake access, reserved-adjacent), B-F12, and
C-F10 as a guard — **and two I did file do not** (A-18's framing is second-pass; my §A.2 two-sided credits to
`00_Frame` and `00.1` are pass-produced, not inherited). **What changed my mind on C-F9 specifically:** I
filed the proportional model's consequences under epistemics and at A-4 and **never once asked what a robot's
national origin consists of** — a question about the majority population of half the frame, which my own walk
of L54 should have reached and did not.

## 1.F · WHAT THE LOSING READING WOULD COST

**Taking any single reader's count as the result costs a different thing in each direction.** Take B's 26 and
Step 2 inherits two fires it cannot act on (out-of-set, and second-pass) plus a mis-scored pass at L83 that
tells it the regime reclassification is clean. Take C's 17 and Step 2 inherits **L127 marked CLEAR on Gate 9 —
the single most consequential mis-signal available**, because L127 is the line ITEM 3 is about and the line
`00_Frame` L365 hands Step 2 as its generator conflict. Take my 19 and Step 2 never asks what a robot's origin
is made of. ⇒ **The merge is not a courtesy. Each list is missing something the others hold.**

# ITEM 2 · `01` §5.1'S CLASSES

## 2.A · VERDICT, FLATLY

> ### **THREE SEPARATE DEFECTS. Not three descriptions of one.**
> **They share exactly one consequence — `01` §5.1's four-class partition cannot be run as written at Davis —
> and they have three different causes, three different owners and three different fixes. Collapsing them
> would hide two of the three fixes.**

| | Reader | Cause | Owner | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **(a)** | **A · D-5** — no class for an origination that ages into inheritance | **Temporal.** The classes are defined relative to a **parent**, and a location's own past is not a parent | ULM methodology | A new **call form** (not a fifth class) |
| **(b)** | **B · D1** — `Originated` un-assignable in-run | **Procedural.** A later binding law removed the class's validating test | **Developer / runbook** | ⭐ **An ENUMERATION correction — see 2.C** |
| **(c)** | **C · B.0a** — `Aggregated` unavailable | **Configurational.** `DR-4` deferred the children | **Nothing to fix.** `DR-4` is a standing ruling | **Record the cost, do not lift the deferral** |

## 2.B · WHICH CLASSES A STEP 1 AT THIS LOCATION CAN ACTUALLY ASSIGN

**The rule, quoted exactly** — `01_Frame_Typology_and_Inheritance.md` §5.1, **L405**:
> *"Every element of a location's culture is **exactly one** of these. Mis-classifying is the most common
> structural error available at this step, and it fails in both directions."*

| Class | Assignable at Davis? | On what condition |
|---|---|---|
| **Determined** | ✅ **YES** | ⚠ **With the ancestor stamp.** Davis has two live ancestors at different epistemic status — the unwritten subnet (`PA-6`: UNKNOWN, not NOTHING) and the Federation above it. **All three readers reached this independently** (A-D4, B's §B.0 preamble, C-D3/B.0b) **and all three chose `Determined` as the conservative value for ambiguous items, for the same reason: `Determined` is the class that FORBIDS inventing a local variant, and inventing one is the more expensive error.** ⭐ 3-0, unprompted, on a call the instrument does not ask for |
| **Inflected** | ✅ **YES — and this is where the work is** | `01` **L412**: *"The parent supplies the form; the location supplies its version… **This is where most good local culture actually lives**, and a pass that skips it in favor of wholly-invented material is working harder for a worse result."* `01` **L416**: *"**The Inflected class is the workhorse and is systematically under-used.**"* |
| **Originated** | ⚠ **PROVISIONALLY — assign, and defer the differentiation** | **B's "un-assignable" is too strong; B's HANDLING is exactly right.** See 2.C |
| **Aggregated** | ⛔ **NO, downward. ✅ YES, upward, as a relation** | Children are `NONE — BY DEFERRAL` under `DR-4`. **Upward — Davis as a component of the subnet — is legal and all three readers found it** (A-B.4, B-B.4, C-B.0a) |

## 2.C · ⭐⭐ B's DEFECT IS REAL AND B'S DIAGNOSIS IS ONE STEP SHORT — THE FIX IS AN ENUMERATION

**The rule B is reading, quoted exactly** — `01` §5.1 **L413**:
> *"**Originated** | Exists here, comes from nowhere above. **Must be differentiated against siblings** |
> Claiming origination for something inherited — which is how two siblings end up 'independently' inventing
> the same custom."*

**The law B is reading it against, quoted exactly** — project `CLAUDE.md`, ⛔⛔⛔ ONE LOCATION:
> *"**RESOLVED 2026-09-06 — DIFFERENTIATION IS A TERMINAL CHECK:** … **All five comparison instruments
> (`03` §5b · Gate 6's against-siblings half · Step 6 + the table · `02` §4's three-step rule · `02` §G8's
> z-score) move OUT of the per-location pass and into a single terminal check on the finished corpus**."*

> ### ⭐ **COUNT THE ENUMERATION. `01` §5.1 L413'S SIBLING REQUIREMENT IS NOT ONE OF THE FIVE.**
> **`Gate 6`'s against-siblings half is in `04`. L413's requirement is in `01`, in the class definition
> itself, and it was not enumerated.** ⇒ **This is a SIXTH comparison instrument that the terminal-check
> resolution did not move, and it is the only one sitting inside a definition rather than inside a procedure —
> which is exactly why the sweep missed it.**
>
> ⇒ **`Originated` is not un-assignable. It is assignable with its validating test deferred to the terminal
> check, on the same footing as the other five.** **B's operational handling — `PROVISIONALLY-ORIGINATED`,
> deferred — is already the correct procedure; B simply did not realize it was the procedure rather than a
> workaround.** ⛔ **Fixing the enumeration dissolves B's defect entirely, and costs one line in the runbook.**
>
> ⚠ **And the shape is familiar:** this is the same defect C found at `00.1` §0 (*11 claimed, 10 enumerated*)
> and B found at `00_Frame` §0.3 (*L120 carries two comparatives, one enumerated*). **Three unenumerated-item
> findings in one pass, at three different scales.**

## 2.D · ⭐ C's DEFECT IS THE LARGEST OF THE THREE AND IS CORRECT AS WRITTEN

**The rule, quoted exactly** — `01` §5.1 **L414**:
> *"**Aggregated** | The parent's own character is partly the sum or the tension of its children | **Ignoring
> it produces a Band 5–6 polity written as if it were a large Band 3 town.**"*

**C's finding, which I confirm and did not reach in Round 1:** ⇒ ***`DR-4` does not only defer distributions.
It removes one of the four inheritance classes, and it removes precisely the one whose omission produces the
band error.*** **Davis is declared Band 5 at Census I.**

**And the cost is measurable, not rhetorical** — `01` **§5.4, L539 and L544**:
> *"**Uniform** — genuinely the same throughout. **(Rare above Band 4. Suspect any claim of it.)**"*
> *"**A Band 4+ pass that answers everything as Uniform has not been written at its own scale.**"*

⇒ **With children deferred and `Aggregated` unavailable, every Davis-internal claim Step 2 writes is
implicitly Uniform** — a value `01` L539 instructs the reader to *suspect* at this band. ⛔ **This is not a
proposal to lift `DR-4`. It is a requirement that Step 2 DECLARE the implicit Uniform rather than let it pass
as a result**, which `00_Frame` §0.1 Configuration (a) already half-does and C completes.

⭐ **C's merge proposal is right and I endorse it:** Davis's **upward** `Aggregated` contribution is the same
obligation as `01` §5.2 **rule 5** (*"Register the assumption where the parent's eventual pass will see it"*),
which **all three readers independently confirmed is NOT DONE** — no subnet registry exists to write into.
**One obligation, not two. Discharge them together.**

## 2.E · MY OWN DEFECT (A · D-5) — HELD, RE-FRAMED, AND DEMOTED IN PRIORITY

**I HOLD that the instrument cannot express it, and I sharpen the framing against my own Round 1 wording.**
I filed it as *"a second edge of `M-157`."* **That is wrong.** `M-157` (`01` L398–402) says:
> *"**The `Determined` class WIDENS across the Act 1 → Act 2 boundary** … so `Inflected` and `Originated`
> narrow. ⛔ **A classification made once has been made for an UNSTATED DATE.** ⭐ Ask of every call: **as of
> WHICH ACT?**"*

⛔ **`M-157` is about the PARENT widening. An act stamp records WHEN a call was made — it does not supply a
CLASS to move the element INTO.** The four classes are defined relative to a **parent**, and **a location's own
past is not a parent.** ⇒ **The instrument has no notion of SELF-INHERITANCE**, and no amount of act-stamping
produces one. **That is a cleaner statement of my defect than the one I filed**, and it is a separate finding
from `M-157` rather than an edge of it.

⚠ **And I demote it.** Its **only live instance at Davis is L127's terrain mastery** — which ITEM 3 puts under
challenge and ITEM 4 leaves as the sole `Originated` candidate if it survives. ⇒ **Rule on ITEM 3 first. My
D-5 is downstream of it and may have no instance at all.**

## 2.F · HOLD / MOVE

**HOLD on my diagnosis, MOVE on its scope and its priority.** I also **MOVE against B's framing** (the fix is
an enumeration, not a class the pass cannot use) **and toward C's finding**, which is better evidenced than
mine — it cites the class's own error note and lands on Davis's own declared band, where mine rests on a
single line that may not survive ITEM 3.

## 2.G · WHAT THE LOSING READING WOULD COST

- **If B's reading wins** (`Originated` un-assignable): Step 2 is told, per B's own hand-forward item 8, that
  it *"must not select a generator whose output requires an Originated call."* ⛔ **That is a real and
  unnecessary amputation** — it would forbid the research vocation and the terrain-mastery condition from ever
  grounding a finding, on the strength of an enumeration oversight. **Cost: a generator's worth of material,
  surrendered to a clerical gap.**
- **If C's reading is collapsed into the others** and treated as "another class problem": the band error `01`
  L414 names goes unguarded, and Step 2 writes a Band 5 city at Band 3 scale **without any instrument flagging
  it** — because the flag was the missing class.
- **If mine is collapsed:** an element that Davis invented in Act 1 and merely *has* by Act 2 gets written as
  though residents of the later frame are still its authors. **Cost: roughly four-fifths of the frame
  mischaracterized on the one item the pass calls its sharpest.**

# ITEM 3 · L127 AND THE GPS LINEAGE CLAUSE

## 3.A · VERDICT, FLATLY

> ### **NECESSARILY A DEVELOPER RULING. NOT RESOLVABLE BY A PASS — for two independent reasons, either alone sufficient.**
> **AND THE QUESTION IS NOT "DOES L127 VIOLATE GPS?" IT IS A SEVERABILITY QUESTION — which the pass's own
> ratified Step 0 ALREADY ANSWERS TWICE, OPPOSITELY, EIGHT LINES APART.**

### The two reasons a pass cannot settle it

1. ⛔ **L127 carries a dated compliance assertion by a prior authority.** The line reads *"(Refined
   2026-07-25 — GPS-purposes-only pass: …)"*. **A reader cannot overturn a dated assertion of compliance on a
   ratified root; only the authority that made it can.** *(This is A-D7's countervailing, and I hold it — it
   is the stronger half of my Round 1 position, not the weaker.)*
2. ⛔ **Severability is a question about what the fiction is meant to be, not about what the text says.** The
   text asserts a cause and asserts an effect. **No reading of the line determines whether the effect survives
   the deletion of an inadmissible cause**, because the law does not address that case. **A pass that answered
   it would be legislating.**

## 3.B · ⭐⭐ THE DECOMPOSITION NOBODY PERFORMED — **L127 IS FOUR PROVENANCE ELEMENTS, NOT ONE**

**Both flagging readers put a single yes/no on the whole line.** A: *"site lineage with the names removed."*
C: three properties, bundled. ⛔ **They do not fall together, they have different standing, and they can be
ruled on separately.** From L127 as written:

| | Element | Standing under the law as the brief states its scope |
|---|---|---|
| **(i)** | *"on Davis Station infrastructure"* | **Site FABRIC.** ⚠ Genuinely ambiguous, and **isolated by no reader.** Physical structures at a coordinate may be substrate like the rock and the lakes — or *"whose station it occupies"* verbatim |
| **(ii)** | *"The Australian Antarctic Division operated here from 1957"* | ⭐ **Cleanest case, and it is already settled elsewhere.** B tiered the identical claim at **L62** as *"NOT AN IN-WORLD CLAIM AT ALL — GPS coordinate only."* **Consistency requires the same treatment inside L127. Costs the pass nothing; nothing is built on it** |
| **(iii)** | *"continuously maintained by a rotating succession of national operators — which nations held it, and in what order, isn't relevant"* | ⛔ **LINEAGE, named as lineage.** The brief's scope statement is *"builders, flag, **lineage**, abandonment and fate — **provenance, not ethnicity**."* ⇒ **The 2026-07-25 refinement neutralized the ETHNICITY half of a law whose stated scope is PROVENANCE.** **This is the strongest half of the challenge and it is A's and C's point, correctly made** |
| **(iv)** | *"preserved journals, logs, and orientation manuals left behind across the centuries gave the exiles a real documentary starting point"* | ⭐⭐ **THE ONLY ELEMENT `G4` ACTUALLY RESTS ON — and the only one that is arguably severable.** What it asserts about Davis is a property of the founding population's **epistemic situation**: they learned their ground from paper, not from people. **That claim can be true without any real site's lineage being an input** |

## 3.C · ⭐⭐ THE SHARPEST QUESTION — AND IT IS SHARPEST BECAUSE THE PASS ALREADY ANSWERED IT TWICE

⛔ **I did not have this in Round 1 and neither did C. It is in `00_Frame.md` §0.1a and it is decisive.**

**`00_Frame.md` L178–181 — the `Resettled` modifier ruling, ground 1, quoted exactly:**
> *"The modifier's obligatory question is **'what did the second population inherit, misread, or fail to notice
> about the first?'** — and **the only route to a 'first population' here runs through the real station's prior
> occupancy, which GPS-purposes-only EXCLUDES.** Narrowing the modifier to survive GPS **deleted the question's
> own object**, so what remained was no longer that modifier."*

**`00_Frame.md` L189–191, eight lines later, quoted exactly:**
> *"⛔ **THE FINDING SURVIVES THE MODIFIER'S DEATH** … ***a documentary inheritance without a living
> institution is the sharpest single input this location supplies.*** **It is carried at `G4`, and re-seated
> additionally as a `G2`×`G4` generator conflict.**"*

> ### ⇒ **THE PASS HAS ALREADY RULED ON SEVERABILITY. TWICE. IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS. IN ONE SECTION.**
> - **L178–181 rules it INSEVERABLE** — that is the entire force of *"deleted the question's own object."*
> - **L189–191 rules it SEVERABLE** — the finding survives, and is promoted to the location's sharpest input.
>
> **And the documentary corpus is produced BY the excluded prior occupancy**: L127 says the manuals were
> *"left behind across the centuries"* — left behind by the rotating succession that L178–181 excludes.

⚠ **The reconciliation that would make both true, stated fairly, because it is defensible and it IS the
ruling:** ground 1 excludes the first population **as a subject** (who they were, what they believed, what the
second population misread about them); L127 keeps only the **artifact** they left. **If a document at a
coordinate is site fabric — like the infrastructure the city was founded on — both statements stand.**

### ⭐ THE QUESTION FOR THE DEVELOPER, IN ITS SHARPEST FORM

> #### **`00_Frame` §0.1a ground 1 rules that the real station's prior occupancy is excluded by GPS-purposes-only, and kills a type modifier on that ground. `Specs/Davis.md` L127 states that the same prior occupancy left behind the journals, logs and manuals the founding population learned its ground from — and §0.1a carries that inheritance, eight lines later, as this location's sharpest single input, seated at `G4`.**
> #### **Which is intended: is a documentary corpus left by an excluded prior occupancy site FABRIC (admissible, like the infrastructure the city was founded on) or site HISTORY (excluded, like the occupancy that produced it)?**
> #### **And if fabric — may the pass assert the CONDITION (they learned their ground from paper, not from people) while never asserting the CHAIN that produced the paper?**

⛔ **I do not answer it. But note what the second half buys:** ⭐ **if the ruling is "fabric, and the chain is
never asserted," the cost to this pass is ONE SENTENCE — L127's causal clause stops being quotable — and
nothing else in the table below falls.**

## 3.D · ⛔ THE BLAST RADIUS — **BOTH FLAGGING READERS GOT IT WRONG, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS**

**The readers disagree, and the brief is right that they do. Here is what actually falls if the condition is
ruled inadmissible.**

| What | A said | C said | ⚖ **What is actually true** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`G4`'s content** | *"the pass loses one of its three"* | *"collapses to the bare exile fact at `L129`"* | ⚠ **BOTH OVERSTATE.** `G4` **retains L129** (the exile founding) **AND L131's Saint exclusion criterion, which is in-world Tepenian material and survives** — C's own FIRE 3 establishes exactly that, and C's own ledger item 15 classes the Saint framework `Determined` and act-stable. ⇒ **`G4` is wounded, not lost, and not bare** |
| **The `Originated` class** | *"the single clearest Originated item"* lost | *"may empty entirely"* | ⛔ **C says "may." Under ITEM 4's ruling it EMPTIES, flatly** — see below |
| **The `G2`×`G4` conflict** (`00_Frame` L365, handed to Step 2) | loses a term | *"has no second term"* | ✅ **CORRECT, both. This is the real loss** — the conflict Step 2 was handed is *"a documentary inheritance without a living institution, against ~400 km² of exposed rock and a −10.0 °C mean."* **Delete the first term and Step 2's assigned conflict does not exist** |
| **The Gate 9 fire** (load-bearing item 4) | — | — | ⛔ **Falls with it.** A fire about an inadmissible conversion is not a fire |

> ### ⭐⭐ AND THE REASON A AND C DIFFER IS **NOT** THE GPS QUESTION — IT IS ITEM 4
> **A's ledger demotes the research vocation to `Inflected`. C's keeps it at `Originated`. B holds it as
> `PROVISIONALLY-ORIGINATED`, "the strongest candidate."** ⇒ **On C's ledger, losing L127 leaves `Originated`
> with one item; on A's, it leaves `Originated` EMPTY.** **The blast radius disagreement is a classification
> disagreement wearing a GPS costume, and neither reader noticed.** *(This is why ITEM 4 exists.)*

## 3.E · ⚠ ONE MORE CROSS-CONNECTION, BECAUSE IT CHANGES THE SIZE OF THE LOSS

**B's D10 — filed as a wording flag — is load-bearing on the blast radius and B did not know it.**
`00_Frame` §0.1a ground 2 calls the documentary condition *"explicitly superseded"* on the strength of
*"the exiles still built their own practical mastery… independently."* **B's objection is right:** *"Mastery
being built is not misreadings being corrected; supersession of a starting point is not correction of its
errors."*
⇒ ⭐ **If the condition IS a bounded, superseded Act 1 condition, losing it costs less. If B is right that it
runs longer than §0.1a claims, losing it costs more.** **The developer should rule on the wording and the
severability together**, because §0.1a ground 2 is one of the three grounds the `Resettled` ruling rests on.
⛔ **The `Resettled` ruling itself is NOT reopened** — ground 1 is independently sufficient and untouched, and
B says so explicitly. **The flag is on the word, because Step 2 inherits the word.**

## 3.F · HOLD / MOVE

**HOLD on the escalation — MOVE on the question's shape and on the blast radius.**
- **Shape:** I framed it (A-D7) as *"whether that 2026-07-25 refinement was made with the later lineage
  clarification in view."* ⛔ **That question is unanswerable and also the wrong one** — it asks about a past
  author's state of mind. **The answerable question is severability, and `00_Frame` §0.1a poses it in the
  pass's own voice far more sharply than I did.** What changed my mind: re-reading §0.1a ground 1 against
  §0.1a item 3. **I had read both in Round 1 and did not put them side by side.**
- **Radius:** I said *"the pass loses one of its three."* **Too coarse in one direction** (`G4` retains L129 and
  L131's criterion) **and too soft in the other** (under my own ledger, `Originated` goes to **empty**, not
  to one — I stated the demotion and never costed it against D-7).

## 3.G · WHAT THE LOSING READING WOULD COST

- **If the challenge is dismissed without a ruling** (the quiet outcome, and the likely one if nobody escalates):
  Step 2 builds its assigned generator conflict, its sharpest input and one of three selected generators on a
  line under an unadjudicated challenge — **and `00_Frame` §0.1a's internal contradiction stays live, so a
  later reader can kill the same material by citing ground 1, after Step 2 has been written on it.**
- **If it is ruled inadmissible LATE:** Step 2's spine, the `G2`×`G4` conflict, `Originated`, and load-bearing
  fire 4 all unwind together, **after** the work that rests on them exists. ⭐ **`01` §5.2 rule 4 is the rule
  this is about** — *"Do not build the location's single strongest finding on a provisional assumption"* — and
  **the pass has done exactly that, on a line it labels its strongest, while the assumption was never even
  recorded as provisional.**
- **If it is ruled inadmissible EARLY:** the cost is real but bounded and **the remedy is already unanimous** —
  all three readers, by three routes, point Step 2 at **inflection rather than invention** (ITEM 4).

# ⭐ DIVERGENCES THE BRIEF DID NOT NAME

**The brief asked for these and was right to. Two of the four are larger than one of the brief's three.**

---

# ITEM 4 · ⭐⭐ **IS THE RESEARCH / LIMNOLOGY VOCATION `Originated` OR `Inflected`?** — three readers, three answers

**This is the biggest unnamed divergence, and ITEM 3's blast radius turns on it.**

| Reader | Call | Reasoning |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **`Inflected`** | *"`Inflected` in form (research is inherited) and `Originated` in object (its subject is water bodies only Davis has)… This one is two."* Took the conservative value under `01` §5.1's stated error, and **named the problem rather than widening the class set** |
| **B** | **`PROVISIONALLY-ORIGINATED`** | *"The strongest candidate, because it rests on a physical fact of the site rather than on a practice — but the class's own test is unavailable. Held."* |
| **C** | **`Originated`**, and load-bearing | *"An `Originated` vocation growing directly out of a `Determined` physical fact is the cleanest origination pathway `01` §5.1 describes."* Built `B.2`'s headline `G3` finding on it |

## 4.A · VERDICT — **`Inflected`. A is right. C should move, and C's own headline says so.**

**The rules, quoted exactly** — `01` §5.1, **L412 and L413**:
> *"**Inflected** | **The parent supplies the form; the location supplies its version.** A national holiday,
> observed *this* way"*
> *"**Originated** | **Exists here, comes from nowhere above.** Must be differentiated against siblings"*

**Apply L413 literally.** Does an ecological and limnological research vocation *"come from nowhere above"*?
⛔ **No. Research comes from above.** Limnology is not invented at Davis. **What is local is the OBJECT** —
hypersaline lakes and landlocked marine basins — **and the object is `Determined` by physical geography, which
all three readers class `Determined` in both Acts.**
**Apply L412 literally.** Parent supplies the form (a national scientific life); **the location supplies its
version** (the specific water bodies it physically has). ⇒ ***Textbook `Inflected`.***

> ### ⭐ **AND C's CALL CONTRADICTS C's OWN HEADLINE RECOMMENDATION.**
> C's `B.3`, in C's own words: ***"Davis's local culture is to be found by INFLECTION, not by invention. Step
> 2's effort belongs on… the five `Inflected` slots."*** **That recommendation is right, it is the single most
> actionable thing in C's entire output, and it is weakened by C promoting the strongest available `Inflected`
> item out of the class it is recommending.** ⭐ **C's headline survives the correction and gets stronger:
> the Inflected slate goes from five to six, and the sixth is the best one.**

⛔ **What C's pathway gets right, and I want it kept:** *"a vocation growing out of a `Determined` physical
fact"* is a genuine and reusable observation. **It just names an `Inflected` mechanism, not an `Originated`
one** — the parent supplies the form of "doing science," the ground supplies what there is to do it on.

## 4.B · THE CONSEQUENCE — stated flatly, because it is ITEM 3's real cost

> ### ⛔ **IF L127's DOCUMENTARY CONDITION IS RULED INADMISSIBLE, DAVIS'S `Originated` CLASS IS EMPTY.**
> **Not "may empty" (C). Empty.** The corpus contains exactly two `Originated` candidates; one is now
> `Inflected`, and the other is under ITEM 3's challenge.

⛔ **And per NO FORCED FIT — an empty class is a RESULT, not a gap.** `01` **L416–419** supplies the response
and forbids the wrong one:
> *"**The Inflected class is the workhorse and is systematically under-used.** When a category comes up empty,
> the order of attempts is: check what the parent determines → check what the parent supplies that this place
> inflects → check who arrived carrying one (`Borrowed Form`) → check whether the place is already doing it
> somewhere unnoticed (`The Unrecognized Instrument`) → **only then invent.**"*

⭐ **The convergence I did not have to manufacture:** **A's classification call, B's conservative deferral and
C's headline recommendation all arrive at the same instruction** — *Davis's culture is found by inflection.*
**They arrive by three different routes and none of the three readers noticed the other two had arrived.**

## 4.C · HOLD / MOVE

**HOLD.** My Round 1 call was `Inflected` on the conservative fallback; I now hold it on the **text**, which is
stronger than a fallback. ⚠ **And I withdraw one thing I wrote:** I called it *"a case that does not fit the
closed set… This one is two."* **It is not two.** It is one — `Inflected` — and I reached for the fallback
before finishing the reading. **L412 fits it without strain.**

## 4.D · WHAT THE LOSING READING WOULD COST

**If C's call stands:** (1) Step 2 treats an inherited form as a Davis origination — ⛔ **`01` L413's named
error, verbatim: *"claiming origination for something inherited."*** (2) ITEM 3's blast radius is understated,
so the developer is asked to rule on a question whose stakes are misreported. (3) ⭐ **Worst: the strongest
`Inflected` item in the corpus is removed from the class `01` L416 says is systematically under-used — in a
pass whose `Originated` class is thin and whose `Aggregated` class is unavailable.** **The pass would be
working harder for a worse result, which is the exact failure L412 names.**

# ITEM 6 · ⚠ **C WANTS THE SECOND TEST APPLIED MORE WIDELY; A SHOWS IT IS OVER-BROAD AS WRITTEN**

**Neither reader saw the other's half. Both are right, and the order matters.**

- **C-D4** applies `00_Frame` §0.3's proposed second test — *"does this claim require knowing anything about
  any other place in order to be true?"* — to the rest of the admitted set and finds **four new instances in
  admitted, UN-STRUCK lines** (L72's *"moderate by Antarctic standards"*, L143's *"broader Tepenian practice"*,
  L143's *"large enough… small"*, L83's *"small… moderate"*). **B-D6 finds a fifth** (a second comparative on
  L120, inside an existing PARTIAL strike).
- **A-D3** shows the same test, **adopted as written, would strike a line `00.1` §4.2 expressly KEEPS** —
  L143's *"the largest ice-free oasis in the country"* — which §4.2 keeps correctly, as **developer-ratified
  inherited fact** carried on L143's own authority *"never on L120's or L60's (rankings a prior pass
  performed)."*

## 6.A · VERDICT

> ### **Both hold. The second test needs a PROVENANCE EXCEPTION before it may be applied at all — and until it has one, C's four instances are FLAGS, not strikes.**
> **A-D3's distinction is the operative one and it is the thing the test cannot see:** ⭐ ***a RATIFIED
> superlative is a FACT; a PASS-PERFORMED superlative is an INSTRUMENT.*** **The second test cannot
> distinguish them, because both have identical surface form.**

⭐ **And C's own field data proves the exception is necessary rather than theoretical:** C found L143's
*"broader Tepenian practice, not unique to Davis"* fails the test outright — **and then had to use it anyway**,
because it is decisive for the classification at C's ledger item 12 (*a practice broader than Davis cannot be
`Originated` here*). **C used a clause C had just ruled illegal, correctly, and said so.** ⇒ **That is the
exception arriving on its own, in the field, before anyone wrote it.**

⚠ **C-D4's L72 case is the one genuinely unresolvable instance and C handled it exactly right:** *"moderate by
Antarctic standards"* cannot be disambiguated from the line between a legal real-world comparable (`01` §5.3a
substitute 3) and an illegal implicit-normal over this setting's own cities. ✅ **C's mitigation — use the
figure and the mechanism, never the adjective — is the correct conservative move and costs nothing**, since
the number and the mechanism carry all the content.

**HOLD**, strengthened. **Cost of the losing reading:** adopt the test unqualified and it strikes ratified
canon, including the one clause the function generator's classification depends on. Reject it and five live
comparison vectors stay in admitted, un-struck lines with nothing watching them.

# ITEM 7 · ⚠ **`00_Frame` §0.6's SPINE QUESTION PRESUMES ELECTION** — 3-of-3 on the substance, 1-of-3 on the conclusion

**All three readers found the ambiguity. Only B drew the conclusion.**

- **A-4:** *"whether robots **chose** or **were selected**. 'Relocated' is voice-neutral, and the gap is
  load-bearing."*
- **C-F2 Q1:** *"a **choice** differential rather than a **selection** differential… Those are two completely
  different cities."*
- **B-D11 / Fire 2** alone goes on to say the **ratified spine wording bakes one answer in**:
  `00_Frame` §0.6 — *"When leaving became possible, **robots left** at substantially higher rates than humans.
  **What about this place made that so?**"* ⇒ B: *"**Both clauses encode choice**, when the inheritance
  establishes only outcome."* B's neutral form, offered and not substituted: ***"Davis retained 57.87% of its
  robots and 77.61% of its humans. Was that difference chosen, permitted, or imposed — and by whom?"***

## 7.A · VERDICT — **B is right, and this is time-critical in a way the other items are not.**

**Step 2 writes the spine, from §0.6's wording.** ⇒ **The presumption becomes load-bearing at exactly the
moment it becomes unrecoverable.** ⛔ **A presumption of election, once written into a spine, is not visible
downstream as a presumption — it is visible as the city's character.** ⭐ **B's neutral form costs nothing**:
it preserves the finding, preserves the 19.74 pp spread, preserves the question's force, and **declines only
the one thing the data does not support.**

⚠ **The countervailing, stated:** §0.6's wording is ratified, and B correctly declines to substitute.
⇒ **This is a one-line ruling for the pass owner, not a reader's edit.**

**MOVE.** I noted the ambiguity (A-4) and stopped at *"the inherited material does not say which."* **B went
one step further and the step is the whole value.** What changed my mind: **the spine is written next.** An
ambiguity noted at Step 1 and a presumption embedded in Step 2's spine are not the same object, and I treated
them as one.

**Cost of the losing reading:** Step 2 writes *"what about this place made robots leave"* and every later phase
answers a question about **preference** — leaving *"who was moved, and by whom"* not merely unanswered but
**unaskable**, because the spine has already ruled it out. ⭐ **This is load-bearing fire 1 ("which of two
cities is Davis") being silently pre-decided by a wording choice.**

# ITEM 1 · THE GATE 9 ENUMERATION

## 1.1 · Verdict, flatly

> ### **The three counts are not three answers to one question. They are three answers to three different
> questions, and the reconciled result is not a count at all.**
> **19 / 19-of-26 / 16-of-17 differ by granularity (~60%), scope (~25%) and sole attention (~15%), plus exactly
> ONE genuine disagreement about whether a mechanism fires.** ⛔ **A Gate 9 hit count is not a Gate 9 output.**

## 1.2 · The rule I decided on, quoted exactly

**`04_QA_Gates_and_Differentiation.md` L216–219, Gate 9:**

> *"**Gate 9 — Asymmetry.** For every finding describing a **threshold, gate, conversion, verdict, admission or
> status change**: *the mechanism runs both ways — did the file write both?* Ask what happens to someone the
> mechanism decides **against**, whether that outcome is as durable, and **whether there is any route back.**
> **A "no route back" nobody has perceived as a problem is a textbook shadow.**"*

**The gate asks THREE questions per site and never asks for a total.** It specifies no unit of enumeration, no
denominator, and no scoring. **A count is therefore an artifact of the reader's chunking, not a property of the
corpus** — which is exactly what the three numbers demonstrate.

**`00_RUNBOOK.md` L2494** supplies the only quantitative statement the method makes, and it is a rate, not a
count: *"**This fires on inherited material at a very high rate**, because early material is typically written to
explain how a place *works*, which is a framing that documents the favorable path and stops."* **All three
readers confirmed that prediction. None of them needed a number to do it.**

## 1.3 · The reconciliation, itemized

| Source of the gap | Effect on the count | Who |
|---|---|---|
| **Denominator is not comparable** | **Only B enumerated non-firing sites** (19 fire + 4 pass + 3 near-miss = 26). C reported a denominator of 17 (16 + 1 pass) with **2 mechanisms declined and excluded**. **A reported no denominator at all** — A's five two-sided items are listed as evidence that the omissions are not stylistic, not as a scored complement | all three |
| **Scope — do pass documents count as "inherited material"?** | **B fires on `00.1` §3/§7 (the generator docket) and passes on `00_Frame` §0.1.** C scores `Specs` + census only. A lists pass documents among two-sided items without scoring them. **+2 to B's denominator on scope alone** | B vs A/C |
| **Granularity on `L143`** | A splits it into **3** fires · B into **5** · C into **3.** A multi-clause ratified line has no natural unit | all three |
| **Granularity on the orbital event** | A = 2 (mechanism, differential) · B = 2 (Fire 1, Fire 2) · C = **1** | C −1 |
| **Granularity on the entries** | A = 2 (road, harbor separately) · B = 1 · C = 1 | A +1 |
| **Granularity on tiers/model** | A = **1** (tiers and model fused) · B = 2 · C = **3** (tiers, robot origins, de-stacking) | C +2 |
| **Sole attention** | A alone: regime reclassification, water redistribution, `Significance` shape, **undated transition**, placeholder (5). B alone: ice-free threshold, "clear majority," import side, Falkland gate, docket (5). C alone: robot origins, de-stacking, lakes access, revision date (4) | — |
| ⛔ **GENUINE DISAGREEMENT** | **`L127`. A fires it (`A-5`). B fires it (Fire 7). C scores it a PASS — the only pass in C's corpus** | 2–1 |

### ⭐ The scope question, ruled

**`00_RUNBOOK.md` L2489: *"Run the asymmetry check on existing findings before writing new ones."*** — *existing
findings*, not *the source spec*. **The pass's own Step −1 and Step 0 outputs are existing findings, so B is
right that they are in scope.** ⛔ **But a fire on the pass's own docket is a finding about the METHODOLOGY, not
about Davis, and it constrains Step 2's content not at all.** ⇒ **In scope for the audit; out of scope for the
load-bearing set. It belongs in DEFECTS.** *(This resolves the scope gap without inflating the result.)*

## 1.4 · ⛔ THE ONE GENUINE DISAGREEMENT — `L127`, and the reconciliation is neither A/B's nor C's

**C's position:** `L127` writes the favorable side (*"a real documentary starting point"*), the unfavorable side
(*"No living environmental **knowledge** survived that chain of handoffs"* · *"Learning from a written record
isn't the same as being taught by a living institution"*), **and a route back with its cost** (*"the exiles still
built their own practical mastery of the terrain independently, over generations of their own"*). ⇒ PASSES.
**A's and B's position:** the conversion's **cost** is unwritten and the no-route-back is **total and
unperceived.** ⇒ FIRES.

> ### **MY VERDICT — `L127` PASSES ON ONE LIMB AND FIRES ON ANOTHER, AND THE GATE HAS THREE LIMBS.**
>
> - **Limb 1 — *"did the file write both?"* → ✅ PASSES, uniquely in this corpus.** C is right, and C's
>   technique note is the better half of the disagreement: **the only two-sided line in the inherited set is
>   also the line the pass independently named its sharpest input.** That is worth keeping.
> - **Limb 3 — *"is there any route back?"* → ⛔ FIRES.** **C read *"built their own practical mastery"* as the
>   route back. It is not a route back; it is a SUBSTITUTE.** The chain of handoffs cannot be re-established and
>   the teaching institution is gone permanently. ⭐ **And `L127` frames the loss as a *starting point* — i.e.
>   favorably — which is `04` L219 verbatim: *"A 'no route back' nobody has perceived as a problem is a textbook
>   shadow."***
> - **Limb 2 — *"is that outcome as durable?"* → ⛔ FIRES.** The loss is permanent; the mastery took generations.
>
> ⇒ **A and B are right that it fires. C is right about what is remarkable in it. Neither "fire" nor "pass" is
> the correct single label, which is itself evidence that a per-site binary score is the wrong instrument.**

## 1.5 · ⭐ THE AGREED LOAD-BEARING SET — the fires that actually constrain Step 2

**Filter applied** *(stated so this reads as a filter, not a merge)*: a fire is load-bearing iff **(i)** its
unwritten side is something **Step 2 would otherwise fill silently** while writing the spine or exercising a
generator; **(ii)** it survives both ONE LOCATION tests; **(iii)** it is not already closed by a ruling or a
reservation. **Fourteen sites qualify out of roughly twenty-six distinct across the three readers.**

| # | Site | Unanimity | What it constrains, and how |
|---|---|:-:|---|
| **1** | **The orbital departure's MECHANISM** — eligibility, refusal, route back, all unwritten behind a written arithmetic (`L15`–`L16` + census) | **3/3** | ⭐⭐ **The spine.** Gated vs open decides which of two cities Davis is, and it decides the causal reading of the 19.74 pp differential. **Step 2 writes the spine; this cannot be deferred past it** |
| **2** | **The founding-wave demotion** — founding population Australian exiles (`L129`); Australia **4.93%**; Primary tier **19.04%**; **no arrival mechanism written for the other twenty communities** | **3/3** | ⭐⭐ All three independently rated it **large and entirely peer-free.** The largest clean `G8`/`G4` hole in the set |

| **4** | **The quarrying absence** — `L143`'s 2026-07-16 reassignment against **~400 km² of exposed rock**, with no sentence joining them | **3/3** | C rates it the sharpest; two guards attach *(reserved finding `A-1` is adjacent; the recipient must never be named)* |

| **8** | **Exile as a gate** (`L129`) — threshold, decider, reviewability, heritability all unwritten | **3/3** | ⛔ **Resolves UPWARD.** All three declined to site an answer. **Load-bearing as a PROHIBITION plus a Federation-level request**, not as a local site |

The file never says | **3/3** | ⛔ **Load-bearing as a GUARD.** B's operative form: **no resident may ever be narrated as belonging to a "Notable-tier" community.** C's extension is correct: the scope limit must cover the **invariance**, not only the values |
| **10** | **`Access type: ON`** — a value with no value set | **3/3, same handling** | ⛔ **Load-bearing as a PROHIBITION.** All three took the conservative reading unprompted and refused to guess the complement. **Do not read a meaning off it** |
| **11** | **Subnet membership** (`L5`) — what it obliges and provides | **3/3** | Low priority: its entire content is **`PA-6` UNKNOWN, which is not NOTHING.** A guard |

### ⛔ WHAT I DROPPED FROM THE MERGED LIST, AND WHY — because a filter that drops nothing is not a filter

  anyway. Not a Gate 9 fire.** Same disposition for **B's "ice-free is a moving threshold."**
- **`L8`'s `Significance` field shape** *(A, B)* — a **naming hazard**, correctly cleared on content at `00.1`

- **`L148`'s placeholder** and **the census revision date** — declared blanks and authoring-layer items. **To
  defects, re-flagged on reuse.**
- **The Falkland Treaty as a founding gate** *(B)* — B logs it out of admissible set itself. **Dropped.**
- **The generator docket's missing re-grade column** *(B, Fire 19)* — **in scope per §1.3 and moved to defects**,
  because it is a fact about the instrument and not about Davis.
- **`L28`'s de-stacking** *(C)* — **merged into #3**; its output is the same bound stated at a different level.

## 1.6 · HOLD / MOVE and cost

**MOVE — off the coordinator's framing.** The question was posed as *"granularity, scope, or genuine
disagreement?"* ⇒ **All three, in measured proportion — and the fourth answer, that only B enumerated a
denominator at all, is the one that makes the numbers formally incomparable.**
**Positions adopted:** **B's** scope reading (pass documents are in scope), **C's** two-sided observation about
`L127`, **A's** `L154`-contrast form of the harbor fire, and **none of the three** on `L127`'s score.

**What the losing reading costs:** **reporting a number.** If Step 2 inherits *"Gate 9 fired 19 times,"* it
inherits a figure that is **60% an artifact of one reader's chunking of one ratified line**, and the eleven
unanimous sites — the only cross-reader signal in the data — become invisible inside it. **`04` L222–223 already
warns that a Gate 9 report can be read for its shape rather than its content; a headline count is the fastest
way to get it read wrong.**

# ITEM 2 · `01` §5.1'S CLASSES — three diagnoses

## 2.1 · Verdict, flatly

> ### **THREE SEPARATE DEFECTS. TWO SHARE ONE ROOT; THE THIRD IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT DEFECT AT ALL.**
> - **A's** *(no class for an origination that ages into an inheritance)* and **B's** *(the validating test is
>   unavailable)* share a root: ***§5.1's classes are RELATIVE, and §5.1 does not require you to state relative
>   to WHAT.*** A hit the **temporal** reference — *relative to WHEN*. B hit the **validation** reference —
>   *relative to WHOM*. **Fixing either leaves the other standing, so they are two defects with one cause.**
> - **C's** is not an instrument defect. **It is a RULING's side effect on the instrument's completeness at this
>   one location** — `DR-4` removes a class that the instrument itself is fine. **Different kind, different
>   owner, different remedy.**
>
> ⛔ **AND I RULE AGAINST READER B ON THE CONCLUSION.** **`Originated` IS assignable in-run.**

## 2.2 · The rules I decided on, quoted exactly

**`01_Frame_Typology_and_Inheritance.md` §5.1 L405:** *"Every element of a location's culture is exactly one of
these."*
**§5.1 L413, `Originated`:** *"Exists here, comes from nowhere above. **Must be differentiated against
siblings**"* — error prevented: *"**Claiming origination for something inherited** — which is how two siblings
end up 'independently' inventing the same custom."*
**§5.1 L414, `Aggregated`:** *"The parent's own character is partly the sum or the tension of its children"* —
error prevented: *"Ignoring it produces a Band 5–6 polity written as if it were a large Band 3 town."*

**⭐ THE PASSAGE THAT DECIDES IT — `01` §5.3 L454–457**, which Reader B read in full and did not apply:

> *"**A pass with no sibling set is the ordinary case, not a degraded one.** The entire spine — the generator
> stack, the four-quadrant frame, the peer-free half of the address axis, the pairing typology, the named-axis
> discipline — **never needed a second location.** Only the *anti-convergence guard* does, **and a guard is not a
> generator.**"*

**And `01` §5.3b L517:** *"| **No siblings** | A unique station · a sole polity · a one-off megastructure |
**Use §5.3a.** This is the ordinary case. |"*

**And decisively, `00_Frame.md` L114–121 — inside B's own admitted set:**

> *"**Sibling set:** EXISTS, and is READ-FORBIDDEN in-run. … Substitutes used, per `01` §5.3a — all four
> declared, with real availability: **1. Its own earlier states — AVAILABLE, three-state, all in-frame.
> PRIMARY.** … **4. The generator-conflict method (`02` §5) — AVAILABLE, six countable.**"*

**And the ONE LOCATION law itself, which does not forbid the substitutes — it blesses them:** *"✅ Use the
location's **own** figures stated flatly, and `01` §5.3a's peer-free substitutes (its own earlier states,
real-world comparables, the generator-conflict method)."*

## 2.3 · Which classes a Step 1 at this location can actually assign

| Class | **Assignable here?** | The ruling |
|---|---|---|
| **Determined** | ✅ **YES — with a mandatory two-ancestor stamp** | Davis has **two** ancestors of different epistemic status (subnet **UNKNOWN**, Federation knowable-but-out-of-contract) and §5.1 has one column for "the parent." **A and C independently built the stamp; B used `PA-6` governance to the same end. All three reached the same conservative default — call it `Determined`, name the ambiguity — and that convergence is itself a result.** ⭐ **This is a 3/3 agreement the coordinator did not name** |
| **Inflected** | ✅ **YES — and it is the workhorse** | **§5.1 L416: *"The Inflected class is the workhorse and is systematically under-used."*** All three assigned it freely. ⭐ **C's inflection map — five `Inflected` slots, every one empty of local content — is the single most actionable output of Round 1 and I endorse it unchanged** |
| **Originated** | ✅ **YES — via §5.3a, with the sibling half deferred** | ⛔ **B's "un-assignable" converts a GUARD into a GATE.** §5.3 L457 says in terms that *"a guard is not a generator."* The Frame Declaration declares substitutes **1** and **4** AVAILABLE. ⇒ **Assign it in-run on the peer-free substitutes; the against-siblings half defers to the terminal differentiation check, exactly as Gate 6 already does** |
| **Aggregated** | ⛔ **EMPTY — and the emptiness is declared, bounded, and downward-only** | See §2.4. **C is right that it cannot run and slightly wrong about what is unrecorded** |

## 2.4 · ⭐ The `Aggregated` correction that applies to ALL THREE readers

**All three placed *"Davis as a component of its subnet"* in Davis's own `Aggregated` row.** ⛔ **Strictly,
`01` L414 defines `Aggregated` as a fact about the **PARENT's** character.** ⇒ ***The upward direction is not an
entry in Davis's classification ledger at all — it is an entry in the parent's, and the obligation it creates is
`01` §5.2 rule 5*** (*"Register the assumption where the parent's eventual pass will see it"*), **which
`00_Frame.md` already records as NOT DONE.** ⭐ **C reached this by a different route and stated it correctly:
*"These are one obligation, not two, and should be discharged together."* I endorse that and generalize it — it
is not a mitigation for the empty class, it is simply where the upward material always belonged.**

⇒ **In Davis's own ledger, `Aggregated` is EMPTY, by `DR-4` deferral, downward only.**

**On C's claim that the consequence is unrecorded:** **partly.** `00_Frame.md` L133–137 **already enumerates**
*"anything reading as internally uniform because no sub-locations exist"* among the findings that depend on the
exceptional property. ⇒ **The consequence IS recorded. What is new in C is the identification with §5.1's own
error note** — that the removed class is precisely the one whose omission `01` L414 says *"produces a Band 5–6
polity written as if it were a large Band 3 town."* **C MOVES from "nobody has written this down" to "the
consequence is recorded at `00_Frame.md` L137 and its identification with the band error is new."** The
identification is real and worth keeping.

## 2.5 · What it costs Step 2, concretely

1. **Every Davis-internal claim this pass writes is implicitly `Uniform`**, and **`01` §5.4 L544: *"A Band 4+
   pass that answers everything as Uniform has not been written at its own scale."*** ⇒ ⛔ **Step 2 must
   DECLARE the uniformity at the point of writing rather than let a later reader mistake it for a finding.**
   *"Davis does X"* at Band 5 with no sub-locations is a **scale disclaimer**, not a distributional claim —
   and `00_Frame.md` L73 forbids the latter outright: *"⛔ No finding in this pass may be presented as a
   distributional result."*
2. **The band error is now a NAMED risk with a named source**, so Step 7's gates can look for it specifically
   instead of hoping to notice it.
3. ✅ **Nothing else is lost.** Three classes carry the work, and §5.1 L416 says the one that carries most of it
   is available and under-used.

## 2.6 · HOLD / MOVE and cost

**MOVE — off the coordinator's framing** *(which offered "one defect or three?" and the answer is "three with a
shared root on two")*, and **MOVE against Reader B's conclusion while adopting B's diagnosis.**
**What changed my mind on B:** B's premise is **correct and valuable** — §5.1's sibling clause is a **sixth**
comparison instrument, and the ONE LOCATION ruling's terminal-check list enumerates only **five**
*(`03` §5b · Gate 6's against-siblings half · Step 6 + the table · `02` §4's three-step rule · `02` §G8's
z-score)*. **B found a real hole in an enumeration.** ⛔ **But the remedy for an unenumerated comparison
instrument is to enumerate it, not to withdraw a class.** *(This is instance 6 of the pattern at ITEM 8.)*

**What B's reading would cost this pass, concretely:** B hands Step 2 the instruction **"Step 2 must not select
a generator whose output requires an `Originated` call."** ⛔ **That instruction would kill C's `G3` upgrade
outright** — *a `Determined` obligation and an `Originated` vocation occupying one sentence* — **which is the
single strongest classification result any reader produced, and the only one that hands Step 2 a spine candidate
with a built-in temporal axis.** ⇒ **The cost of B's reading is the pass's best available spine.**

# ITEM 3 · `L127` AND THE GPS LINEAGE CLAUSE

## 3.0 · ⛔ FIRST — A CORRECTION TO THE PREMISE

> ### **"Flagged by all three" is not what happened. It was flagged by TWO — Reader A (`D-7`) and Reader C (`D-1`).**
> **Reader B did not challenge `L127` on GPS grounds anywhere in 873 lines.** *(Verified by direct search of all
> three proof files for* `site.history|lineage|prior occupancy|occupancy|GPS`*.)* **B's GPS work is real and
> sits elsewhere:** B tiers **`L62`'s 1957 operation date** and **`L131`'s closing clause** — *"carries specific
> weight in Australian Antarctic culture"* — as **`NOT AN IN-WORLD CLAIM AT ALL`**, the second of which
> **neither A nor C flagged.**
>
> ⇒ ***Three readers found three different GPS residues, overlapping on only one. The GPS exposure in the
> admitted set is therefore WIDER than any single reader saw, and the coordinator's "all three flagged the same
> thing" framing conceals that.*** **This is the most consequential correction in this document.**

## 3.1 · Verdict, flatly

> ### **NECESSARILY A DEVELOPER RULING. NOT RESOLVABLE BY A PASS. FOUR REASONS, TWO OF WHICH NEITHER READER GAVE.**

1. **`L127` is ratified canon on a ratified root, refined by a dated pass that declared itself GPS-purposes-only.**
   *Finding a defect is not authorization to fix it*, and **a reader's application of a rule does not outrank the
   ruled line.** *(A gave this one and weighted it heavily. Correct.)*
2. **The law's own text does not make the distinction the case turns on** *(§3.3)*, and the standing fall-short
   rule forbids supplying a criterion the instruction does not state. *(Implicit in C. Made explicit here.)*
3. ⭐ **NEW — the authoritative text is not in this pass's admissible set.** The full statement of the law lives
   in a **separate repository** (`TepenianUniverseTimeline/Reference/No_National_Stereotypes.md`). ⇒ ***The pass
   cannot adjudicate against the law because the pass cannot read the law.*** Both readers argued from their
   brief's paraphrase of it. **That is sufficient to name a violation as a question; it is not sufficient to
   rule on one.**
4. ⭐ **NEW — the live question is about the developer's intent at a dated decision.** The 2026-07-25 refinement
   **was itself a GPS pass.** ⇒ **The question is whether that pass ran with the LINEAGE clarification in view or
   only the NATIONALITY one — a fact about what was in someone's head on a date. No amount of reading resolves
   it.**

## 3.2 · The rule I decided on, quoted exactly

**`Specs/Davis.md` L127, in full, because the case turns on its exact composition:**

> *"**Settled:** Post-Falkland Treaty, **on Davis Station infrastructure.** The Australian Antarctic Division
> operated here from 1957. *(Refined 2026-07-25 — GPS-purposes-only pass:* through the First Interwar Period,
> the station was **continuously maintained by a rotating succession of national operators** — which nations held
> it, and in what order, isn't relevant to the story. **No living environmental *knowledge* survived that chain
> of handoffs** — but **preserved journals, logs, and orientation manuals left behind across the centuries** gave
> the exiles a real documentary starting point for the Vestfold Hills' terrain. Learning from a written record
> isn't the same as being taught by a living institution, and the exiles still built their own practical mastery
> of the terrain independently, over generations of their own.)*"

**The binding law, at the scope the coordinator states it:** GPS-purposes-only covers **a real site's builders,
flag, lineage, abandonment and fate — provenance, not ethnicity** — and exists so that ***a city is
characterized by who lives there, not by whose station it occupies.***

## 3.3 · ⭐ THE DISTINCTION THE CASE ACTUALLY TURNS ON — and neither reader made it

**`L127` contains SIX separable claims, and they do not sit in the same time.**

| | Claim | **Which time?** | Flagged by |
|---|---|---|---|
| **(1)** | Settled post-Falkland Treaty | **In-fiction (2564)** | — *(clean)* |
| **(2)** | *"on Davis Station infrastructure"* | **Real-world plant, carried into fiction** | ⛔ **nobody** |
| **(3)** | *"The AAD operated here from 1957"* | **Real-world, dated** | **B** *(as `L62`)* |
| **(4)** | *"continuously maintained by a rotating succession of national operators"* **through the First Interwar** | ⭐ **IN-FICTION — 2083–2564** | **A, C** |
| **(5)** | *"which nations held it… isn't relevant to the story"* | The neutralization itself | all three |
| **(6)** | *"No living… knowledge survived"* + *"preserved journals, logs, and orientation manuals"* | ⭐ **IN-FICTION — five centuries of it** | **C** *(both)*, **A** *(the corpus)* |

> ### ⛔⛔ **CLAIMS (4) AND (6) ARE SET IN 2083–2564. THAT IS FICTIONAL TIME.**
> **The real Davis Station's occupancy history runs 1957 → the present day. The chain `L127` describes runs for
> five centuries AFTER the real record ends.** ⇒ ***It is not the real site's lineage. It is the setting's own
> history, sited at a real coordinate.***
>
> **The counter, and it is strong:** that fictional chain **exists only because the real site existed**, it is
> patterned on how real Antarctic stations are actually held, and it makes Davis a place characterized by
> **whose station it occupies** for the first 481 years of its documented existence.
>
> ⇒ ***Both readings are available on the law's text, and the law does not choose between them. That is why this
> is a ruling and not a reading.*** **A and C both argued the case as though (4) and (6) were real-site history.
> They are not, and saying so does not make the challenge go away — it makes it a sharper question.**

## 3.4 · ⭐ THE QUESTION FOR THE DEVELOPER — one question, three answerable values, each with its consequence

> ## **`L127`'s 2026-07-25 refinement removed WHICH nations held Davis Station and kept THAT it was held: a five-century in-fiction chain of handoffs (2083–2564), the loss of all living knowledge across it, and the written record it left the exiles to found a city on.**
> ## **GPS bars a real site's lineage, abandonment and fate. Does it also bar an IN-FICTION occupancy history that exists only because the real site existed — or is in-fiction history at a real coordinate the setting's own, once the nationalities are gone?**

| Value | What it means | **What it costs** |
|---|---|---|
| **(a) KEEP AS IS** | Nationality-neutralization was the whole requirement. The 2083–2564 chain is Tepenian history and admissible | ⭐ **Nothing changes.** `G4` stands; the `G2`×`G4` conflict stands; the load-bearing set's #12 stands |
| ⭐ **(b) KEEP THE OUTCOME, CUT THE CHAIN** — ***the middle NEITHER READER OFFERED*** | The exiles found a documentary record and no teacher. **Where the record came from is simply not written** | **`G4` keeps the documentary inheritance and loses the lineage. The `G2`×`G4` conflict SURVIVES intact**, because both its terms are downstream of the record's existence, not of its provenance. ⭐ **This is the cheapest value and it preserves everything Step 2 needs** |
| **(c) STRIKE THE INHERITANCE** | The founding begins with the exiles; nothing precedes them at the site | **The full blast radius — §3.5** |

## 3.5 · ⛔ THE BLAST RADIUS UNDER (c), adjudicated — the readers disagree and both overstate

| Claim | Reader | **My adjudication** |
|---|---|---|
| *"`G4` collapses to the bare exile fact at `L129`"* | **A, C** | ✅ **TRUE, and SHARPER than either said.** Exile-as-founding-mode is **`Determined` at Federation level** — A's own B.1 row says so. ⇒ ***`G4` would not merely get thinner; it would stop generating LOCAL material at all.* A generator reduced to a `Determined` fact cannot differentiate the location.** Neither reader said this |
| *"the `G2`×`G4` conflict has no second term"* | **C** | ✅ **TRUE under (c).** ⛔ **FALSE under (b)** — which is why the middle value matters |
| *"`Originated` may empty entirely"* | **C** | ⚠ **OVERSTATED, and it contradicts C's own ledger.** C's B.3 lists limnology as *"solid"* and `L127` as conditional. **Under my ITEM 2 ruling `Originated` is assignable, so limnology survives (c) and the class does not empty.** ⭐ **C's own strongest recommendation — *"local culture is to be found by INFLECTION, not by invention"* — is unaffected by ANY value** |
| *"the pass loses one of its three"* | **A** | ✅ **TRUE and NOT BLOCKING, and nobody said the second half.** **`00_Frame.md` L149: COUNTABLE: SIX, against a threshold of three.** Losing `G4` leaves **five.** ⇒ ***The pass survives even the worst outcome.*** **This is the single most useful thing to tell the developer: the ruling is urgent for CONTENT QUALITY, not for pass viability** |

### ⇒ THE OPERATIONAL RECOMMENDATION, WHICH NEEDS NO RULING TO ACT ON

> **Step 2 proceeds now, selecting a spine that does not require `L127`'s chain.** Three peer-free conflicts are
> already available without it — **`G5`×`G2`** *(two entries, no failure state)*, **`G2`×`G3`** *(a breadbasket
> with a two-month above-freezing window)*, and **C's `G3` two-class tension.** **The ruling unblocks the
> `G2`×`G4` conflict later; it does not gate the step.**

## 3.6 · HOLD / MOVE and cost

**HOLD on the verdict** *(developer ruling — with A and C)*. **MOVE on the framing, on the premise, and on the
blast radius.** **What changed my mind:** reading `L127`'s bytes rather than the readers' paraphrases. **Both
readers quoted the chain and neither noticed it runs 2083–2564** — five centuries of fictional time — which is
what converts a clear-looking violation into a genuine question, and what makes value **(b)** visible.

**What the losing reading would cost:** ⛔ **If this were treated as pass-resolvable and answered locally —
in either direction — the pass would either (i) build `G4`, the `G2`×`G4` conflict and its spine on a line a
later ruling strikes, forcing a rewrite from Step 2 down; or (ii) pre-emptively discard the pass's declared
sharpest input on a reader's application of a law the reader could not open.** **Both are expensive; the second
is worse, because a discarded input leaves no trace to come back to.**

# ITEM 4 · ⭐⭐ A UNANIMITY NOBODY NOTICED — the undated transition defeats the PRIMARY substitute

## 4.1 · Verdict, flatly

> ### **All three readers found the SAME defect, filed it under three different headings, and none recognized it as one finding. Assembled, it is larger than any of the three parts, and it is a live blocker on Step 2's own declared method.**

| Reader | Where they filed it | What they said |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | `A-18` — a Gate 9 fire | *"**no inherited line dates the transition** … Davis is declared Band 5 for an unstated fraction of its own 248 years"* |
| **B** | `D15` — a doubt about the frame | *"**Census I and Census II are not dated anywhere in my admissible set**, so the other two states are in-frame by assumption"* |
| **C** | `B.5` — a classification finding | *"**The pass is describing two demographically different cities under one frame**"* |

## 4.2 · The rules, quoted exactly — and why the three together are a blocker

**`00_Frame.md` L117–118 declares the pass's anti-convergence method:**
> *"Substitutes used, per `01` §5.3a … **1. Its own earlier states — AVAILABLE, three-state, all in-frame.
> PRIMARY.**"*

**`01` §5.3a L485–497, added 2026-08-31 from a real case, is a MANDATORY pre-commitment check:**
> *"### ⚠ Check the axis against the Frame Declaration BEFORE committing to it … **Before committing to an
> own-eras axis: ask whether its natural third state falls inside or outside this pass's own declared frame.**
> If outside, either (a) choose a different axis … or (b) accept and declare a two-state reading rather than
> silently reaching across the frame boundary to complete the third."*

> ### ⇒ ⛔ **THE PASS'S PRIMARY ANTI-CONVERGENCE SUBSTITUTE IS DECLARED *"three-state, all in-frame"* AND TWO OF ITS THREE STATES HAVE NO DATE AT ALL.**
> **`01` L485–497 anticipates ONE failure mode — a third state falling OUTSIDE the frame. This is a DIFFERENT
> failure mode with the same effect: a state that cannot be placed inside or outside, because it is not placed
> at all.** ⭐ **The check cannot return either of its two permitted answers.** ⇒ ***The instrument's own guard
> does not cover this case, and it should.***

## 4.3 · What it costs Step 2, concretely

- **`01` §5.3a substitute 1 is `00_Frame.md`'s declared PRIMARY** and, per §5.3 L457, **the anti-convergence
  guard is the ONE thing a peer-free pass genuinely needs a substitute for.** ⇒ **If substitute 1 is
  unverifiable, the pass's only load-bearing guard rests on an assumption.**
- **Substitute 2 is declared THIN** *(parent unwritten)*, **substitute 3 UNBUILT** *(no research log — Step 3's
  job)*. ⇒ ⭐ **Substitute 4, the generator-conflict method, is the only one both AVAILABLE and unaffected — and
  it is the one `01` L502–503 says *"needs no comparison set at all."*** **Step 2 should lean on it
  deliberately, and say that it is doing so.**
- ⭐ **And the positive half:** the only era boundary in the whole corpus with a hard date is the **founding**
  (Post-Falkland Treaty, 21 June 2564 = the frame's opening). **B established this and it is the good news: the
  founding end of the axis IS verified in-frame. It is the middle and the present that float.**

## 4.4 · HOLD / MOVE and cost

**MOVE — this was not on the coordinator's list and none of the three readers assembled it.**
**What the losing reading costs:** **filed as three separate small items, it is closed three separate small
ways** — A's as a Gate 9 open question, B's as a doubt, C's as a classification caveat — **and the thing it
actually is, a blocker on the method Step 2 is about to use, is never seen.** ⛔ **That is the precise failure
mode `LAW 0` warns about: none of the QA gates can tell you a pass is thin, and none of them can tell you three
small flags were one large one.**
